# VALQUO_LEDGER.md — the one place that answers "where do we stand?"

One row per external-audit item (`valquo_audit_items.json`, 134 items). This
file replaces reconstructing project state from git history.

## The contract (three rules — read them, they are why this file exists)

1. **Every agent updates its rows as part of its handoff.** A landed item with
   no ledger row is not finished work — the same standing as the existing
   "code without a handoff entry is not finished work" rule in `RUN_RULES.md`.
2. **The ledger is the answer to "where do we stand."** If it cannot answer,
   the ledger is broken and *fixing the ledger* is the task. Never another
   archaeology dig.
3. **Rows are append-and-amend, never silently rewritten.** A status that
   changes keeps its history in the note (`was X (sha) -> now Y`), because this
   project has already been bitten by claims that quietly changed meaning.

## How to read a row

* **status** — `OPEN` / `IN PROGRESS` / `DONE` / `BLOCKED` / `SUPERSEDED`.
* **verdict** — only for items that were actually measured: `ADOPTED` /
  `REJECTED` / `NULL` / `INCONCLUSIVE` / `DEFERRED`. It is filled in **only when
  the write-up literally uses one of those five words.** Most of the B series
  concluded `FIXED`, and `X8` concluded `REPLICATES` — real outcomes, but not
  verdicts in this vocabulary, so their column is blank and the write-up's own
  word is quoted in the note instead. Blank therefore means *"not measured, or
  measured and reported in different words"* — never *"we don't know"*.
* **commit** — a sha, so any claim here is checkable in one step. It is the
  commit whose *subject names the item* where one exists; otherwise it is the
  commit that **introduced the write-up**. Many items landed inside multi-item
  commits ("eleven Part I corrections") that never name them, so for much of the
  B series this is *"where it was recorded"*, not *"where it was fixed"* — a
  weaker claim, and stated here rather than left to be assumed. Unfinished rows
  carry no sha at all: a commit that merely *mentioned* an item reads as
  evidence of work done, and is worse than a blank.
* **handoff** — where the real write-up lives. The ledger is an index, not a
  replacement for it.
* **src** — `auto` = mechanically proposed and not yet read by a person; treat
  as a lead, not a fact. **Anything else** (`human`, or a lane name such as
  `pipeline builder`) = hand-verified against the write-up; `build_ledger.py`
  will NOT overwrite it, only report a disagreement. The test is *"is it
  `auto`?"*, not *"is it `human`?"* — it used to be the latter, which quietly
  demoted every row a lane had signed with its own name.

## Four traps that already produced wrong counts — do not re-make them

1. **A forward reference is not a completion.** "feeds U1", "needed for S12",
   "(supports D1)" say the item is *wanted*, not *done*. Counting these is what
   produced the 68/134 figure against a header-only count of 38/134.
2. **`P1`–`P5` collide with the project's own PHASE labels.** CLAUDE.md's
   "DONE (P4 commit)" is phase P4; audit item P4 is open and explicitly "out of
   band". `P6`–`P10` and `P24.x` are phases only — the audit's P series stops
   at P5.
3. **`M2` is ambiguous across documents.** HANDOFF_STATUS.md's "the audit's M2
   (SanDisk/WDC)" is `CODE_AUDIT.md`'s M2. The external audit's M2 is
   "clustered inference default" and has never been touched.
4. **`D1`–`D10` collide with DECILE labels**, which this project writes
   constantly ("long-short (D1-D10)", "D1 22.8% → D10 10.7%").
5. **A multi-item commit subject donates one item's verdict to every other id
   in it.** `275e9af` reads *"O16/O24 RESULTS: O24 is a NULL; O16 stopped at
   its own reproduction gate"*. The word `NULL` is **O24's** verdict, but the
   subject names both, so the proposal marks **O16 DONE** — an item that
   deliberately returned no verdict at all. Found 2026-08-08.
6. **`DEFERRED` is in the DONE vocabulary, so "deliberately deferred" reads as
   finished.** B23's own heading is `## B23 — DEFERRED, deliberately` and its
   body opens *"Not done, and not forgotten."* The proposal says DONE.
   Found 2026-08-08.

`build_ledger.py` encodes traps 1–4. **Traps 5 and 6 are NOT encoded** — both
were left as reported disagreements rather than fixed, because the fix is a
guess about commit-subject grammar and the human row already wins. Re-check
them on every refresh.

## Ledger accuracy — checked against the tree 2026-08-07 (out-of-band claims audit)

The `CLAUDE.md` claims audit (`HANDOFF_claims_audit.md`, options-bot lane) verified every status
it touched against the commit the row names, not against prose. **The ledger came out well: every
status it checked — B6, B7, B8, B11, B12, B17, M1, R1, R9, R10, X2, X3, X7, X8, U7 — was
corroborated by the code or the artifact.** Two things worth carrying, neither edited here because
the rows are not this lane's:

* **`R3`'s note is stale.** It reads "Shrinks every options t ~1.36x". The corrected figure is
  **√2.212 = 1.487×** on the 3,885-trade book; 1.36× came from the pre-correction 3,042-trade book
  and `CLAUDE.md` already carries the correction. **Owner: whoever owns R3.**
* **`X8` is DONE and `CLAUDE.md` did not know it.** The ledger was right and the brief was wrong —
  which is rule 2 of the contract working exactly as intended. `CLAUDE.md` now records it.

**C6 closed 2026-08-07 (same lane, separate task) — and it is the one row the ledger got wrong,
in an instructive direction.** The row named a blocker requiring Don and the Oracle box
("must scp quant_bots/data/*.py off the box"). That blocker was never real: the missing sources
sat in `options-bot/handoff/quant_bots.zip`, **tracked in this repository the entire time**, and
were recovered byte-identical without touching the box — which is as well, since the box is now
decommissioned. **The lesson is narrow and worth stating: "the only copy is on machine X" is a
claim about where you looked, not about where the file is.** Nobody had grepped the tracked zips.
`options-bot/.gitignore:34` (`!handoff/*.zip`) is the line that saved the project, and it should
not be tidied away.

## Refresh of 2026-08-08 (r1 lane) — counts, and the three ways `--write` was destroying this file

**`--write` had never been run since the out-of-band rows were added, and running it would have
deleted eight of them.** The refresh was done only after fixing that. All three defects share one
signature — the script curating content it did not write — against a docstring promising the
opposite (*"It never silently overwrites a human-verified row"*):

1. **`render()` iterated the 134 audit ids, so any row without an audit id was DROPPED.** That is
   all eight out-of-band rows: `OOB1`/`OOB2`/`OOB3` and the project's own pre-registered
   experiments `LOO`, `SELRULE`, `HACFLOOR`, `MLPREREG`, `MLCOMB` — Sessions 7–11 and the public
   fair-value leak closure. `OOB1`'s note had been carrying the warning *"build_ledger.py
   regenerates from the 134 audit ids only and will DROP this row"*: the defect was **documented in
   the data instead of fixed**, so the next person to type `--write` would have lost the rows and
   the warning together.
2. **The preserve check was `src == "human"` exactly**, so the seven rows signed
   `src=pipeline builder` were treated as machine-generated and rewritten from the proposal —
   `B8` and `P4` lost their `FIXED` verdicts that way. Now anything that is not the literal
   `auto` is protected, so a new `src` spelling degrades to *protected*, never to *overwrite me*.
3. **`render()` emitted the hard-coded `LEDGER_HEADER`**, deleting any prose written under it —
   here the entire "Ledger accuracy" section below, i.e. R3's stale-figure note and the C6 lesson.
   The header is now read back from the file.

Pinned by `tests/test_build_ledger.py` (**20 assertions**, incl. a round-trip of the real file).
**After the fix the whole refresh moved one cell**: `D4`'s note, `no mention anywhere` →
`prose mentions only` (`HANDOFF_data_spend.md:161`, genuine). Status unchanged, still OPEN.

**The fix for defect 3 had a bug of its own, caught by its own test.** The first version split the
header at the first line starting with `|` — which, once this very counts table was added below,
truncated the header there and deleted every section after it. The boundary is now the table's
exact `COLS` header line. The test was strengthened at the same time: it had asserted only that
the header was non-empty and contained no table rows, **both true of a truncated header**, so it
now compares the whole prose block byte-for-byte.

**74 of 134 DONE (55.2%).** Hand-verified 93/134; 41 rows still `src=auto` (leads, not facts) —
and **every one of those 41 is OPEN** (S 24, O 14, M 2, D 1), so the auto rows are entirely
the untouched backlog rather than unreviewed claims. No `DONE` row anywhere rests on machine
evidence alone.

**THE STATUS COLUMNS DO NOT SPAN THE STATUSES IN USE, AND THE `U` ROW IS WHERE IT SHOWS.**
The `U` series is now 4 `DONE`, 1 `PARTIAL` (`U2`) and 3 `DESIGN-RECORDED` (`U4`, `U6`,
`U8`), totalling 8 — but this table has columns only for OPEN / IN PROGRESS / DONE /
BLOCKED / SUPERSEDED, so its `DONE` cell reads 4 and the remaining 4 rows appear nowhere.
Reported rather than resolved by rounding them into `DONE`: a `DESIGN-RECORDED` row has no
measurement behind it and a `PARTIAL` row has an untested half, so counting either as `DONE`
would tell a future session they had been tested. **The `U`-series rows are the authority;
this cell is a summary that cannot express them.**

**`*` — THE SAME THING NOW HAPPENS IN THE `R` ROW, 2026-08-13 (blocked-R recheck).** `R` moved
from 0 OPEN / 4 BLOCKED to **3 OPEN / 0 BLOCKED**, and the fourth — **`R8` is `OBSOLETE - PREMISE
REFUTED`**, a status with no column, so the `R` row's cells now sum to 9 against a total of 10 and
the `ALL` row's to 133 against 134. Not rounded into `DONE`, for the same reason the `U` rows are
not: `R8` was **never measured**, its premise was refuted by reading the code path, and filing that
as `DONE` would tell a future session the total-return work had been carried out. **The four `R`
rows are the authority.**

**AND THE `BLOCKED` COLUMN WAS ALREADY STALE BEFORE THIS CHANGE — reported, not silently
repaired.** Only the `R` cells above were re-derived. The `ALL` row's remaining `BLOCKED` of **1**

### Where sources disagree — 21 items, and the human row wins all 21

The proposal is mechanical and its asymmetry is deliberate (a wrongly-OPEN row costs a re-check;
a wrongly-DONE row stops work happening). Every disagreement below was checked against the
write-up. **No status changed.** Four causes:

| cause | items | why the human row is right |
|---|---|---|
| **Script proposes DONE, item is not done** | `B13`, `B23`, `O16` | The dangerous direction, and all three are false. `B13` is "PARTIALLY FIXED and labelled so" — `MIN_AVG_DOLLAR_VOLUME` still cannot bind. `B23` is trap 6. `O16` is trap 5 and **stopped at its own reproduction gate with no verdict**. |
| **Script proposes IN PROGRESS, item is DONE** | `B9`, `O2`, `U5`, `D8`, `D9`, `M5` | "section exists, no completion word found" — the write-ups conclude in words outside `DONE_CUES` (`RELABELLED`, `adopted: []`, "Protocol WRITTEN"). Vocabulary gap, not a status question. |
| **Script proposes OPEN, item is DONE** | `P2`, `P3`, `P5`, `D1`, `D2`, `D5`, `D6`, `D7` | The collision guards firing as designed. `P2` **has** headers (`HANDOFF_crowding.md:1`, `HANDOFF_STATUS.md:53`) but `P` is a colliding series and neither heading carries an audit cue. `D1`/`D2`/`D5`–`D7` are written up as **table rows** in `HANDOFF_data_spend.md`, not headings, so there is no HEADER evidence to find. |
| ~~**Script proposes OPEN, item is BLOCKED**~~ **THE SCRIPT WAS RIGHT AND THE HUMAN OVERRIDE WAS WRONG** | `R4`, `R5`, `R6`, `R8` | ~~Blocked on **lane ownership**, not on evidence: they sit in `valuation/edge/**`, which `AGENTS.md` assigns to the pipeline-builder lane. The script cannot see an ownership boundary. Defensible either way; kept as BLOCKED because that is what a reader needs to know.~~ **CORRECTED 2026-08-13 (blocked-R recheck): all four are now OPEN or OBSOLETE — see their rows.** The ownership boundary the override defended had already dissolved: `valuation/edge/**` has taken **59 non-merge commits from at least five worktree branches since 2026-08-06** under the gated auto-land Action, and `AGENTS.md` itself is dated 2026-08-05/06 with its own second table marked stale. **The lesson is the one this table exists to teach, pointing the other way:** a hand-verified override is only as fresh as the document it was verified against, and *"defensible either way"* is the phrasing to distrust — it recorded a judgement call with no re-check date, and it held four items shut for weeks after its premise expired. |

## Ingest of 2026-08-14 (options-bot lane) — the 60 MA rows of merged audit #3

`VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT_ULTIMATE.md` is the **merged record of record** for cold audit #3: Pass A
(the Opus session, `MA1`–`MA35`, kept as written in `VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT.md`) plus an
independent blind Pass B (`MA36`–`MA60`), reconciled. All three deliverables were untracked
until this session committed them. The rows below follow the **LA-series precedent**: an
out-of-band cold audit's findings get one ledger row each, appended verbatim, so that "where do
we stand on MA37?" is answerable here rather than by reading a 583-line audit.

**It is SIXTY items, not 61.** 35 + 25 = 60, in the prose and in the JSON, with no id in either
set missing from the other. Counted rather than quoted.

**`src = audit3-ingest`, deliberately neither `auto` nor `human`.** The `src=auto` lesson in
this file is that a mechanically-proposed row is *a lead, not a fact* — and this project has
already been bitten by six `src=auto` rows asserting "no audit section exists" when a full
section did. So these rows say exactly what stands behind them: **every one is transcribed from
the audit's own item entry, and NOT independently re-verified against the code by this lane.**
The audit's evidence line is carried into the note so the next reader checks the claim, not the
row. `build_ledger.py` will not overwrite a non-`auto` row, and it preserves out-of-band ids
verbatim (`extra = [k for k in existing ...]`) — the destroy-on-refresh defect MA60 describes is
already repaired.

**Statuses are evidence-only, and every departure from `OPEN` names its evidence in the row.**

* **`OPEN` (50 rows)** — the default. *(52 on ingest; `MA13` and `MA19` were landed DONE by the edge lane in the same window — see the merge note below.)* For the eleven proposal/hypothesis items (`MA24`–`MA34`,
  `MA54`–`MA58`) `OPEN` means **NOT RUN**, never *broken*; each carries its own kill condition
  and trial price in the audit.
* **`IN PROGRESS` (5)** — `MA1`/`MA2`/`MA3` (greeks lane) and `MA5`/`MA6` (app fixer) on Don's
  direction of 2026-08-14. **All five carry no tree evidence yet** — no commit and no handoff
  section names any of them — so the status rests on the assignment alone and the rows say so.
  That is the honest reading of "in flight", and it is checkable: when a lane lands, its row
  gains a sha.
  * **CORRECTED 2026-08-15 (`MA5`+`MA6` landing): IT WAS THREE, NOT FIVE. `MA5`/`MA6` WERE NEVER
    IN FLIGHT** — the app fixer's commissioning task *named* MA5/MA6, but under the **same id
    collision this ledger already documents in the `MA9` and `MA10` rows**, and that lane's own
    handoff (`HANDOFF_appfixes.md` §33) states it outright: *"the real MA5/MA6 are MEDIUM
    edge-lane items in `valuation/edge/`, i.e. not this lane at all."* It fixed MA9/MA10/MA50.
    So the ingest marked two rows `IN PROGRESS` on the strength of an assignment that was
    actually for two OTHER rows, and **the correction was already sitting in this file, two rows
    away, uncarried.** The evidence rule the bullet itself proposes is what caught it: no commit,
    no handoff section, and — checked at landing — no `worktree-ma5*` branch has ever existed.
    Both rows are now `DONE`, landed by the edge lane. **A cross-reference is only worth having
    if something reads it.**
* **`DONE` (3)** — `MA35`, closed on **code** rather than on its commit subject (`.gitattributes`
  carries `*.pdf binary`, and this session measured the merged PDF's staged blob byte-identical
  to the file on disk at 204,636 bytes); plus **`MA36` and `MA37`, taken and closed by this lane
  in the same session** under `PREREG_ma36_ma37_record_integrity.md`. They were ingested
  `IN PROGRESS` and amended to `DONE` in place, which is rule 3 working as written — the row
  keeps its history rather than appearing fully-formed.

**A MERGE THAT WOULD HAVE BROKEN THE ONE GUARANTEE THIS FILE MAKES.** The edge lane landed real `MA13` and `MA19` rows (**DONE**, `src=human`, `0eb95b1`) while this lane was ingesting all 60 as `OPEN`, and the two sides conflicted. Keeping both — the reflex, and what `merge=union` would have done — produces **two rows with the same id and no rule for which wins**, which is precisely why `.gitattributes` lists this file as deliberately NOT union-merged. **The human row wins both**, per the contract; the ingest's `OPEN` versions are discarded rather than kept beside them. Resolved to **60 unique ids**, asserted rather than eyeballed. Final: `OPEN` 50, `IN PROGRESS` 5, `DONE` 5 (`MA13`, `MA19`, `MA35`, `MA36`, `MA37`).

**TWO THINGS A MECHANICAL INGEST WOULD HAVE GOT WRONG, both corrected in place.**

1. **`MA18`'s severity.** The items JSON reads `MEDIUM`; the audit's **own section 2.3
   Correction 1** re-rates it **HIGH** and section 9 lists it under HIGH. The correction never
   reached the machine-readable set. `MA18` is *"is the bound forward row being written?"* —
   the audit's **#2 action item by its own ordering** — so a verbatim ingest would have
   under-rated precisely the finding that cannot be recovered later. Ingested as **HIGH**;
   the deliverable is left unedited, because it is a record of what the auditor found.
2. **`MA7` is not done, and a commit subject says it is.** `14c00ac` reads *"MA7: /api/rank is
   the sharper case"* and changed **only the audit documents**. That is **trap 5** — a commit
   subject donating a verdict — caught on ingest rather than after it had marked a live
   uncapped-vendor-spend finding as closed.

**AND THE INGEST FOUND THE LEDGER'S OWN REFRESH TOOL BLOCKED — BY THREE ROWS THAT PREDATE IT.**
`python scripts/build_ledger.py` was **refusing to run at all**: *"REFUSING TO PROCEED — these
ledger rows could not be parsed and would be DELETED by a rewrite"*, naming `S23`, `M1-PARSE`
and `V2G`. Four raw `|` characters sat **inside** cells — `(|HAC t| <= 0.87)`,
`|Spearman(term_slope, atm_front)|`, `max|dev|` and one literal `\|` — and the guard is right
that escaping does not help, because the parser counts raw pipes. Verified against `HEAD`
before this ingest: **all three were already malformed**, so the refresh has been unavailable
for some time and rule 2 (*"if the ledger cannot answer, fixing the ledger is the task"*) makes
it in scope. **Four characters changed, no word and no claim touched** — each pipe pair replaced
by the word `abs`. `M1-PARSE` is the row worth noticing: its own subject *is* this hazard, and
it was malformed by it. `S23` was the dangerous one — its two pipes sat in the **verdict** cell,
so every later column shifted and the parser read `src` out of the handoff slot; it survived
only because the string it landed on was not `auto`, which is the asymmetry doing its job by
luck rather than by design. The tool now runs and reports all 60 MA rows preserved verbatim.
**One cross-lane test moved with it, declared rather than absorbed:**
`tests/test_la_screener_batch.py::test_the_real_ledger_has_no_UNKNOWN_losses` **asserted the
three malformed rows exist**, as an allowlist meant to fail *"if a FOURTH appears"*. Its
expected set is now **empty** — strictly stronger, since the guard fires on the first malformed
row rather than the fourth — and its docstring records who changed it and why.

**Where the MA rows do NOT belong: the trial counter.** None of these is a pre-registered test,
so none appears in `RESEARCH_LOG.md` and `N` does not move on ingest. The two this lane took
(`MA36`, `MA37`) are correctness repairs and are logged `FIXED` at zero trials, on the `PT-BUG12`
precedent.

| R8 | R | Total return, not price-only | **OBSOLETE - PREMISE REFUTED** |  |  | HANDOFF_ci.md | 2026-08-13 | human | was BLOCKED (lane) -> now OBSOLETE, 2026-08-13 (read-only). Deliberately NOT one of the five documented statuses, because none fits: it was never measured so it is not DONE, no other item supersedes it, and leaving it OPEN would invite work that must not be done. **R8 asserts that every forward return in the panel is close_end/close_start-1 on a series that "handles splits but excludes dividend income". IT DOES NOT EXCLUDE THEM.** The panel's close IS Sharadar `closeadj`, which is split- AND dividend-adjusted, so the panel's forward returns are ALREADY total return. Four independent lines of evidence: **(1)** SharadarProvider.price_history requests "date,closeadj" (data_providers.py:149). **(2)** sharadar_freeze._split_prices writes prices/TICKER.csv as date,close taking the closeadj column, and its docstring says so - "close is closeadj (split- AND dividend-adjusted), which is what the live export wrote and what the panel expects" (sharadar_freeze.py:326,339) - and WRDSProvider.price_history reads exactly that file (data_providers.py:335-357). **(3) MEASURED on the very file the panel reads:** SPY compounds **15.27%/yr from 2009-01-15 to 2026-01-15**, which is the total-return level and matches the record's own "SPY total return +15.32%" from `benchmarks.spy`; price-only would be roughly 2pp lower. And per-name adjustment factors track each name's own yield - 2009-01 file price against the actual split-adjusted price gives MSFT 1.41x, JNJ 1.67x, KO 1.76x, XOM 1.90x, monotone in dividend yield, which is the signature of dividend adjustment and of nothing else. **(4)** Audit B1, from a different lane with no stake in R8, independently found and FIXED a bug CAUSED by this series being dividend-adjusted - "corrupts every dividend payer on every date by a factor that grows with lookback" (options_backtest.py:208). **The audit itself hedged and told the reader to check first** - "closeadj is dividend-adjusted retroactively ... resolve which series is actually feeding forward returns before assuming a direction" - and nobody resolved it, so the item sat BLOCKED for eight days on a premise ten minutes of reading refutes. **NO LOOK-AHEAD FROM THE RETROACTIVE ADJUSTMENT, and this is an argument rather than a measurement:** in a ratio of two adjusted prices every adjustment factor for a dividend AFTER the window cancels, while dividends INSIDE the window are correctly included. **THE TILT CONCERN CANNOT ARISE AT ALL:** the benchmark leg is computed from the same fwd_ret column, so both legs sit on the same total-return basis, and R8's central worry - that the top decile loses more to the omission than the benchmark does - has no mechanism. **SEPARATE DEFECT FOUND AND REPORTED, NOT FIXED:** fundamental_panel.py:3597-3598 states the opposite in a shipped docstring - "NO dividends anywhere. The panel's forward returns are price-only" - and uses it to justify omitting dividend TAX from the after-tax block, calling the pair "Consistent". They are not consistent. Dividend income IS in the gross return, so the after-tax figures omit tax on income they do include and **net-of-tax is OVERSTATED** - the flattering direction. That is a new item for the edge lane, not R8. |

THE FINDING THAT REFRAMED THE TASK, and it corrects THIS ROW: the note below says the band is ALREADY LIVE in the taxable configuration so an adopt would change the DEFAULT rather than introduce the band. That is FALSE, and it was verified false before the register was written. exit_frac was consumed live in exactly three places and NONE of them applied it - valquo_index.py:306 wrote it into the payload as metadata, :358 printed it in a CLI banner, and web/app.py:331 displayed it. The only code that had ever APPLIED a band was the backtest. build_index was a pure top-N selection with no band and no notion of a held name, and valquo_index.py:298 said so in a comment: the band "requires the PREVIOUS book, so it is applied at rebalance time, not in this snapshot", emitted as an instruction for a human rebalancer rather than executed. So this adoption WIRED THE BAND INTO LIVE CONSTRUCTION FOR THE FIRST TIME, the taxable config's declared 20 pct band had never affected a book anyone received, and the fidelity gate was load-bearing rather than ceremonial. THE GATE PASSED, and it is provably not vacuous. The rule moved to valuation/edge/no_trade_band.py and BOTH callers import it, so fundamental_panel._band_select IS no_trade_band.band_select - one code object, not two equivalent implementations, pinned by an identity test. The live entry point build_index was then applied to the panel's most recent cross-section and reproduced the measured arm's book NAME-FOR-NAME: 184 names, identical set AND identical order, and 0 mismatches across ALL 69 dates. Non-vacuity is asserted rather than hoped for - with no held set both paths collapse to plain top-N and the comparison proves nothing, so the band is CHAINED across all 69 dates exactly as turnover_and_costs chains it, and at the gate date the banded book differs from plain top-N by 65 of 184 names with 65 retained; the run ABORTS if that is not so. Exact composite ties at the gate date: 0, so no argsort ambiguity. WHERE IT APPLIES AND WHERE IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT: S14 measured the DECILE book, and exit_frac is a fraction of the ranked UNIVERSE, which is meaningful for a decile book and not for a fixed-N one - on a 25-name book against a large universe it would hold nearly every name nearly forever, and the panel's own comment says exit_mult is the only band meaningful for a fixed-N book. So taxable moves 0.20 to 0.30 and roth stays BAND-LESS, because a banded top-25 book is a construction nobody has measured and shipping one would borrow S14's evidence for it. VINTAGE 4 OPENED 2026-08-13, DERIVED AND NOT ASSUMED per PT-GAPDUE: current_vintage() returned vintage 3 opened 2026-08-11, and the FIDELITY-2 amendment clause is explicitly self-limiting - it amends in place only at ZERO accrued complete days and stops applying the moment one complete day accrues. Vintage 3 had accrued TWO, so the clause did not apply, Amendment 1's ordinary rule resumed and vintage 4 opened. Rule 6 is paid in full and not hidden: the accrued forward clock resets to zero and vintage 3's two days are spent. THE FIRST SHADOW PAIR IS NOW OPEN - V1 shipped as instrumentation with no pair to measure, which was the point, and the pair 4 over 3 is live with vintage 3's parameters pinned two days before this adoption was known about, so the predecessor is a genuine snapshot rather than a reconstruction. no_trade_band was added to PARAM_KEYS - pre-registered this time, unlike themes_scored_live - because without it vintages 3 and 4 hash IDENTICAL, the band changing no weight and no theme, and same_model would report no change while the book demonstrably changed; EXACTLY ONE KEY separates the pair, which is the honest description of this adoption as a change to selection and not to scoring. ADOPTS NOTHING; adoption would be a VINTAGE EVENT and the vintage is DERIVED per PT-GAPDUE. Equity N 165 to 170, one trial per item. NOTE WHAT DOES NOT WORK, because it is the obvious first try: data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK##########.json carries only the CURRENT sic and sicDescription, so it is a second snapshot rather than a history. The audit proposes half-lives of 3, 5 and 10 years; 5 IS THE SHIPPED DEFAULT. So the arm can only ask whether a different half-life beats the shipped one, and it is defined that way. REJECTED at both settings and by the widest margins in the family: 3-year half-life d_alpha minus 4.29pp early and minus 2.98pp late with d_t minus 2.272 and minus 1.081; 10-year minus 4.21pp and minus 2.66pp with d_t minus 2.328 and minus 1.062. BOOK-LEVEL VERDICTS ONLY - no arm may be promoted on a per-signal or per-theme IC, which P6.3, X3, S20/S21, S3 and S16 have now demonstrated five times. THE FAMILY-WISE CLAUSE WAS FIXED BEFORE ANY RESULT: five arms against one bar make at-least-one-clears roughly a 23 pct event under independence and the arms are positively correlated, so a single clearing arm is recorded ELIGIBLE - UNREPLICATED, 1 OF 5 SIBLING ARMS and never adopted. | S28 | S | Distribution, not just the mean | **DONE - SHIPPED** | Four additive distribution blocks in the payload, SCHEMA_VERSION 5 to 6, reporting only and pinned as such |  | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md session 30 | 2026-08-12 | auto | REPORTING INFRASTRUCTURE: no hypothesis, no threshold, no verdict, and NO PUBLISHED CLAIM MOVES. Every book statistic this project ships is a mean or a t on a mean, and a mean is the one summary that cannot show a book being carried by a handful of quarters. New statistics.distribution() returns n, mean, sd, min, p05, p25, median, p75, p95, max, the COUNT and FRACTION of negative periods, and the DATED worst and best period. Wired into four payload blocks: construction.top_decile_alpha_distribution, construction.long_short_distribution, portfolio.return_distribution and portfolio.excess_vs_equal_weight_distribution. WHAT IT SHOWS ON THE SHIPPED BOOK, and this is why the item was worth doing: the published plus 7.17 pct per year top-decile alpha is a mean of 69 quarterly draws of which TWENTY ARE NEGATIVE, 28.99 pct, with a worst quarter of minus 6.83 pct on 2016-01-20 and a best of plus 11.47 pct on 2022-07-22, and a MEDIAN of plus 1.41 pct against a MEAN of plus 1.79 pct - so the headline is right-skewed and the typical quarter is worse than the average one. The long-short spread is negative in 33.3 pct of quarters, worst minus 20.01 pct on 2025-07-29. THE UNITS ARE CARRIED IN THE BLOCK because the obvious misuse is annualising a quantile: top_decile_alpha is periods-per-year times the MEAN, and that scaling is a statement about a mean, never about an order statistic. CONSISTENCY IS ASSERTED, NOT ASSUMED: four times the distribution's mean reproduces top_decile_alpha to 4e-17 on the real 69-date panel and exactly on a synthetic one, which is the check that the distribution describes the SAME series the headline is a mean of - a block attached to the wrong series would look reasonable and quietly mislabel the worst quarter in the record. PINNED AS REPORTING-ONLY by a test that fails if any threshold, gate or verdict ever compares or branches on a distribution field, and that guard was checked for vacuity - it inspects 14 code-level references, so it is not passing by seeing nothing. The dated extremes are matched against the ORIGINAL series rather than the cleaned one, because pairing a cleaned value with an uncleaned date is how an off-by-one mislabels the worst quarter. M6's field-level schema guard covers all four blocks. Zero equity trials - infra n=1 on the M2/M6 precedent, and infra N gates no published claim |

The 0.0545 availability term is a SELECTED quantity - you only avoid it if you trade when the market is there - and the register forbids quoting it as a saving; only the 0.0408 price-improvement term is a property of execution. So the apparent overcharge is 9.53 dollars per contract of which just 4.08 is defensible. THE STRONGEST CONDITIONING IS NOT THE ONE PREDICTED: entry premium, not quoted spread. A cheap option pays nearly its whole quoted half-spread and an expensive one under 60 pct of it, which is what a fixed tick on a small premium implies. F1 quoted spread-pct clears full-sample but not both halves on the primary arm; F3 dte clears nothing; F4 market cap clears full-sample only; F5 time of day clears both halves on the primary arm and not on the fallback. No hypothesis, no threshold, ZERO trials. Memo at DESIGN_u4_u6_u8.md. The row's src=human note was accurate. THE BLOCKER IS MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED, AND IT IS COVERAGE: the audit's method at :2103 is to replay the equity book's entries and exits substituting the option expression 'using the mined chains for the names where they exist', and measured on the corrected 69-date panel against the 187-name mined universe, of 7,132 names ENTERING the top decile across 68 transitions only 129 have mined chains = 1.81%, and of 7,095 LEAVING only 128 = 1.80%. Per rebalance that is a MEDIAN OF 2 covered names in a book whose mean top-decile size is 165.6, with ZERO covered entries on 18 of 68 dates; restricted to the options window it is 82 of 4,490 = 1.83%. The replay would substitute the expression on two names out of 166 and still produce a number. A SECOND INDEPENDENT BLOCKER: the entry leg is a cash-secured PUT and the banked book is opt_right == call on 3,870 of 3,870 rows, so no put-chain history exists to replay against. The derived layer's iv_put_25d is a surface point, NOT a tradeable quote with a bid, an ask and an open interest - and O18 measured spread cost at rho 0.6743 of the quoted half-spread, so a CSP study without quoted put spreads would price the one leg that decides the answer by assumption. WHAT IT WOULD TAKE: a put-chain mine over the EQUITY book's own names rather than the 187 megacaps the alert universe was built from, point-in-time quoted put spreads, and a pre-registered treatment of the right tail - S22 measured top-decile alpha still accruing at TWO YEARS, so a covered call written on a name leaving the decile is written on a population whose forward return is measurably not zero. It also inherits O26 as a prior. Same class as S25 and the B13 liquidity blocker: unobtainable without new data, and a proxy universe would answer a different question under this one's name. IT REMAINS THE MOST TRADEABLE IDEA IN THE CATALOGUE IF THE DATA IS EVER BOUGHT - the audit is right about that and nothing measured here contradicts it. CORRECTION 2026-08-13 (V6-OPT, against my own prior session): this row was closed on TWO blockers and ONE OF THEM IS FALSE. The retracted claim was that the chain cache is 100% calls so no put history exists to replay a CSP against. That measurement - opt_right == call on 3,870 of 3,870 rows - is of the TRADED BOOK, and the CACHE was never checked. Measured: data/options holds 1,288,750 puts against 1,288,751 calls across 40 sampled tickers with bid, ask, volume and open interest, ZERO tickers with no puts, and data/options_derived carries the same contracts with iv, delta, mid and spread_frac. V6-OPT went on to price 2,038 real 25-delta puts from that cache and settle them to expiry, so quoted put spreads demonstrably exist. THE COVERAGE BLOCKER IS UNTOUCHED and still carries the verdict on its own, so the row's STATUS does not change; what changes is that it rests on one measured reason rather than two. A composition fact about a BOOK is not a coverage fact about a CACHE. CORRECTION 2026-08-16 (P1-S0 lane, PREREG_u6_overwrite_leg.md PART A, ZERO TRIALS): THE 1.81% BLOCKER IS MEASURED AGAINST THE WRONG UNIVERSE, AND THE RE-OPEN'S OWN PREMISE IS ALSO WRONG. This row was to be re-opened on the ground that 1.81% counted decile ENTRIES while a covered call is written on HOLDINGS. That premise is REFUTED by measurement: on the identical all-transitions denominator, point-in-time, entries run 648/7,138 = 9.08% with any chain and holdings 897/11,426 = 7.85%, so HOLDINGS ARE 0.86x AS WELL COVERED AS ENTRIES, not the 6-11x assumed. The leg distinction is real and nearly immaterial. WHAT IS ACTUALLY WRONG IS THE DENOMINATOR OF NAMES: this row's own text says the 1.81% was measured "against the 187-name mined universe", the ALERT universe, while the chain cache holds 1,000 ticker directories of which 906 are names in the equity panel - 4.84x more, producing a 5.02x larger coverage figure. The two ratios agreeing to within 4% is the evidence that the universe is the whole explanation. AND THE CORRECTION IS UNDERSTATED because it runs against a STRICTER test: this row asks whether a ticker has mined chains at all, the new measure asks whether a chain exists ON THAT DATE and, for the primary, whether O20's own liquidity screen passes on it. TWO CONTROLS MAKE IT LIKE-FOR-LIKE RATHER THAN MERELY SIMILAR: entry events reproduce at 7,138 against this row's 7,132 (0.08%), and top-decile membership reproduces at 11,426 - EXACTLY the count S10 and V6-B's C7 report independently, which is what establishes the shipped decile convention. CONSEQUENCE: the CSP entry leg is UNTOUCHED and remains coverage-bound, so the row's STATUS does not change. The OVERWRITE leg is buildable - median 12 PIT-liquid and 21 any-chain optionable holdings per covered date against a median decile of 169.5, with ZERO covered dates holding none - and it is BOUNDED, which matters more than the unblocking: it can only ever be written on 7.3% to 13.0% of decile slots, so it can only move the book by that fraction of whatever it does. A reconciliation reported rather than smoothed over: this row records zero covered entries on 18 of 68 dates and the new measure finds 28, which are exactly the pre-2016 dates where the cache holds nothing; a non-dated membership test can score a pre-2016 entry as covered because the ticker is mined in some later year. The two numbers are not comparable and neither is wrong on its own terms. PART B (the overwrite arm) is registered in PREREG_u6_overwrite_leg.md, GATED on P1 Stage 0, and carries a gating power clause that refuses the arm outright if the largest arithmetically possible effect sits below the design's own MDE - the SELRULE precedent. scripts/u6_overwrite_coverage.py, tests/test_u6_overwrite_coverage.py (8), data/free_analysis/U6_OVERWRITE_COVERAGE.json. |

| U8 | U | One risk budget across books | DESIGN-RECORDED | premise CONFIRMED, prescribed method refuted - a drawdown optimiser returns a corner |  | DESIGN_u4_u6_u8.md | 2026-08-13 | human | DESIGN-RECORDED 2026-08-13. No hypothesis, no threshold, ZERO trials. Memo at DESIGN_u4_u6_u8.md. THIS ROW'S src=auto NOTE WAS WRONG - it read 'no mention anywhere in the corpus' and VALQUO_EDGE_AUDIT.md:2123 is a full U8 section. THE AUDIT'S CENTRAL EMPIRICAL CLAIM IS NOW MEASURED AND IT IS CORRECT: it argues that a volatility event firing many options alerts is also the event in which the equity book draws down and that nothing accounts for it. O11 measured that alerts cluster (median 7 per week, max 38, 51.5% of trades in weeks of more than 10) and U3 measured that those are the same episodes (correlation +0.4371; sleeve -84.39% in the equity book's worst quarters; COVID 2020Q1 equity -28.09% against sleeve -76.14%). WHAT THE AUDIT DID NOT ANTICIPATE IS THE DIRECTION: its framing at :2127 imagines a CONCENTRATION problem, more exposure during the drawdown. The measured problem is different in kind - the sleeve loses roughly THREE TIMES what it was supposed to be diversifying. THE PRESCRIBED METHOD AT :2129 CANNOT BE RUN AS WRITTEN, for three reasons in ascending severity. (1) The allocation it would produce is already known and is a CORNER: U3 swept exactly this allocation over X in 90..99 at two concurrency caps, 20 cells, and combined max drawdown was worse than equity alone in EVERY ONE, improving monotonically toward X=100, so a drawdown-minimising optimiser puts ZERO in the sleeve. (2) Ledoit-Wolf shrinks a covariance matrix toward a structured target and on a 2x2 there is essentially nothing to shrink - sophistication as decoration. (3) The drawdown estimate it would optimise rests on ONE episode: S10 measured the worst peak-to-trough as exactly one 63-day period at trough index 44 of 69, and U3 counts 5 equity drawdown episodes deeper than 5% on the covered subsample but only one of severity, so allocating on a contribution-to-drawdown estimate fitted to one crash fits a portfolio to a single observation - the in-sample selection this project already paid for at +8.43%/yr in-search to -0.04%/yr locked hold-out. WHAT IS BUILDABLE is smaller and more useful than an optimiser: one stated capital base, a concurrency-aware combined exposure line from O11's marks, a PRE-COMMITTED POLICY cap on the sleeve's share rather than a fitted one (U3 says the fitted answer is zero and the Sharpe-maximising answer is disqualified by the audit's own bar), and a tripwire when the alert rate enters O11's top decile of weeks. Reporting and policy, no new research, owned by the app/portfolio lane. Re-open the optimiser only if a genuinely long-vol sleeve exists to allocate to - which per U3 the current one is not. |

THE AUDIT'S INDICATIVE 600/yr UNDERSTATES THE RECURRING LINE ~10x (the subscription is 6,000/yr PER EXCHANGE) and has NO COUNTERPART AT ALL for the one-time history purchase, which is the expensive part; the two bases are deliberately not compressed into a single multiple, since a one-time cost divided by an annual rate means nothing. The audit's own caveat that the figure was for a different product was right to be there. THREE FINDINGS THE AUDIT DID NOT HAVE. (a) A SIX-MONTH FREE TRIAL of ad-hoc historical EOD Open-Close exists, filed Dec 2025 and clarified Jul 2026, open to 'both TPHs and non-TPHs who have not previously subscribed or previously received a free trial' - Don qualifies, so the audit's 'one sales call' is obsolete and the correct action costs nothing. It is ONE-SHOT, so when it is spent matters more than whether. (b) THE LICENCE FORBIDS WHAT VALQUO IS: 'Raw data is licensed for internal use only and may not be redistributed externally in any form'; external distribution of DERIVED data is 5,000/mo (60,000/yr) plus approval. A score shown on valquo.co is derived data distributed externally. Same shape as D1's Sharadar finding and JKP's research-only licence. (c) AD-HOC HISTORY STARTS JANUARY 2018, so 24 of the book's 118 months (20.3%) are unavailable at any price AND THEY ARE THE EARLY ONES - D4 cannot be tested by this project's own both-halves standard, in a programme whose signature failure is a result holding on one half and reversing on the other. GATE UNTOUCHED AND POINTED ONE SERIES OVER: O14 (free alert-day tick flow, ~7 hours on data already held) is still OPEN and has never run. Of twenty OPEN O and U items only two are flow items, and one is O14 itself, so D4 names exactly ONE open item that is not its own gate: U2. The audit filed D4 under options while its cited literature (Pan-Poteshman 2006) predicts STOCK returns, and the options entry is dead (R2, -5.0640pp split-clean) - so the only live route is U2 into the equity composite, which S22's flat 3-month-to-2-year alpha makes a ~60x horizon mismatch. REPORTED BECAUSE IT CUTS TOWARD RUNNING O14: O13 (2026-08-11) found the gap is entirely a within-bin RATE effect - the alert loses on the DAY it picks, uniformly - and flow is a day-level feature; but O13's own refusal rules made the other half WORSE in both directions and you cannot short the gap. Sequence: run O14, then U2, both free; only then spend the trial; do not buy. Five bugs reported incl. the gate pointing at the wrong series and nobody checking the start date against the book's window. Zero trials, no code changed. |

BUG: theta_bulk._call_with_timeout does not cover client construction because tb._cli() is evaluated as an argument, which hung a harvest for twelve hours; reported to the options-bot lane, not fixed here. Hit rate 76.0% against the 75% the pre-run probe predicted. BUG 5, found while starting Tier C and it corrects this row's own earlier diagnosis: the 2026-08-17 twelve-hour episode was NOT the unbounded _cli() hang reported at the time. The run was pulling the whole time and only the checkpoint loop was dead - 883 payloads written between 03:00 and 15:00 UTC against 15 manifest records, because one unit's exception escaped the as_completed loop and ThreadPoolExecutor.__exit__ then drained ~1,340 already-submitted futures with shutdown(wait=True). From outside that is indistinguishable from a hang. The exact exception died with the killed process's stderr and is NOT invented here; the mechanism is fixed by per-future exception capture plus cancel_futures. 870 orphaned payloads were adopted from disk rather than re-pulled (9.90 GB, 0 skipped, identity-checked against each payload's embedded symbol/year and stamped adopted_from_disk), which leaves Tier A at 393 of 400 complete. Fixed by a horizon rather than another status: a quarter that has not started is skipped, a quarter in progress is requested only to the last completed session, clamped to YESTERDAY because an EOD bar does not exist until after the close. Verified live - the 2026 units return ok_partial with quarters_future [Q4], pulled_through 2026-08-17 and 156 trading days each. quarters_future is deliberately a THIRD field, never folded into quarters_missing, because a year short by calendar is not a year short by fault and conflating them makes a live year read as damaged and re-pull forever. FIELD-LEVEL HALF ONLY: B22 had already closed the block level (RESULT_BLOCKS + missing_result_blocks), and HANDOFF_optionsbot.md had already reported the remaining scope as field-only while correctly declining to edit this row - that report is adopted here rather than re-derived. valuation/edge/payload_schema.py enumerates from the PRODUCER at runtime, never from a registry, which is M3's lesson: a guard reading a registry cannot see an unregistered field, i.e. exactly the thing it exists to catch. Every source key must be carried, renamed, or allowlisted WITH A REASON. IT FOUND FIVE LIVE DROPS, TEN FIELDS, NONE PREVIOUSLY KNOWN: (a) portfolio dropped B17's ENTIRE disclosure - label_warning, target_n, exit_rank, held_min/median/max, charges_costs, charges_taxes - all computed by _backtest_hold and none carried, so portfolio.cagr shipped as 'the top-25 hold book' with no warning that it holds ~exit_rank names and pays neither costs nor taxes; the fix for B17 was being computed and thrown away, and the canonical file now carries held_median = 42 for the book labelled 'top 25'; (b) cpcv dropped adopt_detail and challenger_weights_cols, which session 12 banked specifically so 'what would this run have scored one haircut lower' is arithmetic - banking a number into a dict nobody serialises is not banking it; (c) ev_freshness dropped `rows`, the DENOMINATOR of the `fresh` fraction. THE TENTH FIELD IS THE BEST EVIDENCE FOR THE GUARD: ev_freshness.rows was found BY THE GUARD ITSELF on its first real run, and it had escaped the hand-built BLOCK_SPEC because that spec was derived by walking each producer's AST and ev_freshness builds its dict incrementally (out['rows'] = int(n)) rather than returning a literal - so static analysis could not see it and a runtime producer-enumerating guard could. All ten now ship; SCHEMA_VERSION 4 -> 5, purely additive, a v4 reader still works. Known-bad fixtures per M3 pin it against the REAL R9 incident (top_decile_alpha_tstat computed as 4.5174 and recorded as None) and against the options-bot lane's own a_brand_new_metric demonstration, plus a test proving the guard is NOT vacuous on a complete payload and one proving a block that THREW is not also reported as dropping fields. A DEFECT IN THE CHANGE ITSELF, caught before shipping: main()'s blanket except Exception ('Never allowed to fail a completed backtest') would have SWALLOWED PayloadSchemaError and printed it as a warning nobody reads - a check that cannot fail anything is not a check, the very pattern M6 closes. It now has its own handler ordered AHEAD of the blanket one, keeps every artifact already written, and exits non-zero; pinned by an AST test. No env-var escape hatch (RUN_RULES A5); the allowlist is the legitimate door and it leaves a diff. archive_scan also fixed - it stored fair_value but neither the refusal flag nor its reason, so the permanent survivorship-free archive could not tell 'refused' from 'not computed'; reported by the live-data lane as 'edge lane, not mine', and it was ours. X7 ran at N=84 (haircut 2.97685), session 10 at N=121 (haircut 3.09703); scripts/placebo.py then feeds the ADOPTED weights to quantile_backtest, so adoption is monotone decreasing in N and a draw that stops adopting is re-scored under different weights. Session 10's retained artifact records 1.0453572947436582, IDENTICAL to this session's base-weight recomputation to sixteen digits. Substituting the adopted value into session 10's 100 draws gives EXACTLY 8 at t>=2.0 (X7's figure), the adopt count at N=84 comes back 21 (M1's recorded 21%), and the naive p95 stays 2.1437 with max 3.436 - which is why session 10's control reproduced X7's percentiles to the digit while missing one draw: 2.1273 lands just below the 95th percentile. CONSEQUENCE THAT OUTLIVES IT: THE CALIBRATED PLACEBO FLOORS ARE FUNCTIONS OF N and nobody knew; here they happened not to move because the affected draw landed below the percentile, which is luck not design. Every future sweep must record the N it ran at, and a floor may not be compared across sweeps at different N without checking. The SHIPPED strategy is unaffected - it does not adopt, it keeps current-default, so no haircut touches its ls_t; the exposure is to the CALIBRATION, not the headline. Instrumentation: cpcv_validate now banks adopt_detail (margin, se, haircut, n_trials_used, folds_positive) and challenger_weights_cols - the challenger's weights WHETHER OR NOT adopted - so "what would this run have scored one haircut lower" is arithmetic. See HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 32. **A WRITE DOOR EXISTS ON THE LIVE SERVICE 2026-08-18 (app-fixer lane), AND THE ROW STAYS BLOCKED BECAUSE NOTHING CALLS IT YET.** POST /admin/track-row?append=1 on the existing X-Admin-Token appends today's row to the service's OWN bound series on the Render disk, computed by the SAME index_mark.contract_row() the CLI uses, so there is still one function and no second implementation to drift. **TWO PREMISE CORRECTIONS, AND THE SECOND IS WHY THE DOOR WAS NEEDED. (1) GET /admin/track-row WAS NEVER READ-ONLY** - the route has declared GET and POST since it landed and ?append=1 wrote on either verb, so the third door already half-existed; what was missing was every rule the contract puts on the write. **(2) THE LANDED ACTION CANNOT PRODUCE A ROW ON ANY RUNNER.** .github/workflows/track-row.yml landed 2026-08-16 (0e0e86d, PR #1) calling the in-repo CLI, but .gitignore excludes /data/ with no negation and git ls-tree -r origin/main -- data/ returns ZERO paths, so on a fresh checkout the book data/valquo_track.json does not exist and contract_row refuses at load_book before reaching a vendor; its only reachable outcome is the refusal branch, a nightly pushed note about a missing book. The edge lane measured and flagged this (HANDOFF_STATUS.md, flagged not fixed, .github/ out of lane) and its own recommendation names this door as the fix, because THE RENDER SERVICE IS THE ONE PLACE HOLDING BOTH THE BOOK AND THE HISTORY. **THE CONTRACT'S RULES ARE ENFORCED IN CODE AND IN THE LIBRARY RATHER THAN THE CALLER, via index_mark.append_row(append_only=True), so both doors obey one implementation (the B7 split this project keeps paying for).** Intraday marks refused with NO parameter anywhere that switches the refusal off, pinned by an AST test rather than a substring sweep; append-only, so a date at or before the last recorded row is refused and a gap stays a logged gap, filling one remaining a deliberate human act on the CLI's --date under contract section 3; idempotent per day, a second POST returning the row ON DISK and never the freshly computed one, because a vendor revision or a fallback answering where the primary did not can make those differ; and the file's previous BYTES stay an exact prefix, the same terms track-row.yml's own cmp check uses, carrying a positive control that a default-mode write genuinely breaks the prefix. **THE ORDER OF THE FIRST TWO CHECKS IS LOAD-BEARING: today's date is not strictly greater than itself, so if append-only ran first the second POST would be REFUSED as a backfill rather than answered as a no-op** - and with two crons by design in that workflow, both firing on one day is the NORMAL path, so that ordering error would have produced a pushed failure note every single day. **FOUR DISTINCT STATUS CODES because the caller is unattended: 201 wrote, 200 already recorded, 409 refused append-only, 422 refused by the mechanism (the ordinary session-has-not-closed evening case and every non-trading day), 500 only for an unexpected exception.** 4xx rather than 5xx is NOT a reversal of section 7.2a's rule - that rule is right that a 5xx tells a scheduler to retry something not broken, and a 4xx says this did not happen and retrying unchanged will not change it. GET keeps its 200 on a refusal deliberately, since a GET asks what today's row would be and no-row-because-the-session-has-not-closed is a complete answer, while a POST must report whether the write happened; the existing test pinning that 200 is untouched and still passes. **A GET CAN NO LONGER WRITE (405), a deliberate removal of a documented door** - a side-effecting GET on the one dataset here that cannot be re-derived is reachable by a retry, a prefetch, a proxy or a pasted link, and none of those is a decision to record a day; measured before removing that NOTHING in the repository calls it. **A LATENT DEFECT FOUND IN AN EXISTING TEST: test_the_endpoint_returns_exactly_what_the_module_computes read src[i:i+2000], and the handler is now 5,921 characters with index_mark.contract_row NOT in its first 2,000** - so the delegation check was one prose edit from becoming a window over the DOCSTRING ALONE, passing vacuously while reporting that the endpoint delegates. Now bounded by the next @app.route and given two further assertions (no file IO in the handler, append_only=True actually passed). **AND THE COMMENT-VERSUS-CODE TRAP FOR THE FOURTH TIME IN THIS RECORD:** my close-refusal test's source half fired on the handler's own COMMENT explaining why the refusal is not a parameter, failing a correct tree; it reads the AST now, where comments do not exist. **A FOURTH DEFECT OF MINE, FOUND BY INSPECTING THE RUNNING ENDPOINT RATHER THAN BY A FAILING TEST: one payload key changed TYPE depending on the outcome** - the 201 body carried valquo_pct 4.0 and the 200 body carried the string "4.0" for the same recorded day, because the no-op path returns a row that has been through a CSV. A consumer doing arithmetic on it works on the day it writes and breaks on the day it retries. index_mark.typed_row() now casts the recorded columns back, and AN UNREADABLE CELL IS KEPT VERBATIM RATHER THAN NULLED, since replacing a corrupt cell with None would hide a bad record behind a well-typed payload - the same failure as normalising a ragged file. **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION: tests/test_index_mark.py 44 passed (31 before), 13 new and 2 repointed with every assertion kept; 10 mutations, 10 CAUGHT 0 MISSED**, covering dropping append_only, re-opening the GET write, collapsing 201 into 200, collapsing 409 into 422, making a refusal a 200, removing the duplicate-date no-op, removing the backfill refusal, returning the recomputed row from the no-op, dropping the type cast, and nulling an unreadable cell instead of reporting it. **ZERO TRIALS - a door and its enforcement, no hypothesis and no verdict against a bar; equity N stays 231 and no research-log row is written.** NO ROW WAS WRITTEN TO THE REAL TRACK (every test runs on state_isolation temp paths with an injected tape) and data/ was not touched. **WHAT REMAINS IS DON'S AND IT IS A .github/ EDIT, UNTOUCHED HERE PER MA11's LAND POLICY: point track-row.yml at the service instead of the CLI**, branching on the status alone, which also removes the git commit data/ step entirely and so retires the gitignored-book defect rather than working around it. Contract section 7.2b documents the door; see HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 39. **THE SEED DOOR LANDS 2026-08-18 (app-fixer lane) AND THE ROW STILL STAYS BLOCKED, BECAUSE NOTHING HAS BEEN SEEDED YET.** Session 39's write door was pointed at the live service by Don's PR #2 (cb8c86e) the same day it landed, ran at 20:31 UTC, authenticated, reached the door and returned **HTTP 422: the book file /app/data/valquo_track.json is missing or unreadable** (refusal note pushed as designed at 121f5c3). **That is load_book working exactly as written, and it settles the question: THE WRITE DOOR WAS NEVER THE BLOCKER.** data/ is gitignored, so the book has never shipped with any deploy and exists only on Don's machine; the service has nothing to mark. **POST /admin/track-seed installs the book and the recorded history**, same X-Admin-Token, with every rule in index_mark.seed rather than in the caller so the HTTP door and any future caller cannot drift - the handler is pinned to contain no open(, no csv., no os.replace, no conformance( and no json.dumps. **A PREMISE CHECKED FIRST THAT REMOVED A WHOLE DESIGN: /admin/ingest-index-track already existed and CANNOT do this.** It writes the STORE (index_track.STORE_KEY) and from_store is a display-only fallback, while the contract's reader index_track.load and both halves of index_mark read and write FILES - so a seed built on it would have installed a track the writer cannot see. **A finding falls out of that and is named rather than fixed: the live service may already be DISPLAYING a track from the Store while the file-backed contract series is empty**, i.e. two representations of one track on one service with only one of them the contract's. Seeing a track on the site is not evidence the bound series exists. **THREE RULES. (1) THE BOOK MUST BE THE INDEX**, through valquo_index.conformance - PT-SPLIT's own check, 50 names and the 8% cap binding - with why_not carried verbatim and NOTHING AT ALL written on a refusal, which is the load-bearing half. The cap is DERIVED from the observed maximum weight and the derivation is stated: the real book carries no effective_max_weight field, a cap is an upper bound so an observed max at or under 8% cannot come from a cap that failed to bind, and the single divergent case misses the 50-name floor by a wide margin; a recorded value is preferred if the file ever gains one. **(2) AN UPLOAD MAY EXTEND AND MAY NEVER REWRITE**, checked twice and reported separately: the RECORD check compares the first N rows cell for cell and names the row, the column and both values, and the BYTE check requires the disk bytes to be an exact prefix of the canonicalised upload, in the same terms track-row.yml's own cmp uses. When the first passes and the second fails the reason names the ENCODING difference instead of reading as a mystery refusal. Both sides go through ONE serialiser, which is what makes the rule immune to the caller's line endings - and that was verified against reality rather than asserted: **the canonical bytes are BYTE-IDENTICAL to Don's real valquo_track_history.csv**, so a re-seed never needs the encoding escape hatch. **(3) A BOOK MAY NOT BE SEEDED WITHOUT A HISTORY TO STAND ON - the trap that would have lost the recorded days INVISIBLY**, because the next append would start a fresh series at today's date while day_n, computed from the inception date, made the new first row look perfectly plausible and nothing raised. **A FOURTH RULE IS ABOUT THE SEQUENCE RATHER THAN THE SEED: the uploaded header must be the one append_row would compute**, because append_only REFUSES a header it would have to widen - so a differently-shaped header installs a series the unattended writer can never append to, succeeding, looking healthy, and pointing every later refusal at the writer. The end-to-end sequence is the deliverable and one test runs the seed and then the write door against one service and asserts the second succeeds. **FIVE STATUS CODES: 201 the copy changed, 200 nothing changed, 409 the upload disagrees with the record, 422 not the Index or no history to stand on or malformed, 400 no book, 500 unexpected only** - and 200 is a FACT rather than an assumption, since both writes are skipped when the bytes already match, asserted on the book's mtime. The history is written FIRST and the orderings are not symmetric: history-then-book leaves a failed book write refusing at load_book, i.e. exactly today's state, while book-then-history leaves the one state rule 3 exists to prevent. **scripts/seed_track.py IS THE ONE COMMAND** - dry run by default, --send to do it, SITE_BASE_URL or PUBLIC_BASE_URL and ADMIN_TOKEN from .env, and the token is printed on NO path including the failure paths, asserted rather than intended. **A STALE-WINDOW GUARD IN AN EXISTING TEST, AND IT IS SESSION 39's src[i:i+2000] DEFECT IN A NEW SHAPE:** test_the_module_never_writes_unless_asked sliced contract_row up to def append_row, which was the same thing until seed and its three helpers were added BETWEEN the two - at which point the window covered four functions that write files by design and the guard FAILED AGAINST A CORRECT TREE. A window anchored on a distant landmark measures whatever drifts into it; it is bounded by the next top-level definition now, with a non-vacuity test asserting the window really contains contract_row's body. **And my own smoke test refused for the WRONG REASON** - the rewrite case ran against a file an earlier case had extended, so it was refused as a truncation, and session 39's lesson (a test that fails for a reason other than the one it names is not evidence about that thing) was hit again the next day. **AND I SILENTLY DELETED AN EXISTING TEST BY NAMING MINE THE SAME THING:** the seed door's byte-prefix positive control was written as test_the_byte_prefix_check_is_not_vacuous, which is what the WRITE door's control is called - Python rebinds silently, globals() keeps one entry, and the older control stopped running the moment the seed tests landed, with nothing raising and the suite green throughout. Caught by counting def test_ in the file (62) against what the runner reported (61), and a hand comparison is not a thing to rely on twice, so that census is now itself a test and was checked for vacuity by reintroducing the collision. **A test deleted by a name collision is invisible in a green run.** **AND prefix_verified REPORTED A CHECK THAT HAD NOT RUN** - on a first seed there is nothing on disk to be a prefix of and the payload said true anyway, so it is None there now and True only when a prefix was actually compared, the idiom contract_track.recording_ok already uses. **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION: tests/test_index_mark.py 64 passed, 64 definitions against 64 reachable (44 at the end of session 39); full gate 112 of 112 suites, exit 0; mutations 14 of 14 CAUGHT 0 MISSED on the seed door with the write door's 10 re-run and still 10 of 10.** **THE MUTATION HARNESS FOUND TWO REAL COVERAGE GAPS, which is the whole reason to run it:** deleting the byte-prefix check and deleting the disk-header check both left the suite GREEN, because every fixture written was caught by an earlier branch and neither was ever reached; two tests were added to reach them, and without the harness those two rules would have shipped tested-in-name-only. **A LIVE HTTP RUN of the real script against a real local instance** covered what a Flask test client cannot - urllib, the header, .env and the exit codes - across seven scenarios: dry run sends nothing exit 0, install 201 exit 0, re-send 200 exit 0, extend 201 exit 0, rewrite 409 exit 2 with the file unchanged, non-Index book 422 exit 2, unreachable host exit 3, and the token in no output on any of them. **THE REAL BOOK AND HISTORY** were run through seed() into temp paths: 86 positions, conforms true at an effective cap of 0.02315, 4 rows installed, reading back through index_track.load as 4 rows ending 2026-08-17, and a re-seed a clean no-op. **ZERO TRIALS - a door and its enforcement, no hypothesis and no verdict against a bar; equity N stays 231 and no research-log row is written.** NO ROW WAS WRITTEN TO THE REAL TRACK (state_isolation temp paths and an injected tape throughout) and data/ was not touched. **WHAT REMAINS IS DON'S AND IT IS ONE COMMAND: python -m scripts.seed_track --send**, then trigger the Action rather than waiting for 22:12 UTC. Running it against the live service is an irreversible write to the bound record and was deliberately NOT done here. **AFTER THE SEED THE SERVICE COPY IS THE RECORD** and the local files become a stale backup that nothing syncs back - a deliberate choice of ONE recorder over two, given that this project has already published two different Valquo Index vs SPY numbers from two books. **.github/ UNTOUCHED and it needs no change** - track-row.yml is already correct and will simply stop getting 422. Contract section 7.2c documents the door and the doors table in 7.2a gains a fourth row; see HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 40. |

REPRODUCED EXACTLY, not inferred: seeding a store from the engine's own committed `data_export/paper_track_index.csv` and calling `recap.build(store, "daily", day="2026-08-05")` returns that line verbatim. NOTHING WAS MISCALCULATED -- `saas/recap.py` read `paper_track.index_summary`, the Tradier sandbox book (10 names, equal-weighted at 10%, inception 2026-08-03, weights the contract's own 8% cap forbids), and printed it under the words "Valquo Index vs SPY". A DIFFERENT WINDOW TOO: the engine's inception is 3 days later and skips the accrued drawdown the contract binds. FIXED STRUCTURALLY: new `index_track.vs_spy_claim()` is the ONE authority for any Index-vs-SPY statement, reads only the bound source, has NO fallback to any other recorder, and returns the numbers with the BOOK and the WINDOW in the same string, so the two cannot come apart in transit. `summarize()` now takes its excess from it as well, so the two derivations that happened to agree became one that must. Recap's `_delta()` -- which took its own `index_ret - bench_ret` -- is DELETED, as is the `_pp` formatter that existed only to print it. THE SITE HAD THE SAME BUG AND THE LABEL DID NOT SAVE IT: `hero.py` fell back to the engine on every fresh deploy (data/ is gitignored) with its own `(idx - bench) * 100`, honestly setting `source: "paper-sandbox"` -- which NO TEMPLATE EVER RENDERED (verified by grep). A label a surface can decline to show is not a safeguard, so the fallback is removed rather than relabelled. Email digest CHECKED and makes no vs-SPY claim; pinned so a future one must use the recorder. Landing page, hero band and Index tab now render book plus window beside every figure. PINNED, AND THE PINS WERE MUTATION-TESTED RATHER THAN ASSUMED LIVE: (1) a recorder handed excess -9.99 against legs of +5.00/+1.00 must print -9.99, never the +4.00 difference -- a deliberately recomputing `_claim_line` prints +4.00 and fails it; (2) the 2026-08-05 rows in the store with no bound rows must yield "No Index-vs-SPY figure", never +0.18 pp; (3) `paper_track.index_summary` made to raise, and the recap still builds; (4) an AST scan of recap/notify/emailer/hero for any subtraction of two return-ish operands -- run against the PRE-FIX recap.py it flags `line 212: index_ret - bench_ret`, so it is not vacuous. ONE EXISTING TEST REPLACED, strictly harder: `test_hero_names_which_forward_record_it_drew` asserted the fallback is legitimate if labelled; it is now `test_hero_will_not_render_the_sandbox_book_as_the_index` and the old behaviour cannot pass it. Also corrected: `track_export.py` called the engine's table "the daily Valquo-Index-vs-SPY series" in prose. 926 passed / 0 failed across 29 suites in the CI environment. NOT DONE, still PT-SPLIT's: the engine is not re-pointed at the Index book -- that is a construction change on a live Render service. WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO: it cannot recall the 2026-08-05 post. |

| PT-VINTAGE | X | The vintage rule - what closes a vintage, what does not, and what a verdict is about | **DONE** | ADOPTED 2026-08-09 |  | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md session 15 | 2026-08-09 | pipeline builder | Contract section 5a, implemented as track_meter.VINTAGES with current_vintage(). Any ADOPTED change to scoring, weights or construction closes the current vintage and opens the next; ADOPTED means it ships in the live scoring path, not proposed or tested. REBALANCING UNDER UNCHANGED RULES IS NOT A VINTAGE EVENT. Each vintage carries its own clock (own inception, own 6-month gate, own 60-month verdict); the gate and the meter attach to the CURRENT vintage and are computed on that window alone, so a verdict is a statement ABOUT A VINTAGE and must name it. The cross-vintage chain is kept and published as 'the system as operated' - the honest answer to what a user would have experienced - and is explicitly NOT the object of a contract verdict because it mixes models; as_operated() carries that disclaimer in its own payload and a test asserts the disclaimer names both 'model' and 'vintage'. RULE 6 IS THE BRAKE: a vintage change resets the whole accrued clock and buys nothing statistically, so anyone shipping a scoring change should know it costs the clock. Two construction points that took a rewrite each: a later vintage is BASELINED AT ITS OPENING LEVEL, not at zero, because the recorded series is cumulative since run #1 and zero-baselining would fold run #1's drift into vintage 2's first month; and as_operated uses RAW ENDPOINTS rather than complete months, because vintage 1 ran six days and holds no complete calendar month, so a month-based leg reported 0.0% for a window that actually moved -2.85pp. |

Four surfaces read it: the hot-list legend (server-rendered directly over the ranked table), the discovery blurb ("a coarse ordering, not a precise one"), the per-name why-this-score panel in app.js, and /methodology's "Where it is weak" section. THE CALIBRATION PRINTS ABOVE THE ATTRIBUTION BARS, not under them: a caveat after the evidence reads as a footnote, before it, it frames how the bars are read. PER_NAME IS AN EXACT SUBSTRING OF DEFENSIBLE, not a shorter rewrite, asserted by a test - otherwise the name row and the legend become two independently-editable statements of one limit and a reader on a name row gets the softer of the two. app.js HOLDS NO COPY of the wording (reads window.SCORE_CONFIDENCE; a test asserts the sentences are ABSENT from the static file) - the same rule PT-OUTBOUND landed for outbound figures. THE SCOPE LINE IS THE PART THAT TOOK CARE: V3 settles the SCORE's precision and explicitly NOT the backtested return spread ("a composite can rank names in an order that is indistinguishable from chance at a given rank and still have a real top-minus-bottom return spread"). So the recency caveat attaches to the top decile's SCORE versus a chance book, NOT to top-decile alpha, the long-short HAC t of 2.620 vs its 2.28 floor, or R1's factor alpha; the top-decile mentions in methodology.html and portfolio.html that describe RETURNS were deliberately left alone, and a test fails if a return figure appears inside the calibration block. THE GROUP HALF NEVER TRAVELS ALONE: V3's flattering finding (top decile beats a chance book AS A GROUP, p 0.008) holds on only 21 of 69 dates, so a test walks every rendered page and fails if the group claim appears without its recency caveat in the same block; the caveat is stated as a BARE COUNT OF DATES, never a rate, because 21 of 69 overlapping cross-sections of largely the same names are nowhere near 21 independent draws (session 9: 16 co-moving countries were worth 2-4 draws and a bar with a claimed alpha of 3.84% measured out at 28.7%). PINS MUTATION-TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: four mutations - legend softened to "only weakly distinguishable", robustness count inflated 45->60, group caveat stripped of its date count, thin-data finding reworded - ALL FOUR CAUGHT; plus a non-vacuity test proving the markdown normaliser does not collapse distinct sentences into a match. 14 new tests (tests/test_score_confidence.py); 34 suites, 1036 passed, 0 failed. RESULT: beta coverage 46/403 (11.4%) -> 500/500 (100.0%) with ZERO throttle events. What made it affordable is BATCHING not patience: yf.download pulled 500 monthly close series in 13 requests in ~40 seconds; only the vendor beta field (.info) cannot be batched, so that leg is paced at 2.5s. Resume follows mine_options_cache's tri-state rule - a failed or throttled unit is NOT recorded, so coverage cannot be inflated by running into a quota wall. The fetch and the measurement are now separate programs: report makes ZERO network calls and drives the REAL _resolve_beta ladder against the cache, which is the structural fix for 'a measurement that consumes the resource it is measuring'. RUNG DISTRIBUTION over 500: vendor 432 (86.4%), vendor_corroborated 31, fallback 22 (4.4%), computed 14, vendor_uncorroborated 1. MY PRE-REGISTERED PREDICTION WAS WRONG (60/40): I expected a HIGHER fallback share than the 46-name sample's 10.9%; it is LOWER at 4.4%, because that sample was enriched with problem names by construction (7 named cases + 5 deliberately out-of-band). B1 do-no-harm HELD on real cached data through an entirely different fetch path: GILD 0.305, CI 0.288, KSPI 0.886 (n=30), CHTR 0.669 all reproduce Part 7.6 exactly. THE FINDING: applying the coverage rule to the LIVE product, three themes carrying 0.375 of the 0.875 deployed weight mass - 42.9% - contribute NOTHING to any live score. capital_discipline and institutional are null on 500/500 served rows; insider is 100% non-null with exactly ONE distinct value. The hot list is a four-theme book (value, quality, size, momentum) wearing the weights of a nine-theme one. insider's deadness is documented at screen.py:288; the other two are not, and capital_discipline has the second-strongest backtest IC (+2.76). SECOND DEFECT, IN MY OWN V2 METER, AND THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION: every V2 coverage floor counts NON-NULL ROWS, so a constant theme passed all seven; and _spearman does not return NaN on a constant predictor, it returns an arbitrary number (roughly [-0.15,+0.17] vs random targets, EXACTLY +1.0 vs a monotone one). The meter would have banked those as genuine monthly observations and the anytime-valid band would eventually have called it significant - the absent themes always refused safely, this one would have produced a verdict. Fixed with a per-date degeneracy floor (MIN_DISTINCT_VALUES=2), recorded as a TIGHTENING permitted by PREREG_v2_theme_health section 10. Also fixed: the cache did not survive its own round trip (5y of monthly closes straddle DST, so str() serialisation emits mixed UTC offsets pandas refuses to parse); and the silent trap that motivated B1 - batched yf.download returns a tz-NAIVE index against a tz-AWARE market proxy, giving an empty intersection, compute_beta 'unavailable', and rung 3a keeping the vendor beta, i.e. corroboration silently disabled on every name with nothing raised. THEME LEG: 0 usable rows -> 500 real rows at scan_date 2026-08-08 via the public credential-free /api/hotstocks, replayed through the project's own Store.save_snapshot into a DEDICATED db (never data/screener.db). B4 HELD - not one of ten verdicts changed; B5 HELD - still zero closed 63d windows. What changed is the REASON: seven themes moved from 'empty in the record' to '0 closed monthly windows', i.e. from blocked by absent data to blocked by elapsed time. At 500 names a live IC of +0.0379 is detectable by month 60 vs quality's backtested +0.0356, confirming V2's calibration from the other direction. The endpoint serves only the LATEST scan, so the record accrues FORWARD one day per run and can never be backfilled - 9 real dates exist on Render that this repo cannot reach. 1199 tests across 26 suites, 0 failures. Zero trial cost; equity N stays 129. 5 BUGS FOUND. |

TWO PRE-SPECIFIED ARMS AND NO SELECTION STEP, so no decide/measure split is needed: A7 = the deployed seven themes at 0.125 each; B4 = value quality momentum size, the four that reach a live score. THE RESTRICTED ARM IS THE LIVE BOOK RATHER THAN A MODEL OF IT, and that is proved not asserted: C1 (three dead themes NaN) and C2 (insider CONSTANT, the other two absent - the live condition exactly) both reproduce the B4 composite name for name at max abs dev 0.000e+00 over all 113,945 rows, because composite renormalises by the PRESENT-weight mass and zscore returns all-NaN on a zero-variance column. HARNESS CONTROL: the incumbent arm reproduces the record to the digit - alpha 0.071741423321, LS naive t 2.8361, LS HAC t 2.6199, alpha HAC t 4.3762, monotonicity -0.891, equal-weight benchmark +18.137%. RESULT: B4 alpha +5.86% against A7's +7.17%, d_alpha -1.3133pp against the -1.95pp bar, paired HAC t -1.4040 over 69 paired dates, so the pre-registered verdict is IMMATERIAL and building the live sources is NOT the project's highest-value work. THE POWER CAVEAT MUST TRAVEL WITH IT: the HAC se of the paired annual difference is 0.9354pp so the design resolves 1.8708pp at abs(t)=2 - well matched to its own bar - but power against a TRUE 1.95pp gap is only 55.0%. IMMATERIAL means the cost could not be separated from zero at roughly a coin flip's power, NOT that it was shown to be small. THE SECOND FINDING IS MORE SERIOUS AND THE REGISTER ASKED FOR IT SEPARATELY: the live four-theme book FAILS the calibrated long-short floor, HAC t 1.8811 against 2.2837 and naive 2.0044 against 2.1437, where the deployed book clears at 2.6199. It DOES clear the top-decile alpha floor at 3.2087 against 2.2913, and since the shipped product is a LONG-ONLY hot list that is the product-relevant statistic - so the long-only book users receive stays demonstrable while the long-short figure quoted beside it does not. BOTH SPLIT DIRECTIONS AGREE IN SIGN ON ALPHA AND NEITHER IS SIGNIFICANT (early -1.14pp at t -1.3519, late -1.48pp at t -0.8984), a better stability profile than session 7's LOO arms, four of seven of which changed sign. REPORTED BECAUSE IT CUTS AGAINST THE VERDICT: the late-half LONG-SHORT difference is -5.63pp/yr at HAC t -2.0639, which crosses the conventional bar - not the pre-registered primary statistic, uncalibrated, and one of six cells, but a reader gets it rather than only the agreeing cells. EXPLORATORY DECOMPOSITION, NO VERDICT, registered as carrying none because session 7 established on this exact panel that a full-sample ablation arm is not a finding: dropping institutional is the ONLY one negative in both halves (-1.41% full, -0.89% early, -1.91% late), so 13F is the source to build first; dropping capital_discipline is POSITIVE in both halves (+1.37% full) despite it holding the second-strongest panel IC (+2.76), a restatement of X3's finding that theme IC does not predict marginal contribution; dropping insider flips sign between halves. WHAT THIS DOES NOT SAY, and it is the most likely misquote: an immaterial ALPHA cost is not a finding that the live absence is acceptable.
