Either build the sources or quote the headline for the book actually computed - screener lane's call. C3: the calibrated floors were measured at N=121 and re-verified at 129; adoption is monotone decreasing in N so an identical adopter set means identical scores and identical percentiles, and recomputing from the banked X7_RECONCILE margins gives 20 adopters at N=129 and 20 at N=135 with the set identical, so the floors still stand at this N. ONE SHIPPED-CODE CHANGE: quantile_backtest gained an OPT-IN return_series - it already computed the per-period draws and discarded them, so a paired comparison had no way to reach the shipped arithmetic and X3 had written a second implementation of the same series. Default payloads are bit-identical, pinned by test, and a further test pins the two implementations to agree. Equity N 131 -> 135, four arms; the halves are the same arms on subsets and are not charged, on session 7's precedent of charging 7 for 7 LOO arms measured both directions. |

THE JOIN WAS THE HARD PART. 13F keys on CUSIP, the universe keys on ticker, and no free CUSIP master exists, so the key is a recorded two-rung ladder: the company's own SC 13D/G filings carry its CUSIP and every candidate must pass the mod-10 CHECK DIGIT (398 names), then normalised exact name match (13); 49 ambiguous and 28 unmatched are refusals, never coin flips. Ownership anchor (13F dollars / market cap) passed 423/423 with median 0.682 - a 68pct institutional-ownership median across a large-cap screen is the strongest single evidence the join landed on the right issuers. TWO DEFECTS FOUND IN MY OWN INSTRUMENT. (a) The CUSIP check digit valued letters off a '0-9A-Z' alphabet - off by ten - which silently rejected every CUSIP containing a letter, i.e. most ADRs; caught by a KSPI fixture before the run. (b) THE PRE-REGISTERED ANCHOR IS ONE-SIDED IN EFFECT: (0,1.50] rejects implausibly HIGH ownership and waves through implausibly LOW, so a join onto a stale CUSIP nobody reports holding lands at ~1e-6 INSIDE the band. It passed 12 names - CMCSA, RIO, BTI, HSBC, MT, AMP, CM, MGA, SHG, IHG, TELNY, GALDY, IFNNY - megacaps credited with ONE reporting institution, two of which returned null only by luck. Fixed with a STRUCTURAL floor MIN_HOLDERS=2 (sm_breadth is growth in holder count; one holder cannot express breadth or its change), recorded as a tightening with BOTH figures published: 421/500 pre-registered, 411/500 tightened. Four names at 2-5 holders remain suspicious and are named rather than removed by tuning. A THIRD CONTAMINATION, REFUSED SAFELY: a company that is ITSELF an asset manager files SC 13Gs ABOUT OTHER ISSUERS and EDGAR's submissions feed carries filings it MADE as well as those naming it as subject - PFG returned six candidate CUSIPs at one vote each and most_common(1) would have broken that tie by insertion order. 29 of 500 names hit a tie; a tie is now a refusal. Join failure runs 26.8pct inside Financial Services against 13.2pct outside. THE INSIDER THEME COMES ALIVE AND ITS SHAPE IS THE FINDING: median 43.68, 179 names (35.8pct) exactly 50.0, but 278 below 50 against only 43 above - as constructed it is overwhelmingly a 'who is selling least' sort, not a 'who is buying' one. 16 names pin at exactly 10.0, the 50+40*tanh(pressure/4000) FLOOR, which corroborates audit item S3's saturation mechanism on live data. RATE LIMITS, V2F's lesson transferred: the 13F leg BATCHES ALL THE WAY (two ~100MB zips replace tens of thousands of per-filer fetches, ~31s to aggregate), while the per-ticker legs are LATENCY-bound not quota-bound - one serial process reached ~3 req/s against SEC's ~10 req/s ceiling, so four interleaved shards took ~3 hours down to ~48 minutes. SEC pushed back 27 times across the fleet and NONE was recorded: the manifest refuses a non-terminal status by construction, so a throttled unit is retried and coverage cannot inflate by hitting a wall. Final 500/500 on all three legs. report makes ZERO network calls. ADOPTION IS EXPLICITLY NOT LICENSED BY THIS: coverage is necessary, not sufficient. FLAGGED NOT DECIDED: conformance is a size and a cap and is SILENT ABOUT PROVENANCE - the published book is a decile of the DAILY LIVE SCAN (~594 names, FMP/free stack) while the contract's 86-name series was a decile of the FULL POINT-IN-TIME SHARADAR UNIVERSE (861 eligible); same construction, different universe, so holdings will NOT match name for name and BOTH satisfy the rule as written. publish() banks `source` into the ingest response and store meta so the difference is visible up front rather than inferred later from a divergence. Whether a live-scan-sourced book is the object the contract binds is a CONTRACT question - if not, conformance needs a provenance condition in the edge lane. LIVE STATE CHANGE ON NEXT SCAN, stated plainly: the engine holds 10 experiment-stamped names; the first conforming publish closes those 10 (not in the new book) and opens ~59, exits CLOSED not deleted per session 16, MIN_BOOK_RETENTION does not trip. 17 new tests (tests/test_index_book_publish.py); 37 suites, 1096 passed, 0 failed. CANNOT BE CONFIRMED IN PRODUCTION FROM HERE: the next scheduled hot scan (22:23 UTC weekdays) writes the file on Render - read the run log for 'index book: PUBLISHED' then /admin/run-paper-track for seed.seed_refused null. |

Any backup run over 19 minutes stamped the next calendar day. THE DAMAGE WAS NOT ONLY A WRONG LABEL: /admin/ingest-snapshot keys idempotency on hot_processed_{scan_date}, so two dates are two keys - the forward hot10 track recorded a SECOND pick row for the same close and the Discord digest posted twice, while the workflow comment calls the backup a no-op if the primary already landed. Both crons stamp the same UTC date (22:23 and 23:41), so the backup is idempotent as advertised. Does NOT by itself guarantee a trading-day date; that is LA7. |

(1) status('2026-08-08', today=2026-08-10) returned level 'fresh' with the message 'As of 2026-08-08 (last close).' - a Saturday, on which there was no close. The module whose entire purpose is stopping stale data being presented as fresh was endorsing a session that does not exist, and is_trading_day lived one import away and was never asked. (2) status('2026-12-24', today=2026-12-28) returned age_trading_days 2 for a gap containing exactly ONE session, counting Christmas Day. (3) THE STATED JUSTIFICATION ARGUED FOR THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THE CODE DID: 'Holidays are not modelled - the cost of being one day generous around Thanksgiving is far lower than the cost of crying wolf.' Not modelling them makes the computed age LARGER, so the badge fires EARLIER - that IS crying wolf. FIX: delegate to market_session, and refuse to call a non-trading-day snapshot fresh. A FOURTH DEFECT WAS FOUND WHILE FIXING IT AND IT WAS MINE: freshness.trading_days_between and market_session.trading_days_between had the SAME NAME in sibling modules and returned DIFFERENT answers for the same interval (2 vs 1 over Christmas) - the second was added by LA3 days earlier, so this batch created the collision it is now closing. Deliberately NOT a new `level`: app.js:1812-1815 switches on fresh / warn / stale / unknown and treats stale/unknown as red, so a misdated snapshot takes `warn` (a visible note, not an alarm) and carries machine-readable as_of_is_trading_day for anything stricter; the one thing it may never be is fresh. The badge got MORE generous, which is the direction the old docstring claimed to want, pinned against a naive re-implementation. |

| LA12 | LA | sector median_upside was computed over a stale subset while count reported the whole sector | **DONE** | FIXED - the median now carries its own denominator |  | HANDOFF_live_data_bugs.md Part 16 | 2026-08-11 | human | /api/hotstocks calls sector_attractiveness(all_rows) on rows straight from the database, BEFORE estimate_fair_values has run on anything - that runs only on the served slice. Only names given a full DCF carry an `upside`, and production runs SCAN_DCF_TOP=12 over the whole market, so _median (which drops Nones silently) returned a median over one or two names sitting beside a `count` reporting the entire sector: two populations in one row. FIX: ship median_upside_n beside it, so the number can never be read against the wrong denominator again. NO FLOOR WAS INVENTED and that was deliberate - a threshold here would be an uncalibrated constant, whereas a denominator lets the reader apply their own. `count` was NOT changed: it was never wrong, it was being read against a median that meant something else, and 'fixing' it would break the correct field to hide the symptom. Pinned by the invariant that median_upside_n is 0 exactly when median_upside is None. Nothing renders this today (app.js reads avg_composite only), which is the reason to fix it while it is still cheap rather than the reason to leave it. |
| LA14 | LA | market_holidays(y) could return a date in year y-1 | **DONE** | FIXED - the set contains only dates in the year it names |  | HANDOFF_live_data_bugs.md Part 16 | 2026-08-11 | human | _observed(date(y,1,1)) moves a Saturday New Year's Day to the PRECEDING Friday, which is 31 December of y-1. Measured before the fix: market_holidays(2028) contained 2027-12-31 and market_holidays(2033) contained 2032-12-31. DROPPING IT IS THE FACTUALLY CORRECT NYSE RULE, NOT MERELY TIDINESS: the exchange does not close on 31 December when 1 January falls on a Saturday, so the holiday is not observed at all - and the neighbouring year must not gain it either, which is checked (market_holidays(2027) does not contain 2027-12-31 and is_trading_day(2027-12-31) is True). It was inert for is_trading_day, which asks market_holidays(d.year) and so never saw the stray, and inert CORRECTLY for the same reason; the exposure is to any caller that ITERATES the set rather than testing membership, which would receive a date outside the year it asked for while the year that date belongs to silently lacked it. Nothing iterates it today - this closes the hole before something does. The raw computation is split into _holidays_unfiltered so the filter is visible and testable rather than folded into the comprehension that corrects it, and a test asserts the filter actually REMOVED something (a guard that passes because it never had anything to catch is not a guard) plus that ordinary years are untouched and no year from 1999 to 2060 leaks a date. |
| LA10 | LA | A withheld row keeps the labels that described the value it withheld | **DONE** | FIXED 2026-08-10 |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 24 | 2026-08-10 | app-fixer | THE SHAPE, NOT THE SIZE, IS WHY THIS WAS WORTH FIXING. `estimate_fair_values` writes `fair_value_method` ("blended") and `fair_value_confidence` ("medium") beside the number; `withhold.withhold_implausible_fair_values` then blanked `fair_value` and `upside` and left both labels standing, so a refused row shipped as `{fair_value: null, fair_value_method: "blended", fair_value_confidence: "medium", fair_value_withheld: true}` - the confidence of a number that is not in the payload. That is the same "a label outlives the value it described" shape `withhold.py` exists to eliminate, one level up: the original KSPI bug was a FIGURE surviving its own suppression, this is the DESCRIPTION of it surviving. FIXED AS ONE DEFINITION, NOT TWO EDITS: `publication.strip_derived_fields` is the single rule for what a refusal does to a row, and both refusal paths call it - the scan-side `record_refusal` and the web-side band withhold. Two places decide a row is withheld; there is now one definition of what that does, for the same reason that module owns the band. THE TWO FIELDS ARE TREATED DIFFERENTLY ON PURPOSE: `fair_value_method` is SET to "withheld" (already the project's vocabulary - `fairvalue.py`'s own refusal branch writes it and test_guards.py:316 pins it - and a positive label beats an empty cell, the same argument as writing a refusal REASON rather than leaving a gap), while `fair_value_confidence` is CLEARED, because it is a graded scale with no withheld rung and a word in a scale invites a renderer to rank it. NO VISIBLE CHANGE, AND THAT IS THE CORRECT OUTCOME: `app.js:1039` tests `fair_value_withheld` and returns "withheld" BEFORE it ever reads the method, so the page has always shown the right thing - the WIRE was what disagreed with it. The audit's "cosmetic today, a future renderer would pick it up" is confirmed exactly. PINNED BY EXTENDING THE CATCH-ALL, WHICH WAS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO IT: `_walk_fair_values` walked ratios, and a withheld row has no ratio, so every band assertion passed on precisely the rows carrying the stale labels. It now carries the two labels too, and `test_no_public_api_response_describes_a_fair_value_it_withheld` asserts a marked row has no live method or confidence anywhere in a public body, with a non-vacuity floor of >=2 withheld rows so it cannot become a test of nothing. Mutation-verified 3/3: the bug as reported, and BOTH half-fixes (method left standing; confidence left standing) - each caught on the real production AEG row (5.25x, $49.91 against $9.50). test_withhold 30/30; full gate 37 suites, every one exit 0. |
| LA13 | LA | surfaces.py claims every /api route is classified, and the exemption lived in the test | **DONE** | FIXED 2026-08-10 - and the gap under it was bigger than the claim |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 24 | 2026-08-10 | app-fixer | THE AUDIT FOUND A DOCSTRING GAP; UNDERNEATH IT WAS AN UNTESTED GUARD. `surfaces.py:63` promised "every registered /api route is knowingly on one side or the other - a new route lands in neither list and fails the suite", and `test_public.py:127` skipped `/api/option-alerts/` with a hard-coded prefix. The exemption is correct (both handlers call `_admin_ok()`), but it lived in the TEST, so the module stating the policy had no record that a third category existed, and a future admin-token route under a different prefix would have fallen through to public by default. FIXED BY WRITING THE THIRD SIDE DOWN: `ADMIN_TOKEN_PREFIXES`, derived from `private.ADMIN_PREFIXES` rather than restated (the list that lets these routes through the lockdown must be the list that classifies them here), plus `is_admin_token()` and `classify()` as the single reader. The suite now walks `classify` instead of keeping a second copy of the policy. THE FINDING THIS PRODUCED, WHICH THE AUDIT DID NOT NAME: no test anywhere asserted that these two endpoints REFUSE an un-tokened caller. The four references to them in the suite were a source-string check (test_intraday:327), an assertion that the lockdown lets them through (test_private:145, `always_open` is True - reachability, not refusal), and the two skips. Deleting `_admin_ok()` from a handler would have been caught by nothing. Classifying a route secures nothing, and a category is strictly WORSE than a hard-coded skip if it becomes a one-line way to move a route out of the public set while enforcing nothing - so `test_the_admin_token_category_is_not_a_way_to_leave_a_route_unguarded` calls every admin-token route for real, with no token, on every verb it accepts, and requires 401/403. Mutation-verified 3/3: dropping the category from `classify` (both routes report unclassified), dropping `_admin_ok()` from the handler (GET /api/option-alerts/open answered **200** to an anonymous caller - the one that matters), and drifting `private.ADMIN_PREFIXES` apart from the surfaces list. ALSO SWEPT, OUTSIDE THE ITEM'S WORDING: `/admin/*` is not classified by surfaces.py at all (it only speaks about /api), so the walk cannot see it and the same untested-guard gap applied. The enforcement pin was already generic, so `test_every_admin_route_refuses_a_caller_with_no_token` extends it. Measured first, then pinned: all 13 route-verbs ALREADY answer 401 with a real ADMIN_TOKEN set - the case that means anything, since an unset token fails closed trivially - so this pins a property the app had rather than reporting a hole. test_public 31/31 (was 27); full gate 37 suites, every one exit 0. |

Arm B (persistent reinvestment floor, explicit years AND terminal) passed all SEVEN of its original bounds and was rejected on harm the register never encoded - a passed-every-bound result under an unregistered rejection is the state that decays into 'we tested it and it worked'. PREREG_C_reinvestment_complete_bounds.md was committed ALONE at abeb4f7 before scripts/reinvestment_complete_bounds.py existed. THE REGISTER DOES NOT CLAIM BLINDNESS AND SAYS SO: Arm B's harm numbers were already published, so what is claimed instead is that the bounds were committed before the RE-RUN, that C1-C3 have no tunable threshold at all (an output is either still a number or it is not), and that the two bounds carrying real freedom state the range over which the verdict is invariant. RESULT: all seven original bounds HELD AGAIN on a fresh snapshot six days later - F1 0 violations, F2 33 to 0, F3 0, F4 median terminal FCFF -53.6pct, H1 0 of 98 control names moved, H2 0 flips, H3 median fair value -8.3pct - so Arm B really does what the original register asked and the original register asked the wrong questions. FIVE OF THE NEW BOUNDS FAIL: C1 EV stays positive 92.35pct against a 99pct floor (15 names), C2 terminal value 15 violations, C3 DCF 15, C4 8 fair values rose by more than 1pct, P2 all 26 capex-boom names raised year-1 reinvestment beyond the 10pct tolerance. VERDICT REJECTED-COMPLETE, mechanical. THE MECHANISM REPRODUCED ALMOST EXACTLY ACROSS TWO SNAPSHOTS AND THAT IS THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE HERE: charging MORE reinvestment RAISES the published fair value because blend._usable drops a non-positive lens and renormalises - EQIX +120.5pct against a recorded +121, GM +98.7 against +92, XEL +76.2 against +73. Not a one-snapshot artefact. THE 2026-08-05 241-NAME PICKLE IS UNRECOVERABLE (repo, both data trees, all three tracked handoff zips and every on-disk cache are empty) so the register fixed a substitute IN ADVANCE with zero discretionary name selection: the 191 bundled tickers plus exactly the seven foreign filers the Part 8 record itself names, 198 total, covering 33 of 33 decisive names and 10 of 10 named harm names, with five of the seven added names in the population the fix is meant to HELP. 196 of 198 fetched; the two misses are BRK.B (ticker format) and HES (acquired), not throttling. Every bound is SELF-RELATIVE - treated against control on the same snapshot, same process, same CompanyData objects - so the verdict is well defined on a rebuilt snapshot and only magnitude comparability is lost. VOID PRECONDITIONS WERE REGISTERED BECAUSE A THROTTLED FETCH EMPTIES THE TREATED POPULATION AND EVERY OUTPUT-VALIDITY BOUND THEN PASSES TRIVIALLY: 98.99pct fetched against a 95pct floor, 98 treated against 80, 33 decisive against 20, all cleared. TWO ESCAPE HATCHES FIRED AS REGISTERED AND BOTH CUT AGAINST THE REJECTION. C5 is INDECISIVE not violated - 71 changed against a 33-name decisive set is 2.15x, inside the band where the multiplier I chose would decide the answer - so it was EXCLUDED and the verdict rests on the other eleven; counting it would have made the rejection look stronger than it is. And H1 was scored on the WRONG FIELDS first: the first cut compared ev, tv and dcf as well, which is STRICTER than the registered bound and made H1 read VIOLATED on one name, so it was corrected to exactly the fields the register names and H1 HOLDS. Re-scoring a bound on fields the register does not list, after seeing the result, is the exact error this task exists to correct. THREE BUGS FOUND DURING THE REBUILD, all before they could corrupt a number. (1) V2G's dataset reader assumed a FLAT zip; SEC packs 01sep2025-30nov2025 flat and 01jun2025-31aug2025 nested under a directory, so opening by bare name raised KeyError and killed the run - it never bit V2G because both quarters it happened to need are flat, and a reader that works on the two archives you first tried is not a reader that works on the format. Measured end to end on 60 names: institutional 0 to 26 non-null with 26 distinct, insider 1 to 20 distinct - it had been the constant 0.0 that factors.py:284 writes when insider_score is absent, which is why a 100pct-covered column carried no information. WHY +10pp IS THE WRONG BAR, ARGUED FROM THE DISTRIBUTION RATHER THAN FROM WANTING TO PASS. Attainability, measured over all 33 exit arms ever scored on the 3,885-trade book: exactly ONE clears +10pp, tp100_only at +10.78pp, and it FAILS paired-cell FDR, lifts tail concentration from 86.75% to 92.75%, and per inventory item J fails the name-year sign test on the alert book at z -5.76 while passing on random at +10.55 - so the bar's rejection region over this family holds one point and it is occupied by the one arm the record already refuses. A bar only a discredited arm can clear is not selecting for quality; on this family it selects for removing the stop. THE UNIT IS THE REAL PROBLEM AND LOWERING THE NUMBER DOES NOT FIX IT: this book's expectancy is a tail statistic - hit rate 35.3%, median trade -52.2%, 86.75% of gross winnings from the tail - and the GAINS are worse, with the top 1% of trades carrying 106% to 210% of each arm's entire gain, meaning the other 99% are collectively negative. THE OBVIOUS FIX IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT AND IT IS THE ONE I EXPECTED TO ENDORSE: switching to a RELATIVE bar because the payoff is multiplicative fails on the two books we have - between look 1 and look 2 the absolute gain drifts +17% (tp200) and +52% (tp150) while the relative gain drifts +62% and +109%, because the baseline expectancy itself fell -27.6%, so the ABSOLUTE figure is two to four times the more stable unit. Reported because it cuts against adopting. The bar is also an order of magnitude from the design's own resolution: month-block clustered SEs on the paired gain run 0.29pp to 2.10pp, resolving ~0.6-4.2pp at t=2. No new market data is needed. THE HARNESS REPRODUCES THE RECORD INDEPENDENTLY: this scorer was written for the path study, not for O1, and returns shipped at +3.410308%/trade with gains of +3.1948/+3.8238pp against O1's banked expectancy_diff 0.031947661/0.038237652 - the same numbers to four decimals from a separately-written path rebuild. WHAT THE REJECTION DOES AND DOES NOT SAY, recorded because it is easy to over-read: it does NOT say raising the take-profit does nothing - every other condition says the effect is real, broad and entry-independent. It says the arm is not DISTINGUISHED WITHIN ITS OWN FAMILY, because 53 of 100 arbitrary jitters beat the shipped rule and the best five beat it by +5.2 to +8.1pp, all the same wider-stop-higher-target-longer-hold shape; when a whole REGION looks good on one corpus the corpus cannot say where inside it the optimum is, and picking one point after five looks is choosing by hindsight. THE OBJECTION IS STATED RATHER THAN LEFT TO A READER: a narrower null would give a lower bar and tp150 might clear it - which is exactly why the construction was fixed in section 3 before it ran and bound whatever it returned, since choosing the null after seeing which one passes is the move the document exists to refuse. BY-PRODUCT UNFLATTERING TO THE INHERITED RULE, reported because it cuts that way: the shipped +100%/-50%/half-DTE exit is slightly BELOW the median of its own family (53/100 beat it, +0.803pp at the median), so it is an ordinary member rather than a local optimum - and it CANNOT be acted on for precisely the reason tp150 was refused, so nobody may later present the shipped exit as validated by this exercise. THE DEFECT IS A FALSE SENTENCE IN THE FLATTERING DIRECTION, WHICH IS WHY IT WAS WORTH MORE THAN A LABEL FIX. Every surface rendered `len(series)` - rows the recorder managed to write - under the word "Days", beside "Alpha / yr" and "Sharpe", and the server's own note read 'Live track is 2 trading days old - far too short to judge' while the track was 7 trading days old. The reader is told THE RECORD IS SHORT; the true statement is THE RECORDER IS MISSING 71% OF ITS ROWS - a different problem, a different owner (PT-WRITER, Cowork lane), and one somebody might act on. The single number that would have made the recording failure visible was being spent to say something else. THERE ARE THREE CLOCKS AND THE FIX IS TO NAME ALL THREE, not to swap one for another: ROWS gate ('have we recorded enough to say anything?'), ELAPSED-TO-LAST-ROW annualises (LA3's fix, the window the return accrued over), and AGE displays (inception -> today). valuation/screener/track_age.py is the third, and it is deliberately NOT a restatement of the second: `_elapsed_trading_days` stops at the last recorded row, so a recorder that died three weeks ago leaves it FROZEN and the track appears to stop ageing at the moment it stopped being written - the most flattering failure mode available, and invisible in every other field. Age runs to today, so a dead recorder widens a visible gap instead of going quiet. Pinned by test_the_default_clock_is_today_and_not_the_last_recorded_row, which exercises the PRODUCTION default (every other test passes `today` explicitly and would pass with the defect restored - the mutation harness found that, not reasoning). THE GATE DID NOT MOVE AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING: MIN_LIVE_DAYS still counts recorded rows, because moving it onto elapsed time would let a gappy track reach the floor sooner and advance the public backtested->live posture on days nobody recorded. LA3 recorded that reasoning; this change is where it could quietly have been undone. `thin` alone cannot detect the move (the contract gate is independently unpassed, so `thin` stays true either way) - the NOTE's branch is the only observable that separates the two rules, and the test asserts on it. SIX SURFACES, ONE SOURCE: the three server notes, the hero band (index.html), the landing page, and two sites in app.js. All read the same `age` dict off the payload; the card is pinned against computing its own (no `new Date(`, no `Date.parse`, no `86400` beside it), so the card, the band, the landing page and the server cannot disagree about how old the track is. Rows are still shown - as a SECOND number, 'Recorded', appearing only when it differs from the age - because hiding the row count would be the same defect with the other number. THE GAPLESS CASE IS BYTE-IDENTICAL, which is what makes it safe to land: on a track written every trading day, recorded == age and phrase() returns exactly the string the old f-string built, pinned across n = 1/2/5/60/252. So the display changes only where it was wrong. CONTRACT POSTURE UNTOUCHED, pinned verbatim: 'It is shown for transparency, not as evidence, and the headline stays on the backtest' and 'Elapsed time alone does not promote a live number'. The one posture word that DID change is the floor's unit - '60-day floor' -> '60-recorded-day floor', and app.js 'withheld until N trading days' -> 'N RECORDED trading days' - because leaving the unit ambiguous beside a now-correct age re-creates the exact conflation the item names. 22 tests; 16/16 mutations caught, each by the test that names it, including the three that initially MISSED (a duplicated literal 0 that made the negative-gap clamp untestable; the default-clock gap above; and the gate move hiding behind `thin`) - all three were real holes in the pins and were fixed rather than argued away. | V3-LABEL | LA | Vintage 2 is live and no surface names the vintage the record belongs to | **DONE** | FIXED 2026-08-11 - and the commissioned label was off by one on both numbers: the live vintage is 3, shadowed by 2 |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 27 | 2026-08-11 | app-fixer | OUT-OF-BAND (vintage-rule surfacing, not one of the 134); build_ledger.py regenerates from the 134 audit ids only and will DROP this row. THE COMMISSIONED LABEL WAS OFF BY ONE ON BOTH NUMBERS, AND THAT IS THE FINDING, NOT A TYPO. The work was requested as 'Book vintage 2 since 2026-08-11 (capital_discipline restored); vintage 1 runs in shadow'. Measured against the register the same hour: track_meter.current_vintage() returns VINTAGE 3, opened 2026-08-11, and shadow_vintage.open_pairs() returns exactly one pair, live 3 shadowed by 2. Vintage 2 was opened by Amendment 1 on 2026-08-10 and CLOSED after ONE accrued day by the theme restoration; vintage 1 is the VOIDED run #1. So the requested string would have published a wrong vintage number and a wrong shadow on the one surface whose entire job is to say which book the numbers describe -- and it would have been wrong from the day it shipped, because the restoration had already taken the vintage. THE FIX IS THAT THE LABEL IS DERIVED, NEVER TYPED. track_meter.vintage_label() rebuilds the sentence from VINTAGES: the open vintage's number, its opening date, its own short label, and the immediately preceding vintage as the shadow. Each register row gained a <=60-char `label` field so the parenthetical also comes from the register rather than being invented at the surface. Rendered: 'Book vintage 3 since 2026-08-11 (capital_discipline restored); vintage 2 runs in shadow'. PINNED TO THE DERIVATION, NOT TO THE NUMBER. tests/test_track_meter.py already learned this the hard way -- it used to assert 'it is vintage 2', and a legitimate vintage event then failed a test that exists to catch two vintages being open at once. So no test here asserts '3'; they reconstruct the phrase from the register and compare, and a single-vintage register is mutated in to prove the no-predecessor branch emits no '; None runs in shadow' stub. TWO SOURCES FOR ONE FACT, CROSS-CHECKED RATHER THAN MERGED: track_meter answers 'which vintage is open', shadow_vintage.open_pairs() answers 'can its predecessor actually be SCORED' (it needs a pinned parameter snapshot). The label computes the predecessor from its own register -- importing shadow_vintage into track_meter would invert the module's dependency direction for a number it already holds -- and tests/test_vintage_label.py asserts the two never disagree. V1's OUTBOUND FENCE HELD AND WAS RE-ASSERTED FROM THE NEW SURFACE. test_shadow_vintage.py forbids the string 'shadow_vintage' anywhere under valuation/web or valuation/saas (PT-OUTBOUND published a research FIGURE to Discord). The label reaches the web layer via track_meter ONLY; a mutation that adds shadow_vintage to app.py's import is caught. The label also carries NO measurement -- no return, excess or paired difference -- pinned by a test that rejects float values and measurement-shaped keys. Naming a vintage is bookkeeping; publishing its paired difference is the thing the fence exists to stop. OWNER-ONLY, AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING: the label ships on /api/index-track and, via paper_track's contract_track block, on /api/track -- both in surfaces.OWNER_ONLY_PATHS, pinned by a test, because the argument that this is safe to render rests on it. PUBLIC POSTURE UNTOUCHED, pinned: the backtested/live gate still reads `long_enough = days >= MIN_LIVE_DAYS` (recorded rows, LA8's rule), and a mutation adding the vintage to landing.html is caught. SECOND HALF OF THE BRIEF -- THE THEME LEGEND -- AND THE ANSWER IS SPLIT. THE BARS WERE ALWAYS DATA-DRIVEN and picked the fifth theme up on their own: _themeBars enumerates Object.entries(w) over whatever weights the payload carries, and health.theme_contributing (the 'themes not driving this scan' warning) is computed server-side over settings.FACTORS_ALL. Confirmed by running them, not by reading them. WHAT WAS HARDCODED WAS THE CAPTION UNDER THE BAR, and on 2026-08-11 it was wrong on TWO counts about the one theme the whole day was about: THEME_INPUTS.capital_discipline read 'low share issuance . low asset growth (dormant - needs data)'. It was NOT dormant -- it had just become the fifth live theme, the adoption that opened vintage 3 -- and factors.py:265 computes the theme as df[['z_neg_issuance']].mean(axis=1), ISSUANCE ONLY, asset growth having been deliberately removed for cancelling out the one input that works. A confident wrong caption is worse than a missing bar: a missing bar invites a question, a caption closes one. FIXED BY MOVING THE COPY TO PYTHON, following the score_confidence.py / hold_horizon.py convention already in the tree: valuation/web/theme_status.py is injected as window.THEME_STATUS and app.js only escapes it. Dormancy is now rendered as its own flagged line rather than folded into the ingredient list. The dead themes say WHY: institutional and insider FAILED the PREREG_theme_restoration.md fidelity gate (Spearman +0.17 and +0.36 against 0.60) -- 'needs data' would have implied the fix is a download, and it is not; low_risk is described as zeroed on evidence, not missing. DELIBERATELY NOT DERIVED FROM A LIVE SCAN: health.theme_contributing measures what survived standardization TODAY and is the right instrument for the scan-health warning, but issuance.py fails to None on an SEC outage by design, so driving the legend off it would make a transient outage read as a retired theme. The legend states the DESIGN, the health block states the DAY. 30 tests (16 vintage-label, 14 theme-status); 19/19 mutations caught, 0 missed, 0 skipped -- including a first pass where 6 SKIPPED silently because multi-line anchors were written with \n against CRLF files and one reason text carries an em dash, which is how a pin gets credited without ever being exercised. ZERO RESEARCH TRIALS -- a labelling and copy fix, no measurement, no scoring change, so NO VINTAGE EVENT: equity N unchanged. |

| V6-LOG | V | Scream-buy record rebuilt - archived reset, entry/target/stop/DTE/status schema, read-time current price | **DONE** | SHIPPED 2026-08-13 - archived reset built, live record NOT yet reset (it is on Render, zero rows locally) |  | HANDOFF_live_data_bugs.md Part 20 | 2026-08-13 | human | Don's direction 2026-08-13, PROMPT_dip_detector_and_screamtrack.md item 2: "the options scream buys track record wiped, and include target sale, price bought in, and current price, same as our paper account tracks." This is the BACKEND half only - schema, archive, status vocabulary and the read-time quote; the tab and its footer are the app fixer's V6 product half and are NOT covered by this row. THE FINDING THAT DECIDED THE DESIGN: every local database holds ZERO scream-buy alerts (.scan-cache/screener.db 0, data/screener.db 0, data/live_cache/served.db 0, data/app.db no such table), because the real record lives in SQLite on the Render service's disk - the same fact track-backup.yml exists to work around. So the reset could NOT be executed here and no code path pretends otherwise; the archive manifest reports n_rows, n_by_status and n_by_epoch so an empty archive is visibly empty rather than quietly successful, which is LA2's lesson that a relative guard cannot catch a quantity that was always zero. THE RESET ARCHIVES AND THEN DOES NOT DELETE EITHER: archive-then-delete still destroys the rows and a dated JSON file is a strictly weaker object than a database row since it cannot be queried, joined or scored, so reset_record stamps a record_epoch and starts a new one and the prior record stays queryable forever - the same reasoning paper_index_closed already carries, that rows leave a book when they do badly so erasing them flatters what remains. It writes the archive FIRST and moves the epoch ONLY if that succeeded, because the other order leaves a reset record with no archive which is the silent wipe itself; pinned by a test that makes the directory unwritable and asserts the epoch did not move. A second reset on one day cannot overwrite the first archive, it takes a -2 suffix. A NULL record_epoch reads as the ORIGINAL record, never as unknown. THE STATUS IS DERIVED, NOT STORED, a deliberate departure from the literal ask of "a status field the close path maintains": the close path already maintains two fields the status is a pure function of, so a third stored copy can disagree with them (the B7 disease in miniature), and deriving answers a case storing cannot - an alert whose contract EXPIRED with nobody closing it keeps status=open forever, so a stored field would read LIVE for a contract that has not existed for months. THE TRAP IN THE VOCABULARY IS THAT time_stop CONTAINS stop: a substring match checking stop first reports every scheduled close as a stop-out, which is the opposite claim about expectancy, so matching is on the leading token and audit B5d's [pnl vs fill] suffix is stripped rather than tripping it. An unmapped reason reads CLOSED (unscoreable) and never LIVE. The coverage test is vacuity-proof: it parses paper_track._exit_decision out of its own SOURCE with ast, collects the literal strings it can return and fails if any lacks a status, plus asserts the enumeration found at least four tokens so it cannot pass by seeing nothing - M3's finding that a registry-driven guard cannot see an unregistered value. CURRENT PRICE IS NEVER PERSISTED: it is fetched at READ time and stale-marked, because a stored current price is a price that was current once and begins lying the moment it is written; a missing quote or one with no timestamp reads stale with a None price, since absent is not fresh and is not zero. Tradier reports epoch MILLISECONDS and reading them as seconds would date every quote to 1970 and mark it stale forever, which fails safe but makes the flag useless rather than informative - handled and pinned. live_quotes_for quotes only LIVE contracts so the broker fan-out does not grow by one call per historical alert forever, and degrades to stale marks on an outage rather than taking the tab down. ONE DERIVATION OF THE EXIT LEVELS, AND IT REMOVED A DUPLICATE: paper_track had the target/stop arithmetic written out TWICE, inline in _place_entry and again in _levels_from, which is how a corrected level and an uncorrected one come to coexist - the exact defect session 16 found in these same exit levels. Both now call scream_log.levels_for and a test asserts the inline form is gone from the source. levels_for returns {} for a missing or non-positive entry rather than zeros, because a target derived from no entry price is not a level and the caller must be able to tell no-policy-applied from target-happens-to-be-0. The +100pct target is a DEFAULT not a constant: an alert that logged its own policy keeps it, which is why the policy is stored on the row rather than read from settings at display time, and policy_is_default says which happened. M6's FIELD-LEVEL GUARD is applied to this payload - records() raises PayloadSchemaError if a stored column reaches the projection unaccounted for, iterating the columns the DATABASE actually returned rather than a registry, and it is tested both ways: a column added behind the projection's back fails the run AND the guard is shown to be inspecting 25-plus real columns so it is not passing by seeing nothing. notify.py was NOT edited - the epoch is stamped in options_tracker.log_alert so every caller lands in the right era without touching saas/. NOT DONE AND NAMED SO: the LIVE record has not been reset and cannot be from a dev box; the step is SL.reset_record(store, out_dir) behind an admin route (web lane) or python -m valuation.edge.scream_log --reset on the service, and the archive it writes lands on Render's disk so it needs exposing through the same /admin/export-track path the backup Action uses if it is to reach the repo. Until it runs the tab correctly shows the original epoch. The reset is deliberately NOT wired into any scan - it is a dated act on Don's direction and takes an explicit flag. Zero trials - a product/reporting change with no hypothesis and no threshold. 14 test groups in tests/test_scream_log.py; full gate 69 suites 0 failures. |
| V6-PRODUCT | LA | Dip Detector tab + scream-buy record reset and rebuilt (product half of V6) | **DONE** | SHIPPED 2026-08-13 - screen only; NO forward-return claim, gated on the V6 register |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 28 | 2026-08-13 | app-fixer | OUT-OF-BAND (product, Don's direction, PROMPT_dip_detector_and_screamtrack.md; not one of the 134, and build_ledger.py regenerates from the 134 audit ids only and will DROP this row). THE PRODUCT HALF OF V6 ONLY. The research half - whether healthy names in drawdown actually recover better than the market - is the pipeline lane's pre-registration and is NOT answered here; this row adopts nothing and measures nothing about forward returns. THE FINDING: the quantity the screen sorts on is computed by every scan and then THROWN AWAY. prices.get_quote computes high_prox = price / trailing-252 max for every name (the momentum theme z-scores it), and drawdown is exactly 1 - high_prox, but screen.py::_rows_from persists only the CROSS-SECTIONAL Z-SCORE. A z-score cannot be inverted to a percentage - it is (x - mean)/sd within the date, so the same z is a different drawdown on every scan date - and rendering it as one would put a fabricated per-name number on a public surface. WHAT IS TRUE, AND IS WHY THE TAB IS AFFORDABLE: standardisation within a date is strictly monotone, so ordering by z_high_prox ascending is IDENTICALLY ordering by drawdown descending. So the z is an exact RANKING key and a useless THRESHOLD key; the screen ranks on it, takes a bounded shortlist and MEASURES the real percentage for that shortlist only. Every drawdown shown is a ratio of two prices. A CHECK THAT DID NOT RUN IS NOT A CHECK THAT PASSED: of Don's four disqualifiers, `withheld` and the fail-closed `no_data` kind are on every snapshot row, while `terminal_share` and `beta_provenance` exist only where a DCF ran - rows carry per-check pass/fail/not_run and the surface says which, the same distinction holdout_theme_validate had to learn (oos_directions_tested = 0 is not a negative result). The health floor is DERIVED not invented: 66 is where scoring._recommendation stops saying Hold and starts saying Buy, and app.js::scoreColor independently uses it; momentum is deliberately EXCLUDED because a name 20% off its high has poor momentum by construction. THE POSTURE IS ONE CONSTANT: every claim-bearing sentence is server-rendered from web/dip_posture.py, the template holds none of it (pinned), the V6 close-out flips dip_posture.STATUS, NULL is exactly as reachable as POSITIVE (shadow_vintage's no-sign-branch rule), a half-finished flip renders visibly empty rather than plausible, the Discord/email digest stays blocked while the register is open (derived from STATUS, not set by hand), and a BANNED list of advice and prediction phrasings is enforced against the RENDERED HTML in both the public and owner renders - including 'sentiment-driven', banned though Don used it, because attributing a drawdown to sentiment is a causal claim and this screen reads no news, flow or positioning. SCREAM RECORD - AND THIS HALF WAS REWRITTEN MID-SESSION, WHICH IS THE FINDING. The prompt says greeks owns the logger and warns 'do not build a second logger'; valuation/edge/scream_log.py did not exist when this lane started, so the tab was built against paper_option_orders, and greeks landed at 7e4ddf2 while this was in flight. (a) 'PRICE BOUGHT IN' WAS THE BROKER FILL: scream_log.entry_premium is the ALERT-TIME premium, paper_option_orders.entry_premium is the sandbox fill - two different books, and SESSION 16 EXISTS BECAUSE THOSE TWO WERE CONFLATED, which the standalone version quoted at length while making the same mistake one layer up. (b) THE EPOCH WAS A DATE COMPARISON: the real boundary is record_epoch, stamped by reset_record, and THE RECORD HAS NOT BEEN RESET AND CANNOT BE FROM A DEV BOX (every local database holds zero scream-buy rows; the real one is on Render's disk), so a date-based epoch would have hidden every earlier row AS THOUGH A RESET HAD RUN - for a track record the worst available failure, because it looks reset. IMPLEMENTED AS ANONYMOUS == DEMO: one flag, PUBLIC_FULL_VIEW, lifting the anonymous tier to the read-only full view the /work button already grants - Track Record, Edge Lab, Index, Signals, Watchlist - and nothing beyond it. WHY IT IS A NEW FLAG RATHER THAN OWNER_SPLIT=false, WHICH IS THE OBVIOUS LEVER AND IS THE WRONG ONE: turning the split off also makes surfaces.may_act true for everyone, which hands anonymous callers /api/scan/run, /api/signals/run, /api/backtest/run and both Edge Lab runners - a free DoS lever on a 512MB box that spends Don's FMP and Anthropic budget on every request, and the store writes that go with it. PUBLIC_FULL_VIEW cannot do that: it is read-only BY CONSTRUCTION because may_act does not consult it, and that is pinned STRUCTURALLY by reading may_act's source (docstring stripped, since the docstring discusses the flag) rather than only by the combinations someone thought to enumerate. IT REUSES THE DEMO RAIL ENTIRELY rather than adding a parallel one, which is what keeps the blast radius small - DEMO_DENIED_PATHS still applies to a stranger under the flag, so every trigger plus /account, /account/alerts, /billing/checkout and /billing/portal stay 403, and a test asserts anonymous and demo return the IDENTICAL decision on every owner-only path, i.e. the lift is exactly the demo tier and not wider. THE LIVE-APP TEST CARRIES ITS OWN CONTROL because create_saas_app IS IDEMPOTENT - a test that builds a "second app" with different flags gets the FIRST app back and passes vacuously - so the flag is flipped on the live CONFIG and the test FAILS if flipping it changed nothing observable. THE CODE DEFAULT STAYS FALSE so a fork, a test box or a fresh instance is never ungated by accident; production opts in via render.yaml, and only an explicit "true" ungates ("yes" fails closed). RAW VENDOR ROWS ARE A LICENCE QUESTION AND "I KNOW THE RISKS" ANSWERS FOR LIABILITY, NOT FOR A VENDOR'S TERMS - they do not move: this grants the demo tier, so the route-by-route audit that cleared demo (Session 18) applies unchanged, and a test asserts DEMO_DENIED_VENDOR_ROWS still exists AND is still consulted so the next Sharadar-backed READ route is not published by default. Disclaimers, vintage labels and paper-account framing are untouched because the demo view IS the owner view rendered by the same templates. One value, no code change, nothing deleted, OWNER_SPLIT stays true underneath - and a test asserts render.yaml actually carries the key and tells the next reader how to flip it. 14 tests. Zero trials. AMENDED 2026-08-14 (MA9): the sentence above - "the regate is one value, no code change, nothing deleted" - WAS WRONG in the direction that matters, and render.yaml said the same thing. PUBLIC_FULL_VIEW=false restores a posture whose only gate is DEMO_ACCESS_TOKEN, and that token's VALUE was being rendered into the HTML of /work, an anonymous page. One-panel caveat attached. THE PER-NAME HALF OF THE V6-B CLOSE-OUT, and it is the fidelity check V6's own register demanded in advance. V6B-PRODUCT published M1 as a PARAGRAPH about a population; this attaches it to the twelve rows a reader is actually looking at. ADOPTS NOTHING - no vintage event, no weight change, nothing enters the composite, and the field is computed AFTER every membership decision so it cannot reach one. THE OWED CHECK: V6's register wrote, before any result existed, that a POSITIVE would NOT license the tab's copy without a separate live-vs-panel fidelity check, because coverage is not fidelity. M1 came back POSITIVE, so the check was owed at the level of the CLASSIFICATION - a rate is only this name's class's rate if this name is classified the way the measured rows were. BOTH OF M1'S CLAUSES TRANSFER, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS BUILDABLE: quality theme z-score is on the scan snapshot as z_quality, written by screen.py from the same build_frame theme column the panel uses, and the 0-100 health score comes from engine.scoring._health_score which M1 CALLED rather than retyping. The rule is reproduced exactly INCLUDING ITS ASYMMETRY - quality strict, health inclusive - pinned from both sides, and pinned against the measurement SCRIPT rather than retyped so a moved boundary breaks the suite instead of silently re-pointing a published rate. THE FINDING, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED, AND IT CHANGES WHAT THE FIELD MEANS: a name that lists on the Dip Detector can essentially NEVER classify UNHEALTHY. Two structural reasons - the screen's prefilter already drops z_quality below 0, which is M1's quality clause less its strictness, and the screen lists on health at least 66 while M1's floor is 50. A sweep of the live gates against M1's rule found the unhealthy class reachable at EXACTLY z_quality 0.0 and nowhere else. So this is NOT a discriminator between two groups; it is a VERIFICATION that each listed name really is in the measured group - which is precisely what licenses attaching the rate at all - and the 43.4% figure is a comparison baseline describing names this screen does not show, stated as such. FOUR HONEST REFUSALS, each firing on real rows: DEPTH (M1 measured names at least 20% down, the control reaches 10%, so a widened slider puts rows outside the measured population on the page - they get their class and NO rate, with the reason); CASH-BURNING (_health_score has two branches and V6-B's panel could not supply the burner branch's input, so its own C4 reports ZERO burner rows - a live burner is excluded rather than scored against a rate that never saw its kind, detected by reading the shipped classification.is_cash_burning flag rather than re-deriving it, audit B7's class); UNCLASSIFIABLE (a missing input returns None and never a class - defaulting would attach 43.4% to a name nobody scored, or 32.5%, and the second is the flattering direction); and SIZE. THE SIZE FLAG IS ONE-DIRECTIONAL BY CONSTRUCTION: the effect runs -14.287pp in the smallest tier to -3.787pp in the largest and the largest is the one quintile that does not hold in both halves on its own, but the artifact publishes each quintile's MEDIAN and NOT its edges, so a live name cannot be assigned to a measured tier and inventing edges would put a confident fabricated number on a public surface. What IS sound is above the line only - a name above the top quintile's own median is necessarily inside the top quintile, since that median sits at about the 90th percentile of the whole distribution - so the flag is True or unknown and NEVER False, because False would read as 'this name is in a tier where the effect is stronger', which the data cannot support. V3's RULE SHAPES THE COPY: a measured group average is not a statement about the next name a reader clicks, and a percentage on a row is the most natural way to be read as this company's chance - so every label says 'of these names', NOT_A_PROBABILITY travels with every number, and the field is named further_fall_rate and never risk, probability or odds (pinned). Both classes are written out in full so the unflattering classification is exactly as legible as the flattering one - dip_posture's NULL-as-reachable-as-POSITIVE rule applied to a class. SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-08-16 by V6B-RENDER: this row also said each label 'quotes the other', which was true when written and is now deliberately false - the per-name label carries NO comparison, because r1 measured that the live screen cannot make M1's comparison at all. The contrast moved to one screen-level note. Equal legibility is untouched. METHOD_NOTE says out loud that the measurement's 'healthy' is a LOWER bar than the one the screen lists on, because a reader who knows the screen gates at 66/66/66 would otherwise assume the rate was measured on names that cleared 66/66/66; it was not. The banned list is IMPORTED from dip_posture rather than copied and asserted against the RENDERED payload - its DISTRESS family is the live risk, since a further-fall statistic sits one paraphrase from a bankruptcy claim whose own arm M2 is VOID on power. Coverage ships beside the rows with a per-reason breakdown, per the standing coverage rule, and an EMPTY screen reports coverage None rather than 1.0. A source sweep fails if the rate ever reaches a sort key or a filter predicate, since ranking on a measured rate is an adoption needing the both-halves gate; the sweep carries its own vacuity check. valuation/web/dip_risk.py new, dip.py wired at the one point where drawdown, sub-scores and the snapshot row coexist for one company at one moment. THE REGISTER GATE IS ENFORCED AT RUNTIME AND NOT MERELY PINNED: if RISK_STATUS ever stops being POSITIVE, for_name withdraws every rate AND THE CLASS WITH IT, because 'healthy' rendered beside a withdrawn measurement still invites the lookup the withdrawal exists to stop - digest_eligible's own lesson applied one surface over, since a test alone catches that only for as long as the test survives the edit. tests/test_dip_risk.py 31/31 at the time of this row (43/43 after V6B-RENDER); test_dip.py 46/46 unchanged. Zero trials - a DISPLAY of an already-measured statistic, with no hypothesis, no threshold and no verdict. |
| MA1 | MA | A scheduled job can change the live scoring weights, invisibly to the vintage contract | **DONE** | DISARMED 2026-08-14; production VERIFIED CLEAN twice by two instruments - the job had fired once, 2026-08-01, and adopted nothing; refusal path confirmed live 2026-08-15T12:21:47Z | 4063f6f + f8f6d31 | HANDOFF_live_data_bugs.md Part 21 | 2026-08-15 | audit3-ingest, closed by greeks | MASTER AUDIT MA1, CRITICAL, DISARMED. THE LOOP WAS ARMED AND EVERY LINK WAS REAL, SHIPPED AND TESTED: auto-scan.yml ran a monthly cron (0 12 1 * *) POSTing /admin/run-learning, which ran autolearn.run_learning, which called store.save_learned(..., adopted=True), which screen._effective_weights then PREFERRED over settings.WEIGHTS_ESTABLISHED. Next firing 2026-09-01. So a scheduled job could change the composite users receive by writing a row into Render's own SQLite - NO code commit, NO diff, NO review - and PAPER_TRACK_CONTRACT 5a's vintage rule could never have fired, because track_meter.VINTAGES is a literal tuple in Python source and there is no path from a database row to it. The forward track would have kept accruing under the old vintage while the model beneath it changed, which is the exact condition Amendment 1 VOIDED vintage 1 for and the thing three clock resets in four days were paid to avoid. WHAT WAS DONE, five parts. (1) cfg.learn_enabled defaults FALSE and is documented in ENV_REFERENCE.md; the audit measured that the string LEARN_ENABLED appeared in no .md, .yml, .example or .bat file in the repository while defaulting ON. Its parse also changed from 'anything except false is true' to 'only true is true', so an unrecognised value now fails closed. (2) The learn job AND its cron line are removed from auto-scan.yml, with the removed curl quoted in the comment that replaces them so the next reader can see what was there; note it ran with a trailing or-true, so it could not even fail loudly. (3) The adoption path REFUSES unless a vintage register entry exists: new valuation/edge/weight_adoption.py requires BOTH the OPEN vintage in track_meter.VINTAGES to carry a weights_adoption entry naming the bucket AND a row in PAPER_TRACK_CONTRACT.md signed by Don naming that SAME vintage number. Both are TRACKED SOURCE, so each takes a commit, a diff and the auto-land gate; neither is reachable by a cron with an admin token. The signature names a vintage on purpose - a bare YES would authorise the first adoption and every later one forever, so tying it to the open vintage's number makes Rule 6's five-year clock reset a conscious payment each time. ENFORCED AT store.save_learned, the one funnel every weight writer passes through, rather than at each call site: gating the callers is how there came to be two of them, and the next writer someone adds would be ungated by default. IT FAILS CLOSED IN EVERY DIRECTION - unreadable contract, absent register key, malformed row, signature naming another vintage, zero or two OPEN vintages, and any exception at all all read NOT AUTHORISED - because the asymmetry is not close: a wrong refusal costs a month of not re-tuning weights CPCV has declined on every run this project has done, and a wrong adoption silently invalidates the forward test, which is the only out-of-sample evidence there is and costs five years to rebuild. (4) MA3's second writer is caught by the same funnel - see the MA3 row. (5) VERIFIED LOCALLY, NOT IN PRODUCTION, and the difference is stated rather than glossed: data/screener.db holds learned_config with ZERO rows and ZERO adopted, .scan-cache/screener.db and data/live_cache/served.db do not exist, data/app.db has no such table. The live record is on Render's disk and this lane has no token, so a new READ-ONLY GET /admin/learned-weight-status and a CLI (python -m valuation.edge.weight_adoption --status) answer it in one call instead of a SQL console. THE READ SIDE REFUSES TOO, AND REPORTS RATHER THAN REPAIRING: an adopted row written before this gate existed stays exactly where it is, stops reaching the scorer, and is named with its date by live_override_report - deleting it would neutralise a live vintage violation and leave the record looking clean. (a) THE EQUAL-WEIGHT BASELINE WAS COMPUTED, QUOTED IN THE ADOPT VERDICT AND EXCLUDED FROM THE DECISION: line 87 computed eq_oos, line 101 wrote 'Recommended over equal-weight (OOS ...)' into the verdict, and eq_oos appeared nowhere in the accepted expression - so the learner could adopt weights that were WORSE out-of-sample than the incumbent while saying in writing that they beat it. That is this project's own named class (B8's rule_fired, LA5's health block) sitting in the one function that could move the live book. Now a leg of accepted, failing CLOSED when eq_oos is NaN because an incomparable baseline is not a beaten one. MEASURED on 40 synthetic panels where 50/50 is the true optimum: the equal-weight leg binds on 19 to 29 of 40 depending on signal strength, and at least one is a STRICT loss rather than a tie, so the defect is real rather than theoretical - the test requires a strict loss for exactly that reason. (b) THE NULL TREATED OVERLAPPING RETURNS AS INDEPENDENT: autolearn reads DAILY snapshot dates and computes fwd_ret over 21 trading days, so consecutive dates share 20 of 21 days of window, yet _std_null divided by the raw date count - understating the standard error by roughly sqrt(21) and turning a nominal 1.64 sigma bar into about 0.36 sigma. Fixed as an overlap_periods PARAMETER, not a constant, because overlap is a property of the CALLER and a blanket sqrt(21) would over-penalise a genuinely non-overlapping panel; the default of 1 reproduces the previous arithmetic to 1e-12, pinned, so nothing silently re-calibrated. autolearn passes its real overlap, ESTIMATED from the dates actually present via median spacing (median, so one dead-scheduler gap cannot make the panel look independent) rather than assumed - daily dates read about 21, weekly about 4. THE KNOWN-BAD FIXTURE THE GUARD NEVER HAD IS NOW WRITTEN: the audit noted both existing tests feed i.i.d. panels, the one world in which _std_null is correct, so the guard had a clean fixture and no dirty one (M3's whole point). The new fixture builds genuinely OVERLAPPING windows with no cross-sectional signal at all, so every accept is a false positive, and asserts the corrected floor is strictly higher and the corrected accept count never exceeds the naive one. REPORTED, NOT REPAIRED, with reasons: (c) the ratio test oos_ic >= 0.5 * is_ic is loosest when the signal is weakest, and (d) the panel is built from the top 60 names BY THE COMPOSITE THE CURRENT WEIGHTS PRODUCE, which is range restriction on a variable derived from the thing being estimated. Both are design changes that need their own register, and neither can ship a weight now in any case. THE AUDIT'S OWN KILL CONDITION IS HONOURED: it says that if the corrected floor changes no historical adopt decision and learned_config is empty, this is documentation-only. The table is empty in every database this lane can reach and the production table could not be checked, so (a) and (b) are recorded as correctness repairs to an arithmetic that was wrong on its own terms, NOT as changing any decision - no historical adopt decision is known to move, because none is known to exist. Zero trials. Every leg of accepted now ships in the payload (beats_equal_weight, significance_floor, oos_dates, overlap_periods, effective_oos_dates) so the verdict can be re-derived rather than trusted. | MA4 | MA | append_row rewrites the contract-bound history non-atomically and drops unknown columns | **DONE** | FIXED 2026-08-15 - the contract-bound history is now written to a temp file and renamed over the original, and the header is the UNION of what is on disk with ROW_COLUMNS instead of a projection onto it. Both pins are demonstrated NON-VACUOUS against the superseded write, kept as a fixture | 21bdf3c | VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT.md MA4 | 2026-08-15 | audit3-ingest | OUT-OF-BAND (audit #3 merged record, not one of the 134). HIGH / mandate 2 - code. Evidence at valuation/screener/index_mark.py:304-344. Fix is two lines: os.replace from a .tmp, and union the existing header with ROW_COLUMNS instead of projecting onto it. The rest of the module is exemplary; the tz-mixing concern in _closes was checked and is correct for US listings.  FIXED 2026-08-15 (21bdf3c). BOTH DEFECTS CONFIRMED IN SOURCE BEFORE BEING FIXED, and they are about EVERY row in the file rather than the row being added, because appending one row rewrote the whole file. (1) `open(path, 'w')` truncated first, so an interruption between truncate and flush left the bound series empty or partial - on the file `track-backup.yml` calls the one thing that cannot be re-derived, whose only other copies are a WEEKLY backup cron and one laptop. Now written to `path + '.tmp'`, fsynced, and `os.replace`d over the original, so a failed write leaves the PREVIOUS file intact - which is also why no separate pre-write copy is taken: the original IS the copy until the rename succeeds. (2) every historical row was re-projected onto `ROW_COLUMNS`, so the first append after the file gained a column would have deleted that column from every row at once, silently. The header is now the union.  NEITHER PIN IS TAKEN ON TRUST. The superseded write is kept verbatim as a test fixture and run against the same inputs: it drops the added column from all three rows, and interrupted the same way it leaves the file SHORTER than it found it. A regression test that cannot be shown to fail against the defect it names is worth nothing.  THREE THINGS THE AUDIT DID NOT ASK FOR, EACH BECAUSE THE FIX EXPOSED THE QUESTION. A RAGGED file is now REFUSED rather than normalised - `csv.DictReader` pools surplus cells under one key and pads short rows, so a rewrite would invent or discard cells in silence; refusing leaves a recording gap, which `track_meter.recording_history` can see, while normalising loses data nothing can recover. A key on the incoming row that is in neither the file nor `ROW_COLUMNS` is still not written - widening the bound schema should take a deliberate edit, not a caller's typo - but it now comes back in `ignored_fields` instead of vanishing. And the guard was checked against the REAL file before shipping, because a guard that refuses the live series is worse than the defect it replaces: the tracked backup parses clean, zero ragged rows.  A PATH CORRECTION AGAINST THE RECORD, MINOR: the shipped `data_export` backup of the live series carries no `day_n` column while `ROW_COLUMNS` says it is 'carried because the existing rows carry it'. The local copy DOES carry it, so the claim is true of one copy and not the other. The union direction only ever ADDS, so this is inert either way. `tests/test_index_mark.py` 31/31. |
| MA5 | MA | Two Harvey-Liu-Zhu bars exist and already disagree (3.0 constant vs sqrt(2 ln N) = 3.2899) | **DONE** | FIXED - one derived definition, `statistics.hlz_hurdle`; NO published comparison moves | 11cfda6 | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md MA5+MA6 | 2026-08-15 | human | VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT.md MA5, MEDIUM, edge lane. STATUS CORRECTION: this row read IN PROGRESS (app fixer) - it was never in flight, see the id-collision note in section 2.2 and HANDOFF_appfixes.md 33. THE AUDIT SAID TWO BARS; MEASURED, THE SHIPPED PACKAGE CARRIED FOUR: statistics.hlz_significant (the CONSTANT 3.0), fundamental_panel._trials_haircut, the inline hurdle in its multiple_testing block, and ablation.py's own copy - and only _trials_haircut saw M1's floor. THE CONSTANT IS NOT A DIFFERENT BAR, IT IS sqrt(2 ln N) FROZEN AT N = 90, so the two crossed on 2026-08-06 when equity N went 84 -> 104 and have diverged ever since; at N = 224 the honest bar is 3.2899, and because the hurdle only RISES with trials a frozen constant can only ever be too EASY. ONE DEFINITION: statistics.hlz_hurdle(n_trials); every shipped site delegates and a test fails if a second sqrt(2 ln N) appears anywhere in valuation/. hlz_significant now REQUIRES n_trials with NO DEFAULT - a default is precisely how it froze, and defaulting to the live log would make a pure-arithmetic primitive read a file from disk. WHICH PUBLISHED COMPARISONS MOVE: NONE, and this was checked rather than asserted. The headline long-short HAC t 2.6199 fails both bars; X2's seven-grid claim CLEARS 3.0 on three of the seven and still clears on exactly three at 2.9768 (N=84), 3.0478 (N=104), 3.0834 (N=116) and 3.2899 (N=224), because every candidate hurdle falls in the empty gap between the 4th and 5th grids (2.926 and 3.374) - it first becomes four-of-seven-fail at equity N > 296.5, about 70 trials out. test_edge.py's own 3.4 example flips at N > 323.8. THE REFACTOR IS BIT-IDENTICAL, not merely equivalent: max abs delta 0.000e+00 over 2,010 values, and _trials_haircut(8) still returns exactly 3.2898772171176964, the literal MA13 pins. A NEAR-MISS THE SWEEP CAUGHT AND THE AUDIT DID NOT NAME: param_search.py:435 computes Hansen's SPA recentring A = (omega/sqrt(T)) * sqrt(2 ln ln T) - the law of the ITERATED logarithm over SAMPLE LENGTH, not a trial count. Folding it into hlz_hurdle would have silently changed the SPA test; it is excluded by STRUCTURE (its log argument is itself a log), not by filename, so a real HLZ copy in that same file still fails. CITATIONS CORRECTED IN PLACE: statistics.py module docstring, fundamental_panel's "lead with the hurdle of 3.0" guidance (now doubly wrong - the 3.52 it cites is the void pre-B6 panel and the live 2.6199 FAILS), RESEARCH_LOG.md's opening prose, DATA_AND_METHODS.md's "HLZ t>3 gate", CLAUDE.md's B9 and X2 bullets, tests/test_edge.py. Dated per-session HANDOFF entries are left as the historical record. 4 fixtures, all 4 failing against the pre-fix tree. Zero trials - a correctness repair with no hypothesis and no verdict. |
| MA6 | MA | The trial counter has one silent path that understates N, and no counter for it | **DONE** | FIXED - path closed, counter added; N DOES NOT MOVE (equity stays 224) | 11cfda6 | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md MA5+MA6 | 2026-08-15 | human | VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT.md MA6, MEDIUM, edge lane. STATUS CORRECTION: this row read IN PROGRESS (app fixer) - never in flight, same id collision as MA5. THE DEFECT: `by_domain[dom] += k` ran only when a domain resolved, so a row whose domain cell is not exactly one of DOMAINS (a typo, a blank, an unregistered name) was added to `trials` and to NO bucket - and `trial_count(domain=...)` reads the bucket. IT WAS THE ONLY DEGRADATION IN THIS PARSER ROUTED TOWARD A SMALLER N, and understating N OVERSTATES the significance of every DSR- and HLZ-gated claim: M1's own stated error, inside M1's own parser, for the second time after session 12's whole-row FIXED grep. CLOSED BY CHARGING, NOT BY GUESSING: an unresolved row cannot be attributed, so `trial_count` charges it to EVERY family - overstating N understates significance, which is the safe direction and the one this module's own opening argument fixes. `by_domain` deliberately keeps meaning "rows that RESOLVED", so `sum(by_domain) + unresolved == trials` stays an invariant a reader can check by hand (shipped as a boolean) AND MA13's committed-literal stamp still pins the same quantity it always pinned - no stamp edit was needed. THE COUNTER: `rows_domain_unresolved` names the rows and `trials_domain_unresolved` counts them, alongside the existing rows_malformed / rows_changed_by_parser_fix. DOES N MOVE? NO - measured, 0 unresolved rows on the log as it stands, equity N stays 224, options 292, infra 15, unified 0, so the DSR bar and the HLZ hurdle are unchanged and NO published claim needs re-checking. The defect is LATENT, exactly as the audit measured it; the fix changes what the NEXT typo'd domain cell will cost. THE AUDIT'S SECOND HAZARD IS ALSO CLOSED, REPORTED-ONLY: both tables in RESEARCH_LOG.md are NINE columns wide with different orders, so `rows_malformed`'s width guard cannot see a row appended under the wrong header. `rows_misfiled_table` detects it by the one zero-false-positive rule available - a verdict cell of the exact form n=<k> cannot be a verdict - and reads EMPTY on the real log. THE REVERSE DIRECTION IS DELIBERATELY NOT DETECTED and is named rather than papered over: catching it needs a vocabulary of verdict words, which is a second definition of "verdict" that would cry wolf the first time someone wrote a new one. ALSO FIXED: `rows(path, use_cache)` accepted the flag and called `_parse` unconditionally - the parameter was a lie; it is honoured now, and both caches are keyed on (path, mtime, size) so a file that CHANGES on disk re-parses instead of serving a stale count. 7 fixtures, all 7 failing against the pre-fix tree. Zero trials - FIXED-class. |
| MA7 | MA | Two public endpoints spend the owner's vendor quota with no cap, and one of them does it 25 names at a time | **DONE** | SHIPPED 2026-08-14 - three endpoints capped, budget denominated in name-valuations | 983e6ee | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 34 | 2026-08-15 | human | VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT_ULTIMATE.md MA7, MEDIUM. THE DEFECT: ratelimit capped /api/scan/run at 3/hour because 'FMP quota, 3 requests per uncached name' and deliberately left /api/value UNLIMITED unless run_ai was set, because 'the plain valuation is the product's core action'. That comment was HALF RIGHT - the AI layer is a paid call, but the plain valuation runs the full adaptive DCF on a CALLER-SUPPLIED symbol, so it reaches the same upstream and spends the same FMP quota the 22:23 UTC scan depends on. The result cache defends against REPEATS; nothing defended against ENUMERATION, and the universe is ~7,100 names. /api/rank was the sharper case and was in no bucket AT ALL: up to 25 value_ticker calls per request at 2,000 Monte Carlo trials each, on a 512 MB box - a 25x amplification of /api/value's cost. THE SWEEP FOUND A THIRD THE AUDIT DID NOT NAME: /api/dip is PUBLIC (surfaces PUBLIC_API), in no bucket, and fans out through the same _get_or_compute for up to MAX_SHORTLIST names - with the fan-out taken from a CALLER-SUPPLIED query parameter, which is the same caller-controlled-cost property that made /api/rank the sharp case. Found by walking app.py's routes for calls that reach value_ticker rather than by re-reading the audit's list, and that sweep now ships as a test so the next such route is caught on arrival. THE BUDGET IS DENOMINATED IN NAME-VALUATIONS, NOT REQUESTS, and that is the one design decision worth arguing with. The requests differ in cost by up to 25x, so a per-REQUEST cap has to be set for the worst case and is then absurdly tight for the common one. Charging the actual scarce unit means the audit's own 120/hour buys either 120 single valuations or ~5 full 25-name ranks or any mix - its number, charged correctly - and /api/rank's cap comes out at the 1/25th the audit asked for without a second constant to keep in step. check() gained a cost parameter DEFAULTING TO 1, so every per-request bucket behaves exactly as before (pinned); the guard moved from len(stamps) >= limit to len(stamps) + cost > limit, which is bit-identical at cost 1. TWO PROPERTIES THAT ARE DELIBERATE AND WOULD BE EASY TO GET WRONG. A request that cannot AFFORD its cost is refused WHOLE and consumes nothing - charging 12 of a 25-name request and then running all 25 would be a limiter reporting a number it did not enforce. And buckets_for returns SEVERAL (bucket, cost) pairs rather than one, because a request can be scarce in two ways at once: an /api/value with run_ai spends the FMP quota AND an Anthropic call, and the old single-bucket form could only charge one of them - it charged the AI and let the vendor spend through, which is exactly the hole. The AI cap is UNCHANGED at 20/hour and is now charged ALONGSIDE the vendor budget rather than instead of it. WORST-CASE CHARGING IS DISCLOSED, NOT HIDDEN: the limiter runs in before_request, so it cannot know which names will be cache hits, and it charges the full fan-out either way. That over-charges a warm cache. It is the only direction available there - the alternative is to charge nothing until the money is already spent - and it errs tight, which is right for a spend limiter. An uncomputable fan-out is charged the CEILING, not a free pass, so the limiter cannot open under its own errors. READS ARE UNTOUCHED and pinned so: /api/hotstocks, /api/health, /api/signals, /api/valquo-index, /api/track and /api/index-track serve the cached snapshot and stay free. Open access is a product decision, not a bug, and a fix that capped reading would be a worse outcome than the defect. ONE TEST WAS INVERTED, NOT WEAKENED: test_security.py asserted bucket_for('/api/value', {...}) is None under the comment '/api/value is the core action and stays free' - the defect, pinned, in the suite meant to catch it. It now asserts the opposite and additionally that an AI request is charged for BOTH things it spends, which the old single-bucket assertion could not express. tests/test_vendor_quota.py 15/15, including a test that pins RANK_MAX against the literal [:25] slice in api_rank's own source so the charge cannot drift from the doing, and the route sweep above. Full gate 80/80 exit 0 (80 not 76 - merging main mid-session brought in four more suites; measured then corrected); mutations 14/14 caught, 0 missed, 0 skipped. Zero trials - a correctness/security change with no hypothesis and no threshold. REPORTED NOT FIXED: MA8's client_ip caveat is untouched and bounds every per-IP cap here - a caller who can rotate IPs faster than the window evades all of them, which is a property of per-IP limiting rather than of this change. THE DEFECT IS NOT A WRONG LINE, IT IS AN UNWRITTEN NUMBER: client_ip took the RIGHTMOST X-Forwarded-For entry, correct for exactly ONE trusted proxy, and that 'one' appeared nowhere - it was implied by `parts[-1]`. The audit's point is that the code cannot tell which world it is in, and the two failure modes are opposite and both SILENT. TOO LOW (configured 1, actually 2 - a CDN in front of Render): the entry taken is the inner proxy's view of the OUTER one, a single shared address, so EVERY visitor lands in one bucket and the per-IP limiter silently becomes a GLOBAL cap one scraper exhausts for everybody - and from the inside that is indistinguishable from the limiter working. TOO HIGH: the entry taken is the client's own claim, trivially spoofable, so the limiter is bypassed by rotating a header. SHIPPED: TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS (=1, env-overridable, because the day this becomes wrong is a deploy-time infrastructure change made by someone who should not have to edit Python), and ratelimit.forwarded_shape() + GET /admin/proxy-shape, modelled on MA1's read-only /admin/learned-weight-status. The audit's own prescribed verification was 'one deploy, one grep'; the chain LENGTH is the whole question and is observable on every request already being served, so this answers it WITHOUT the deploy. THE DEFAULT IS BIT-IDENTICAL TO THE BEHAVIOUR IT REPLACED, pinned across seven header shapes including empty, whitespace and doubled commas - otherwise this is a behaviour change wearing a diagnostic's clothes. A chain SHORTER than configured falls back to remote_addr (the one address that cannot be forged off-box) and never to the leftmost entry, which would be reading the client's claim - the exact failure the rightmost rule exists to avoid. NOT VACUOUSLY GREEN: the report returns 'insufficient' below 20 observations rather than a confident 'consistent' off three requests. It stores COUNTS ONLY - no address is retained - pinned by a test that greps the payload for the octets it was fed, and depths are bucketed at 10 so a pathological header cannot grow the dict. WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: it does not decide which world Render is in. That still needs one real request from production - but now it needs a request, not a deploy. Zero trials. |

The audit's claim reproduces exactly: `on: push branches ['worktree-*']`, `permissions: contents: write`, and the gate runs `python "$f"` over `tests/test_*.py` FROM THE MERGED TREE, so the branch supplies the code that judges the branch. Worse than the audit states: `actions/checkout` persists that write token into .git/config, so a file named tests/test_zz.py could have run `git push --force origin main` and skipped the gate entirely. THE FIX IS A PERMISSIONS SPLIT, because that is the only control here GitHub ENFORCES rather than merely encourages - job `gate` (contents: READ) runs every line of branch code, job `land` (contents: write) merges and pushes and runs none. A stolen token now buys read access to a repo the branch is already in. PLUS `.github/land_policy.py`, COPIED OUT OF MAIN'S CHECKOUT BEFORE THE MERGE, so a branch cannot switch it off by editing its own copy; it refuses any `.github/` change and any test-suite DELETION. IT LIVES UNDER .github/ DELIBERATELY, so weakening it trips its own rule - pinned by test, because moving it to scripts/ would silently reopen the hole. THE AUDIT'S OTHER SUGGESTION IS DECLINED WITH A REASON: running main's copy of tests/ against branch source would red-X every legitimate change that updates a test alongside the behaviour it covers, so the property was obtained by refusing DELETIONS instead, which has no false-positive class. RESIDUAL, STATED NOT PAPERED OVER: for push events GitHub runs the workflow YAML from the PUSHED branch, so a branch that rewrites land-agent-branch.yml escapes the policy; nothing in the repo can prevent that and the control is a GitHub-side ruleset protecting .github/**, which is DON'S to apply. This closes accidents and drift, not a determined agent. ONE BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, DECLARED: the old in-job retry re-ran the whole gate when main moved under it, which is 'run branch code again in the job holding the write token'. `land` still re-merges and pushes when main moved with DOCUMENTATION only, and now FAILS with 'push again' when main moved with CODE - the same outcome the old loop reached after 3 attempts, arrived at sooner, and it can never push a combination no gate tested. VERIFIED AGAINST THIS BRANCH'S OWN DIFF: the policy returns exit 2 and names all three .github/ paths including itself. LANDED IN TWO PUSHES ON PURPOSE - the workflow restructure first, the policy second - because landing both at once would have armed a lock against the very file that might need fixing if the restructure were wrong. 20 tests in tests/test_land_policy.py.
