Copy risk runs the opposite way from th  BUILT 2026-08-15 as `valuation/web/refusals.py`, wired additively into the hot list payload and RENDERED in `static/app.js`; `tests/test_refusals.py` 28/28; mutations 16 caught, 0 missed, 0 skipped.  THE PREMISE HOLDS AND ONE POINTER IS CORRECTED: the band and the `fair_value_withheld` flag are in `withhold.py` as the audit says, but `record_refusal` is in `engine/publication.py`, not there.  FOUR THINGS THE AUDIT'S OWN PROPOSED SENTENCE GETS WRONG, EACH VERIFIED BEFORE THE BUILD. (1) IT CONFLATES TWO KINDS OF SILENCE THAT `publication.py` EXISTS TO SEPARATE - `refused` is a stable statement about the valuation and `unavailable` is a TEMPORARY one about the feed, and that module's own comment says collapsing them is how "we could not look" gets read as "we looked and refused". Not hypothetical: `record_unavailable` was adopted on measured evidence of ~5% of served rows and the live 2026-08-14 scan read ZERO, so the two move independently and a bad feed day is when a collapsed count misleads most. (2) THE CAUSE CLAUSE IS FALSE FOR SOME REFUSALS - `decide()` refuses on the band OR on an unresolved currency mismatch, and the currency branch carries `ratio = None`, measured rather than assumed, so there is no multiple to quote; the shipped copy names the band as the USUAL cause and a mutation removing that hedge is caught. (3) THE DENOMINATOR IS NOT "NAMES IT SCORED" - only the first `refusal_screen` names are ASKED, production runs 500, and the live scan scored 795 while asking 500, so "N of 795" understates the rate by 1.59x. (4) "TODAY" IS WRONG ON A STALE SCAN, so the copy names the scan's own date and never says today.  THE FINDING THE AUDIT DOES NOT CONTAIN: "WITHHELD" HAS THREE MEANINGS, NOT TWO. The serve-time band withhold sets the flag and a reason but NO `kind`, so it is neither `refused` nor `unavailable` and is a statement about the PEER ESTIMATOR rather than the model's own DCF - and its count was COMPUTED AND THROWN AWAY, the return value of `withhold_implausible_fair_values` being discarded at the call site, the same shape MA39 found in the results writer. Now captured and reported separately with its own denominator, because it is tier-dependent.  A DEFECT IN MY OWN FIRST CUT, and it is the same conflation this row argues against: I fed that return value to the peer-estimate sentence, but the function increments for rows ALREADY marked withheld at scan time, so it is the TOTAL in the slice - on the live scan the total is 2 and BOTH are model refusals, so the sentence would have called the model's own refusals peer-estimate withholds. The third state is now derived from the ABSENT `kind` at the call site, both figures are reported under separate names, and three mutations plus a dedicated test pin the distinction.  ONE NUMBER ONE MEANING: every figure is READ from `publication_audit` and nothing is recounted, because a second count is a second definition of "refused" free to drift - the defect `engine/publication.py` was created to end, having found five copies of that one decision.  MEASURED AGAINST PRODUCTION, NOT ONLY FIXTURES: read from the PUBLIC payload on 2026-08-15 for the scan of 2026-08-14 - universe 800, scored 795, asked 500, refused 2, unavailable 0, both withheld served rows of kind `refused`. The rendered sentence is pinned verbatim. HONEST LIMIT: the third state read zero that day so it is fixture-verified only, and no user has yet seen the rendered wording.  FAIL-SOFT: a snapshot with no `health` block reports `available: false` and NO sentence rather than "0 refused", because a missing count and a zero count are different statements.  A DEFECT IN MY OWN TEST: the zero-trials sweep grepped for "threshold" and failed on the module's own docstring saying it "clears no threshold" - prose asserting ABSENCE read as presence. Fixed by reducing to CODE ONLY via `ast.unparse` with docstring nodes stripped rather than by adding a file exemption, which is what stops a sweep finding the next real case, and the reduction is itself checked for vacuity. Zero trials: it measures no hypothesis and clears no threshold, pinned by test. |

THREE DESIGN DECISIONS WORTH THE ROW. A scan that never recorded its size is SKIPPED as unknown rather than counted as a miss - a missing denominator must not read as everybody qualifying. And an unreachable store leaves the rows untouched and reports `available: false`, because defaulting to 1 would caption every name on the list as new today, which is a confident wrong caption rather than a missing one. WHAT IS STILL TRUE, so this is a scoping memo and not a rejection: (a) it is FEASIBLE on owned data - bulk.prepare_daily already down-samples DAILY to ONE ROW PER TICKER-MONTH, so monthly is the NATIVE granularity of the point-in-time market-cap path, and fundamentals are as-of-any-date by construction; the binding cost is compute, ~3x the 69-date build's ~20 minutes on every build thereafter. (b) **EVERY CALIBRATED BAR WOULD BECOME AN EXTRAPOLATION** - X7's floors are calibrated for THIS panel, universe and 69 dates, so 2.2837, 2.7072, 1.8629pp and 19.667% may not be quoted against a monthly panel; it needs its own placebo sweep first and that cost is not in the audit's estimate. (c) S8/S9's prepare_daily staleness defect is inherited and WORSENED - the point-in-time market cap can be up to 31 days stale while the price is same-day, which on a quarterly panel is a precision defect and on a monthly one is a third of the rebalance interval. RECOMMENDATION: not a reason to refuse the rebuild and not a reason to pay for it today - justify it by whatever the NEXT text-or-frequency item actually needs, measured in advance, rather than by S19. **A DEFECT IN THE AUDIT'S OWN CROSS-REFERENCE: row 8 of its section 10 table says 'the unlock is MA24's monthly rebuild' while its own id map eleven lines above assigns the monthly rebuild to MA33 and MA24 to 'Wrong rejections'.** DESIGN_ma_final_batch.md |

THE PREMISE IS CONFIRMED EXACTLY: known_frac had ONE producer and ZERO readers repo-wide, and the consumer divides a whole-chain volume sum by a known-OI-only sum. THE DECIDING FACT IS ONE NEITHER THE AUDIT NOR THE ROW STATES - whether missing OI is PARTIAL within a chain-day or ALL-OR-NOTHING. All-or-nothing would make coi exactly 0 and the shipped `coi > 0` guard would already block the bonus, i.e. the defect would be INERT. Measured over 41,321 front-expiry chain-days on 41 cached symbols: 75.2% full, 0.09% none, 24.87% PARTIAL - so it is live. BLAST RADIUS IS SMALL AND ONE-DIRECTIONAL: 27 days (0.065%) cross the 0.5 bar for no reason but the mismatch, and ZERO cross the other way, so the defect could only ever have ADDED an alert, never hidden one. BOTH OF THE AUDIT'S PROPOSED FIXES ARE WORSE THAN THE DEFECT: scaling coi by 1/coverage kills 501 otherwise-legitimate fires (18.6x) and suppressing below 0.9 coverage kills 1,005 (37.2x). MECHANISM, measured independently: volume is CONCENTRATED in the known-OI rows (median +0.50 excess share), so imputing average OI onto rows carrying far below-average volume is not a neutral correction. SHIPPED INSTEAD: take both sums over the SAME rows (new call_volume_oi_known / put_volume_oi_known), which imputes nothing and introduces no constant. LIVE IS BIT-IDENTICAL - Tradier ships no coverage figure, so the consumer falls back to the old numerator, pinned by test. The banked 22b/R2 books are NOT re-run and were built under the defect; the one-directional finding bounds what that can mean. scripts/ma38_coverage.py, data/free_analysis/MA38_OI_COVERAGE.json, tests/test_ma38_oi_coverage.py 9/9, 4/4 mutations caught. |

WHEN IT BITES IT IS SEVERE AND ONE-DIRECTIONAL: front IV moves a median +0.1262 (12.6 vol points) against a shipped term threshold of 0.0105 - twelve times the bar - the 0.0105 decision flips on 0.29% of chain-days, and 5 of 6 flips are alerts that PASS today and would fail. THE AUDIT'S DIRECTION CLAIM IS CONFIRMED: unfiltered reads bias term_slope positive, i.e. toward passing what it should suppress. SHIPPED: one shared blackscholes.usable_quote, deliberately EXACTLY the greeks rule (no_quote + crossed) and nothing more - penny, wide_spread, dte_band and mny_band are SELECTION criteria and folding them in would make a validity test change results it was never meant to touch. THE ROW IS KEPT AND ITS iv/greeks GO NaN rather than the row being dropped, so no caller's row count or index moves and every existing dropna path just works. pick_contract selection is BIT-IDENTICAL and pinned - options_fill.quote_reject_reason already refused these rows AFTER enrichment, so the 22.1% of the 45-75 DTE band that is one-sided was being enriched and discarded, wasted compute rather than a wrong answer. tests/test_ma45_quote_validity.py 6/6, 3 of 6 fail against pre-fix sources - including the pre-fix enrich_chain returning iv 0.222 from a zero-bid row. | MA46 | MA | Two options recorders define pnl_pct differently post-B15: forward track gross of commission, backtest net | **DONE** | CONFIRMED and FIXED by RECORD-BOTH, after the obvious fix (netting the headline) was found to collide with MA36's exactly-minus-100% settlement convention | 0d527aa | HANDOFF_optionsbot.md 60 | 2026-08-15 | audit3-ingest, closed by optionsbot | OUT-OF-BAND (audit #3 merged record, not one of the 134). MEDIUM / mandate 2 - code. CLOSED 2026-08-15 by the optionsbot lane, wave 2. CONFIRMED EXACTLY, AND MY FIRST FIX WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING. B15 made the backtest return_pct NET of commission and kept the old quantity beside it as return_pct_gross_comm; the forward tracker went on computing ex / entry - 1, which IS that renamed quantity, so the live book was scored against a reference computed a different way on the one axis it exists to measure. THE FIRST CUT TOOK THE AUDIT'S FIRST OPTION - net the tracker's pnl_pct - AND IT COLLIDES WITH MA36: a position that expires worthless settles at zero and must read EXACTLY -100%, which is MA36's whole point and its control test asserts it, while netting makes it -100.26%. The deeper reason it is wrong is that AN EXPIRING OPTION IS NEVER SOLD, so there is no second commission leg to charge at all. One correction would have quietly corrupted another. IT WAS CAUGHT BY THE SUITE, NOT BY ME - four suites went red (test_edge, test_intraday, test_ma36_ma37_record_integrity, test_paper_track), and the temptation is to read four red suites as four stale expectations and edit them; they were not stale, they were right. SHIPPED INSTEAD: the audit's SECOND option, RECORD BOTH. pnl_pct keeps exactly the meaning it has always had, expectancy_pct_net is reported beside expectancy_pct, and pnl_basis names which is which. NO PUBLISHED FIGURE MOVES - proved by those same four suites going green again WITHOUT ANY EDIT to their expectations, which is the strongest available evidence that this is a labelling change and not a restatement. The ambiguity WAS the defect; two names close it. options_fill.net_return_pct is the one shared definition and round_trip DELEGATES to it rather than keeping a second copy that agrees today; the contract count cancels out of the formula, so the net series is complete over the whole book from day one, every pre-MA46 row reconstructed exactly from stored premiums with no migration and nothing assumed about size (algebraically exact; one unit in the last place in IEEE754, stated in the test rather than hidden behind a round). REPORTED NOT FIXED, outside this lane: net_return_pct charges TWO commission legs to match options_fill.round_trip, so for a worthless expiry BOTH modules overstate the cost by one leg - consistent between them, on the banked books, and correcting it would move published figures. cum_pnl_dollars also stays gross, because the count cancels out of the percentage but not the dollars and no contract count is stored. tests/test_ma46_pnl_definitions.py 11/11, 10 of 11 fail against pre-fix sources. |

Now DERIVED from the scheme list, not replaced by a second literal. **(d)** `factor_alpha:387` built `ex_b6_first_37` positionally regardless of `--corrected-panel` - and the sibling q-model block 56 lines earlier ALREADY branches on that flag with a comment explaining why the cut is meaningless on the corrected panel, so one of two siblings honoured the argument. **(e)** `param_search._cap_mask` returned `np.ones(...)` when a tier had <30 finite caps - the FULL universe, wider even than the finite-cap names - so a date labelled by tier silently contributed every name; B12's defect (a count read as an identity) in a different column, failing in the direction that looks like more data. Now contributes NOTHING, which `rank_dates`'s existing `len(ids) < 25` guard already handles, so the fix does not trade a silent wrong answer for a crash. **13 of 15 fixtures fail against the pre-fix tree**; the two that pass are the NaN-free inertness proof and the pre-existing consumer guard, both reported as passing deliberately. | MA50 | MA | /api/hotstocks?top=-1 defeats the per-tier row cap via a negative SQL LIMIT | **DONE** | SHIPPED 2026-08-14 - clamped at six sites behind one shared helper | ac90d47 | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 33 | 2026-08-15 | human | VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT_ULTIMATE.md MA50, HIGH, live paywall bypass. THE DEFECT: web/app.py read top = min(int(request.args.get('top', 100)), cap), which bounds from ABOVE only - min(-1, 500) is -1 - and store.load_snapshot interpolates it into q += f' LIMIT {int(top)}', which SQLite treats as UNLIMITED. The per-tier cap (g.hotstocks_cap, free 10 / premium 500) IS the paywall, so ?top=-1 returned the entire ~594-row snapshot. Masked in production only because OPEN_ACCESS=true makes everyone premium; the moment that flag flips it is a live tier bypass. FIXED WITH ONE SHARED HELPER, valuation/web/query_params.clamp_int, NOT SIX HAND-ROLLED COPIES - the same one-sided clamp had been written independently at six sites in two blueprints, and fixing the reported one while leaving five is how this comes back (audit B7's defect class: three composite functions, one repair). The arithmetic is the audit's OWN prescribed remedy, min(max(1, int(...)), cap), pinned against a reference implementation so a later tidy-up cannot change the semantics; the single declared addition is a parse guard, because the registered fix still raises on ?top=abc and an unhandled ValueError is a 500 on a public endpoint. Garbage degrades to the DEFAULT and is then capped, never toward the cap - a limiter whose failure mode is 'serve everything' is the failure being fixed. Sites: /api/hotstocks (the reported one), /api/signals, the ticker search, the scream-buy top, the dip shortlist, /api/option-alerts/open and the backtest universe limit, that last being the widest of them since an unclamped int became a universe size on a 512 MB box. TESTS: 6 of the 18 in tests/test_row_caps_and_admin_split.py. The route tests record the value that reaches the STORE rather than counting rows in a response, deliberately: the defect is the number handed to SQL, and a row-counting test passes on the empty database a fresh CI checkout actually has. A tier-cap test asserts the cap MOVES with the tier, which catches a fix that hard-codes a number and silently unprices premium. A source sweep fails if any bare int(request.args...) reappears anywhere in web/ or saas/ - and its first draft matched the tail of clamp_int( itself and reported the repair as the defect, fixed with a lookbehind. Zero trials. MERGED 2026-08-14 with the generated audit-ingest row for this id: the title above is the audit's own wording and the status, verdict and this note are this lane's measured result. One row per id, not two. | MA51 | MA | Open redirect on /login?next=<absolute URL> | **DONE** | SHIPPED 2026-08-15 - one shared validator, plus an AST sweep for the next one |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 35 | 2026-08-15 | human | VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT_ULTIMATE.md MA51, MEDIUM. VERIFIED BEFORE FIXING: valuation/saas/auth.py:99 read `return redirect(request.args.get("next") or "/app")` with no same-origin check, exactly as the audit states. THE BLAST RADIUS WAS ALSO VERIFIED RATHER THAN TAKEN ON TRUST: `redirect(` appears 23 times under valuation/ and auth.py:99 is the ONLY one whose argument comes from the request - the other four `next` values (app_saas.py x3, billing.py x1) are server-written literals like "/login?next=/account". So the audit's 'only login honours arbitrary next' holds and the fix is one site. WHY IT MATTERS AT THIS PARTICULAR SITE: an open redirect leaks nothing by itself; it is a phishing primitive that borrows the domain's credibility, and it is most convincing exactly where this one fires - immediately after a SUCCESSFUL login on the genuine site, when the user has just proved the site is real. THE FIX IS A MODULE, NOT AN `if`, and that is the one design decision worth arguing with. One call site argues for an inline check; the SWEEP argues against it, because a guard living inside a view cannot be asserted over the codebase, so the second such route gets written the same raw way and nothing notices - audit B7's defect class, which this repository has paid for three times. valuation/saas/safe_redirect.py::safe_next_path. TWO REJECTIONS THAT THE AUDIT'S OWN PRESCRIBED RULE WOULD NOT HAVE MADE. (a) `//evil.example` is protocol-relative: it satisfies startswith('/') and browsers resolve it to another ORIGIN - the audit names this one, which is why its rule says 'not //'. (b) `/\evil.example` is the one that cannot be justified from the RFC at all: urlsplit reports an EMPTY netloc, so the value looks same-origin to correct standards-based code, while browsers normalise the backslash into the authority position and navigate away. A validator that trusts the parser here is right about the standard and wrong about the thing performing the navigation. Both are pinned, and the urlsplit premise is asserted in the test rather than assumed, so a future urllib change reports itself. tests/test_redirect_and_proxy.py 35/35 across all three items; full gate 84/84 exit 0; mutations 19/19 caught, 0 missed, 0 skipped. The legitimate-producer test reads the surviving `next` values FROM THE SOURCE rather than listing them, so a new producer is covered automatically instead of going quietly out of date. MUTATION TESTING CHANGED THE SHIPPED CODE TWICE, AND NEITHER WAS VISIBLE FROM A PASSING SUITE. (i) THREE OF MY GUARDS WERE UNREACHABLE. Leading with value[:1] != "/" made the urlsplit branches below it dead: a value starting with / can carry neither a scheme (which must precede any slash) nor a netloc (which needs //), so the mutant deleting them passed everything. Restructured so the textual check covers only what the parser gets wrong and the parser covers the rest. (ii) THE RESTRUCTURE THEN EXPOSED A LIVE HOLE: ///evil.example parses to an EMPTY netloc and path /evil.example, i.e. urlsplit reads it as SAME-ORIGIN, so delegating the authority question to the parser accepts it. The authority position is now refused textually, covering //, /// and backslash alike. (iii) A THIRD MUTANT SURVIVED ON A TEST GAP RATHER THAN DEAD CODE: deleting the scheme check still passed, because https://evil.example parses to an EMPTY path and was already caught by the relative-path branch. Only the SINGLE-slash form https:/evil.example reaches it - path /evil.example, starts with a slash, sails through everything else, and browsers resolve it off-origin. The guard was right; nothing proved it load-bearing. THE TRANSFERABLE PART: a guard no test can distinguish from its own absence is indistinguishable from dead code, and reading cannot tell you which. A VACUITY TRAP IN MY OWN TEST, CAUGHT AND REPORTED: the end-to-end test first registered a user via POST /register and treated 302 as success. `signup_enabled` is False by default and a DISABLED signup ALSO returns 302 (to /app), so the guard passed on a redirect that created nothing and the login then failed for reasons unrelated to MA51. A status code shared by the success and the refusal cannot distinguish them. The user is now created through the store. Zero trials. |
| MA52 | MA | DEMO_DENIED_VENDOR_ROWS is an empty frozenset - the only structural guard against a future vendor-backed read route leaking under the demo/public tier is a human remembering to list it | **DONE** | FIXED 2026-08-15 - the licence question is now a REQUIRED answer, which is LA13's own mechanism applied to the second axis. The deny set stays EMPTY and correctly so: the defect was never the emptiness, it was that an empty deny list and an unconsidered one are indistinguishable from outside | 21bdf3c | VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT_ULTIMATE.md MA52 | 2026-08-15 | audit3-ingest | OUT-OF-BAND (audit #3 merged record, not one of the 134). MEDIUM / mandate 6 - adversarial. Evidence at valuation/screener/surfaces.py:232. Complements Pass A MA9. Fix: populate/enforce it plus a test that fails on an unclassified new /api read route.  FIXED 2026-08-15 (21bdf3c), `tests/test_public.py` 39/39, `tests/test_public_full_view.py` 19/19.  TWO CORRECTIONS AGAINST THE AUDIT, BOTH FOUND BY CHECKING BEFORE FIXING. Its file path is wrong - the constant is in `valuation/saas/surfaces.py`, not `screener/` - and the SECOND HALF OF ITS PROPOSED FIX ALREADY SHIPS: a test that fails when a new read route is unclassified has existed since LA13 on 2026-08-10, walking the app's own URL map through `surfaces.classify`, and it is non-vacuous. The audit asked for something already built.  SO THE DEFECT IS NARROWER AND SHARPER THAN THE ROW SAYS, AND POPULATING THE SET WOULD HAVE BEEN WRONG. The deny set is empty because it SHOULD be empty - no read route echoes a vendor row, checked route by route. What was missing is that an empty deny list and an UNCONSIDERED one are indistinguishable from outside, so the licence boundary rested on somebody remembering the set exists. The fix is LA13's own mechanism on a second axis: `vendor_review()` returns None for a route nobody has answered for, exactly as `classify()` returns None for an unclassified one, and the sweep fails on it. A deny list cannot be forgotten if the suite will not go green until the route is on one side of it.  THE REVIEW SET IS WHAT A PREVIEW CAN READ, AND THE SCOPE IS A JUDGEMENT: 20 routes. Redistribution bites where an un-authenticated or preview reader sees the payload, so an owner route the demo tier is denied and an admin-token route that refuses a tokenless caller are excluded - both already pinned elsewhere, and re-asserting them here would have been a second copy of someone else's policy.  THE CLOSEST CALL IS RECORDED AS A JUDGEMENT RATHER THAN ASSUMED. The module's own docstring names the research prefix as the one place Sharadar-derived output reaches an HTTP route, and the gating layer deliberately grants exactly one path under it to the read tier. Its payload is adopted weights, learning history and two summary blobs - IC statistics, weight vectors, accept and reject flags computed OVER the licensed panel. No ticker-date fundamental, no price, no filing figure. Cleared on that basis, and named as the entry to re-read first if that payload ever widens.  FOUR PINS, including one that the `denied` verdict is actually expressible and does deny - otherwise the set the audit asked to populate would be decorative - and one that a cleared entry naming a route the app no longer registers fails, since a stale clearance reads as reviewed forever and covers nothing. |
| MA53 | MA | Inherited LA12 is STILL LIVE, and two public endpoints 500 on a malformed numeric param | **DONE (both halves VERIFIED CLOSED; one NEW defect found and fixed)** | 2026-08-15 - the audit's two claims are both already closed; the sweep found a third it did not name |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 35 | 2026-08-15 | human | VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT_ULTIMATE.md MA53, LOW. VERIFIED BEFORE FIXING, AND THE VERIFICATION IS THE RESULT - BOTH OF THE AUDIT'S CLAIMS ARE ALREADY CLOSED. (a) '/api/hotstocks and /api/tickers 500 on a malformed numeric param' - MEASURED against a live test client: ?top=abc, ?top=-1 and ?limit=abc all return 200. Closed by MA50's clamp_int, which landed hours before this audit item was read; its parse guard was the one declared addition to the audit's own prescribed arithmetic, and this is what it was for. The audit's line numbers had also drifted (536/550 are now 544/558). (b) 'LA12's median_upside population-mix is unfixed' - the MIX is real (median_upside is computed over only the DCF'd names while `count` reports the whole sector), but the defect is NOT unaddressed: LA12's shipped remedy was DISCLOSURE, not equalising the populations - sectors.py emits `median_upside_n` beside it, with `median_upside_n == 0` exactly when the median is None. The audit looked for the remedy it expected and did not find the one that shipped. NOT changed to estimate fair values over the full population: that is a latency and meaning change on a public endpoint with no consumer today (app.js reads avg_composite), i.e. a product decision, not a bug fix. Pinned instead, both the denominator and the invariant. THE SWEEP FOUND ONE THE AUDIT DID NOT NAME, AND IT IS THE ONLY LIVE DEFECT IN THIS ROW: /api/options-alerts?risk_budget= was parsed with a bare float(). float accepts three strings int rejects - 'nan', 'inf', '-inf' - so that parameter had NO defence at all, not even the accidental one of raising. A NaN budget does not raise, does not log and does not stop: it propagates into position sizing where every comparison against it is False, so the downstream guards read as satisfied. Strictly worse than a 500. Owner-only, so not a public hole; fixed as the same class rather than left because the blast radius is small. A CORRECTION AGAINST MY OWN FIRST DRAFT, AND IT NEARLY SHIPPED: I wrote the clamp as lo=0.0001 hi=1.0, reading risk_budget as a fraction. IT IS DOLLARS - DEFAULT_RISK_BUDGET is $1,000 per signal - so that clamp would have silently re-sized every alert to $1. Caught by checking the unit before trusting the bound; a test now pins the shipped default inside the shipped range. A SECOND CORRECTION, AGAINST MY OWN DOCSTRING: I claimed NaN survives a min/max clamp. MEASURED, it is worse and stranger - the outcome is ORDER-DEPENDENT. max(lo,min(v,hi)) -> lo (garbage becomes a plausible-looking floor); min(max(v,lo),hi) -> nan; max(min(v,hi),lo) -> nan. Two of the three natural spellings pass NaN straight through and none of them is a clamp, while all three look identical in review - the same value-dependent-guard family as the zscore zero-variance check this project has been bitten by twice. THE CLAIM, IN THE EXACT SHAPE THE REGISTER PERMITS: names tripping at least two of three published accounting stress tests lost more than half their value over the following quarter 2.66 percent of the time against 0.87 percent, a ratio of 3.04x, replicated at 3.42x early and 2.93x late. THE RATIO AND BOTH RATES, NEVER THE DIFFERENCE, and that is a measured rule rather than a style preference - the base rate is era-dependent at 0.34 percent early against 1.36 percent late, a four-fold move spanning COVID 2020Q1 and 2022, so the absolute gap swings 0.86pp to 2.39pp while the ratio barely moves. A card quoting a percentage-point gap would be quoting an era average that describes neither half. PINNED TWICE AND IN DIFFERENT WAYS: the module cannot subtract the two rates at all, asserted against the SYNTAX TREE rather than by grep so a subtraction cannot hide behind a helper name and so that a comment ABOUT subtraction does not fire it, with its own positive control; and BANNED carries the forbidden arithmetic in words. ONLY THE FOUR COUNTS PER WINDOW ARE PINNED - every rate and every ratio is derived from them, and a test asserts the derived values reproduce the published artifact's own rate_flagged, rate_kept and ratio at max absolute delta 0.0 on all three windows, while a second test refuses any rate literal in the module's code with docstrings stripped so the prose may still quote 2.66 percent to a reader while the arithmetic cannot. COVERAGE FIRST, per the standing rule and stated on the surface before the result: Beneish computable on 68.6 percent of panel rows, Altman 76.7 percent, external financing 94.5 percent, and 22.0 percent of rows carry fewer than two computable inputs and cannot be flagged at all, sitting in the base-rate group by construction which understates rather than flatters the flag. AND THE NOT-SCORED-IS-NOT-CLEAN RULE TURNS OUT TO HAVE A NUMBER rather than only a principle: from the register's own C7 block the 3,191 rows where NOTHING could be computed crashed at 1.75 percent against 0.87 percent for the rows that were scored and came back unflagged, a ratio of 2.01x. Absence of a flag is not absence of risk and on the thinnest data it ran the WRONG WAY. That ratio is derived and ships inside the sentence. THE PREMISE FINDING, AND IT DECIDED THE SHAPE OF THE CARD: NOT ONE OF THE THREE FLAGS IS COMPUTABLE ON THE LIVE PATH. Measured, not assumed - the required-input names are read out of the SHIPPED formula source scripts/s10_accounting_veto.py by AST and the available names by actually building a live metrics row through providers.company_to_metrics. Seventeen fields required, ONE present (revenue); alias-aware it is five, and the twelve genuinely absent are assets, liabilities, workingcapital, retearn, receivables, assetsc, ppnenet, depamor, sgna, ncfo, ncfcommon and ncfdebt - every balance-sheet and cash-flow-statement line, on every live source, and the broker feed's own BROKER_FIELDS list does not carry them either. THE SINGLE DECISIVE FACT, which makes the gate one checkable thing rather than a seventeen-item list to eyeball: ALL THREE FLAGS NEED TOTAL ASSETS and no live source has it. A SECOND AND INDEPENDENT REASON SURVIVES ANY AMOUNT OF NEW DATA: external financing flags the TOP DECILE WITHIN EACH DATE, so it is a cross-sectional rank and a single name has no flag until the whole cross-section is scored - even complete inputs would require scoring the LIST and never the name. SO THE MODULE DELIBERATELY CONTAINS NO FLAG ARITHMETIC, and that is a decision rather than an omission: writing Beneish and Altman into valuation/web/ would put a SECOND COPY of both formulas in the tree, where s10_accounting_veto.py is the one definition and ma28_riskcard.py imports it rather than retyping it, and that copy could not execute on a single live row - audit B7's duplicate-definition class and the cannot-fire-guard class at once. What ships instead is a CAPABILITY GATE MEASURED PER REQUEST: missing_inputs reads the row it is handed, so names_scored 0 is re-derived from live data on every call rather than frozen in a comment. AND A TRIPWIRE SO IT CANNOT QUIETLY BECOME FALSE: test_the_day_the_inputs_arrive_this_card_owes_a_scorer FAILS the moment names_scored stops being zero, with a message saying what is owed - build the per-name half against the one formula definition, and remember external financing scores a list. Today the card says none of these names is scored on this; that sentence becomes false silently and nothing else in the product would notice. track_meter's not-yet-due-versus-due-and-missing distinction applied to a product surface, paired with a POSITIVE CONTROL - a synthetic row carrying all seventeen inputs must come back scoreable - because without it both the gate test and the tripwire would pass on return False. WHAT THE CARD MAY NOT SAY, four families, asserted against the RENDERED payload on dip_posture's design and V4's lesson that rendering is where copy leaks. FRAUD, because Beneish is an earnings-manipulation index in the literature so this is the family a copy edit reaches for naturally and the one that would put an accusation about a named real company on a public page - a published statistic crossing a published threshold is not evidence anyone did anything wrong and the card says so. RETURNS, because the register gated this on the crash rate and EXPLICITLY NOT on alpha with top_decile_alpha computed nowhere in its arm path, and S10-ACCT REJECTED the same flag as a portfolio screen where S10 had already measured that this book's maximum drawdown is one market-wide quarter, COVID 2020Q1 at trough index 44 of 69, which no name-level flag can move. ADVICE, which this product does not give anywhere. And PREDICTION or PER-NAME PROBABILITY, V3's rule, since a percentage on a page about named companies is most naturally misread as one company's odds. THE GUARD CAUGHT THE MODULE'S OWN FIRST DRAFT: why_the_ratio explained the forbidden form BY QUOTING IT, so the sentence saying what may not be said was the first thing to fire. The guard was not weakened, the copy was rewritten - MA5's shape, where a source sweep fired on its own documentation twice. THE SIZE CONTROL IS ON THE SURFACE because it is the finding and it is a reader's first objection: C4 was registered as the likely killer since Altman Z contains market cap directly, and U7, S10 and V6-B were each decided by exactly that failure mode; flagged names ARE smaller at a median 2.69bn against 5.19bn, and the effect nonetheless STRENGTHENS monotonically from 2.01x in the smallest quintile to 5.17x in the largest, 5 of 5 clearing. The card renders both extremes and the mechanism - large companies almost never halve in a quarter unless their accounts are stressed, in which case they still do - which is the MIRROR IMAGE of V6-B M1's gradient whose standing caveat is that the claim is strongest exactly where the product is not. This one is strongest exactly where the product IS. VERIFIED BY EXECUTION AND NOT BY PARSING, the V6B-RENDER lesson applied on the way in: renderHot was run under a DOM shim against four real payloads - normal renders the full card at 6,819 chars, STATUS withdrawn renders the withdrawal note and NO FIGURES, an absent block renders nothing with the page intact, and a malformed block renders nothing with the page intact. The withdrawal branch matters because dip_posture's rule is that a NULL must be as sayable as a POSITIVE, and here that means a retraction must SAY SO rather than fall silent - a surface that quietly stops updating is how a retracted number goes on being believed. A SECOND DEFECT IN MY OWN WORK, and it is comment-versus-code FOR THE FOURTH TIME in this project's record after MA5's source sweep, MA49(c)'s fixture and last session's boundary test: the paraphrase sweep fired on my own JS comment, whose first line quotes the card's own label, so the test asserting that app.js does not retype served copy failed against a tree that retypes nothing. It now strips line and block comments before reading, with a vacuity check that the strip keeps every read it searches for. AND THE FIX'S FIRST CUT WAS ITSELF WRONG IN A MORE INTERESTING WAY - it used a hand-typed phrase list and "ratio of" fired on a PRE-EXISTING sentence forty lines away about currency mismatch, a false positive on innocent code, and a typed list only ever covers the copy that existed when it was typed. It is now DERIVED: no thirty-character stretch of any served sentence may appear in the renderer's code, long enough that ordinary English overlap cannot trip it and short enough that a paraphrase worth having cannot avoid it, covering sentences added later, with its own positive control. NOT DONE AND NAMED SO IT IS NOT MISTAKEN FOR DONE: the per-name half is not built and the tripwire's job is to demand it when it becomes buildable; the 4-flag rule is NOT closed since the audit's version includes NT late-filing notices which are not buildable from anything this project owns, so the measured rule is 2-of-THREE and NARROWER and the card says two of three with a test pinning it; the card is on the hot list only and not on the Dip Detector or the single-valuation page, which is a scope choice rather than an oversight; and whether a reader reads eight sentences under a hot list is UNMEASURED, a product question and Don's. THE AUDIT'S OWN PRODUCT SENTENCE WAS WRONG and correcting it in silence is how it comes back: VALQUO_MASTER_AUDIT.md line 950 pairs these rates with a MINUS 20 PERCENT threshold and they are the MINUS 50 PERCENT rates, where at minus 20 the real figures are 16.8 percent against 9.0 percent for a ratio of 1.88x. The error runs in the direction that DISCREDITS the card, since a 20 percent quarterly fall is ordinary and a 0.87 percent base rate for it is transparently impossible, so shipping it verbatim would have published a number that refutes itself; the card states the minus 50 percent threshold IN THE SAME SENTENCE AS THE RATES, which is the placement that makes the mis-pairing impossible to repeat. tests/test_accounting_risk.py 36/36 new; 24 mutations, 24 caught, 0 missed, 0 skipped. |

O17's C4 was NULL SOLELY on a retention floor of 0.70 set for a PRODUCT reason, not on the effect: +4.686pp/trade, positive in both halves, clearing its own calibrated null in both - every figure verified against data/free_analysis/O6_O7_O17_EARNINGS.json directly rather than quoted from the audit. The alert product is dead so the constraint applies to nothing. THE BAR WAS DERIVED FIRST RATHER THAN LOWERED, on the TP-BAR precedent - a procedure, not a number argued in prose - because retention is not a defined quantity for a standalone strategy; it was replaced by breadth (B1 absolute against the alert book, B2 the p5 of 200 same-size random entry sets) and concurrency (B3, against O11's own caps of 10 and 50). THE DECISIVE TEST HAD NEVER BEEN RUN AND WAS RUNNABLE ON OWNED DATA: as a FILTER C4's null is a random-REMOVAL null, but as a STRATEGY the comparator must be random ENTRY, because R2 is the standing result that this alert loses to random entry by -5.0640pp; the five-seed split-clean random-entry control books carry every field owns_the_event needs. THE EFFECT IS REAL AND SURVIVES THE ALERT'S DEATH: on 27,350 random-entry trades a call spanning the next announcement earns +10.30% against +5.50%, a +4.79pp gain, positive in BOTH halves (+5.03 / +4.70), paired name-year sign test z +2.054 p 0.040. AND THE ALERT STILL SUBTRACTS VALUE INSIDE THE SUBSET THAT WAS SUPPOSED TO RESCUE IT: alert-spanning +8.42% LOSES to random-spanning +10.30% at paired sign z -4.4726, p 7.7e-06 over 952 cells - R2 reproduced inside the earnings subset - so the strategy is 'buy calls spanning earnings' with NO ALERT IN IT AT ALL. IT IS A MEAN EFFECT AND NOT A MEDIAN EFFECT: DTE-matched at +/-5 days on the same name the mean gain clears a shuffled-label null on both books (+9.10pp vs p95 +4.00pp alert, +2.51pp vs +1.44pp random) while median-vs-median is +0.40pp, spanning -51.41% against -51.81%; the typical trade is a near-total loss either way, which is U1's finding restated. THREE EXACT CONTROLS: A1's spans_mean 0.084199 reproduces O17's kept_mean to the digit, the implied all-book mean 0.03734 reproduces its all_mean exactly, and the pooled random book lands at +8.47% against R2's banked +8.3342%. A DEFECT IN MY OWN BARS, FOUND BY RUNNING THEM, AND IT DECIDES THE REGISTERED VERDICT: two of the three do not measure what they were written to. B1's trades-per-year axis is UNPASSABLE BY CONSTRUCTION for a subset of the book it is drawn from, and B1's and B2's NAME axes re-measure the earnings coverage hole - 29 of 186 names are foreign private issuers with zero earnings dates, so the spanning set is capped at 157 names before any concentration exists and it touches ALL 157, i.e. 100% of eligible names and 118 of 118 months. Only B3 measured its property and it PASSES, refuting the prediction that it would bind: the strategy is LESS concurrent than the alert book, peak 37 against 48 and refused at O11's cap of 10 on 34.1% against 48.7%. The register is left UNEDITED and the diagnosis ships beside it as a post-hoc field feeding no pass/fail flag, pinned by a test asserting all_bars_pass is assigned BEFORE the diagnostic exists. So a result that was NULL only because of a bar set for a product reason is now REJECTED only because of a bar that was broken, which is worth more than either verdict. A SECOND DEFECT, and it had already been reported once as a finding before it was caught: the DTE-matched MEDIAN was first read as -35.8pp and described as the typical spanning trade doing worse than its matched counterpart. It is an ARTIFACT - comparing one draw against the MEAN of a right-skewed set is biased low by construction, and the shuffled null median is -41.9pp against the real -35.8pp, so the real figure runs slightly IN THE ARM'S FAVOUR and the reading was exactly backwards. It is kept in the payload beside its own null with a note never to quote it alone, because deleting it would leave the next reader free to recompute it and reach the same wrong conclusion. GOVERNING CAVEAT: O11 - a book with +3.27%/trade positive expectancy still ended at $37,059 from $50,000 at a concurrency cap of 10 - so NOTHING HERE LICENSES TRADING IT. Options N 294 to 297. ADOPTS NOTHING and RECOMMENDS NOTHING in either direction. Read from the FREEZE; the mutable data/options store was never touched. POPULATION: 12,904 matched call/put pairs, 185 names, 2016-01-19 to 2025-10-15, both legs passing the shared blackscholes.usable_quote rule (MA45, imported not re-typed), 60 to 90 DTE, delta 0.85 to 0.95 with delta COMPUTED by solving implied vol on the PUT leg, which is out of the money and well conditioned where a deep-ITM call's vega is near zero. THE FINDING IS THE PRICE CONVENTION: the frontier's risk-free plus 43 bps is a MID-PRICE ARTEFACT. At MID this reads plus 66.94 bps pooled and plus 42.81 bps on non-payers - an independent corroboration of the frontier's plus 43 on a different universe (185 names against 15), a different source and different code - but at EXECUTABLE prices, buying the call at the ask and selling the put at the bid, it reads plus 342.35 bps, 5.1x larger. A COST question has to be answered at the prices an account actually pays. ALL-IN 701.87 bps a year: financing 342.35 plus roll spread 340.06 plus commission 3.57, at a median DTE of 73 and 5.0 rolls a year, or 589.92 after O18's rho of 0.6743 which is an EXTRAPOLATION here because rho was measured on 35-delta 60-DTE contracts. THE ROLL IS HALF THE COST AND IT IS UNAVOIDABLE: the financing benefit REQUIRES rolling, since exercising means paying the strike in cash which defeats the purpose, so every roll pays a full round trip - and that is why 60-90 DTE, the SHORTEST tenor, is the worst case. VERSUS MARGIN: Robinhood Gold at risk-free plus 420 MORE EXPENSIVE, Robinhood standard at plus 995 CHEAPER, IBKR Pro at plus 150 MORE EXPENSIVE. The brief expected cheaper than margin with caveats and AS STATED THAT IS WRONG. The registered prior predicted this and all four of its cells were right (more expensive than Gold 80/20, cheaper than standard 85/15, answer depends on the card 85/15, median mid excess inside 0 to 150 bps 90/10), reasoning from the frontier's own arithmetic. MARGIN RATES ARE ASSUMPTIONS, published retail cards rather than anything this repository measured, and every output says so. TWO POST-HOC CUTS, BOTH LABELLED AND CARRYING NO VERDICT, and the first refutes my own reason for running it: by rate era the option route is more expensive than Gold in ALL FIVE eras, its own cost stable at 616 to 754 bps while the Gold spread swings 52 to 567, so the answer is NOT era-dependent as I expected; and restricting to non-payers gives 578.2 bps, the same two answers, so the C3 anomaly does not move the verdict. TWO OF THE AUDIT'S FOUR PROPOSED DERIVATIONS ARE WRONG AND BOTH WERE MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED. (1) Its rule for claimed items, IN ?PROGRESS against the status cell, MATCHES A CELL THAT SAYS THE OPPOSITE: B13 reads PARTIAL - BLOCKED ON DATA, NOT IN PROGRESS, so the literal rule carries a 50 percent false-positive rate on its own two predicted hits, and this project already found that exact trap once by hand in the PT-WRITER session. Negation is handled and pinned, with a CONTROL that fails if the naive rule ever stops being wrong. (2) A HANDOFF's mtime is not its freshness: measured immediately after git merge origin/main, HANDOFF_edge_audit.md and HANDOFF_optionsbot.md both carried the MERGE MINUTE as their mtime while HANDOFF_ci.md, untouched by the merge, still read three days old - mtime records when git last WROTE the file into this checkout, so in a fresh worktree every handoff reads as newly touched. Age comes from the newest commit touching the file. A THIRD DEPARTURE, DECLARED: the audit's one permitted failing assertion - the generated board is older than the newest tip it describes - CRIES WOLF on this board's own strongest ingredient. Worktrees carrying uncommitted work read 8 of 12 and change whenever anybody saves a file, so a committed snapshot goes stale within minutes and the pin would be red on ordinary work, which is MA21's failure mode reached by a pin rather than by a warning. A generated snapshot rots exactly like a hand-typed one; it just rots honestly. So there is ONE copy of the fact and it is git: ma_in_flight.json is RETIRED into a pointer carrying its own 2026-08-14 contents verbatim (rule 9), --write emits a snapshot to a gitignored .board_state.json on request, and the assertion that CANNOT cry wolf is that the retired file makes no dated claim at all. THE HAND FILE'S OWN BLIND SPOT IS NOW MEASURED: it admitted an agent editing files with nothing committed was invisible to it, and that is 8 of 12 worktrees today, the ingredient most likely to catch a live collision. IT NEVER WARNS - counts only, exit 0 on every finding, exit 2 only when the script itself breaks, and an unmeasurable ingredient reads null and renders UNMEASURED rather than 0, because a zero meaning nothing-in-flight and a zero meaning git-did-not-answer would be checkout_drift's founding defect in a new costume. THE BOARD ALSO CORRECTS THE AUDIT'S OWN TABLE: it predicts 0 lanes in flight and there is 1, worktree-optionsbot-lane at +3, live on both local and remote. A DEFECT IN MY OWN FIRST CUT, caught by running it: origin/X and X were counted separately and it reported LANES IN FLIGHT 2 for one lane. AND ONE OF MY TESTS PASSED LOCALLY AND FAILED IN CI, the worst way to be wrong: test_a_healthy_run_writes_ok_and_exits_zero ran the heartbeat with no --repo, so it measured checkout_drift SHARED_CHECKOUT, a pinned C drive path that exists on exactly one machine, and the ubuntu runner read unknown not found in ok, alarm. The pin is correct and deliberate - a copy measuring its own tree would always see a fresh worktree and always say fine - so the defect is a test that silently inherited a machine-specific default. It now builds its own origin-and-clone via the one fixture builder, and the fix is VERIFIED against the CI condition rather than assumed: re-run with that path pointed at nothing, all 31 pass. The local gate could not have caught it, because locally the path exists. AND A SPEEDUP REVERTED FOR BEING WRONG: replacing 48 per-file git log calls with one --name-only walk cut 10.1s to 1.0s and DISAGREED ON 2 OF 48, always older, because --name-only prints no filenames for a MERGE commit and this repo lands every lane through a merge. The identical command is parallelised instead, verified bit-identical on all 48. THE RETIREMENT BROKE A CONSUMER AND THE BREAK IS THE FINDING: scripts/ma_dependency_map.py READ ma_in_flight.json and stamped an IN FLIGHT flag into two rendered tables and into ma_dependency_edges.json, so retiring the file staled a COMMITTED artifact and its check went red. A CORRECTION AGAINST MY OWN FIRST DIAGNOSIS, WHICH I HAD ALREADY REPORTED: I attributed it to the merge adding nine modules under valuation and said it was not mine. Measured, it is entirely mine - restoring the original file and re-running --check exits 0. I published a hypothesis before running a one-command control. AND THE FLAGS WERE NOT MERELY STALE, THEY WERE ROUTING PEOPLE AWAY FROM FINISHED WORK - the regeneration diff removes PREREG committed blind from MA13, MA19, MA36 and MA37, every one of them DONE, so a dispatcher reading the map was told four completed items were claimed and half-measured. A stale in-flight flag is worse than none, because absence prompts a check and a confident wrong label does not. THE BREAK IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX because it demonstrates the argument for declining MB27 assertion: if replacing one file can stale a committed artifact, a branch moving certainly can. So the coupling is CUT rather than patched - the generator no longer opens the file, in_flight is gone from the schema, the map three pointers now name board_state.py, and it carries a paragraph saying it deliberately does not know what is in flight. And the comment-versus-code family showed up INVERTED: my replacement stripped comment lines and asserted the bare filename was absent, and FAILED AGAINST THE FIXED TREE because the generator now emits prose about the retired file into the map - documentation inside a string literal reads as code to a line filter. It names the read expression instead. DISCOVERABILITY IS PART OF THE ITEM, so RUN_RULES Part B rule 6 is corrected: it told agents that work in flight is unobservable and to ask for a screenshot, which was the boundary MB27 moved. It now points at the script first and keeps the screenshot for what really is unobservable, an agent live reasoning and other machines. A tool nobody is told to run reproduces the disease it cures. Zero trials - facts about what git says, no hypothesis and no threshold. |

THE GATE NEVER BECAME EVALUABLE. MB15's kill condition is a test OF A MAPPING - the venue to retail mapping must reproduce the published ~60% retail share to within +/-15pp - and the cache ships exchange as a bare uint8 with no legend travelling with it, so the mapping is CHOSEN rather than given. Two independent measurements kill it. (1) THE IDENTIFIER IS NOT ON THIS AXIS, BY MARKET STRUCTURE RATHER THAN BY A DATA GAP. In equities the standard retail identifier is the off-exchange TRF print, a venue fact; in options there is no off-exchange execution, and wholesaler internalisation surfaces as an on-exchange price-improvement auction rather than as a distinct venue. ThetaData's published legend DOES carry off-exchange venues - 57 FINRA/NASDAQ TRF, 58 BSE TRF, 59 NYSE TRF - and NOT ONE appears in 70,288,482 prints; all 20 observed codes are lit options exchanges. Their absence is informative rather than an artefact of an incomplete legend, which is exactly why obtaining the legend mattered. (2) THE GATE IS NOT DISCRIMINATING, MEASURED EXHAUSTIVELY: with 20 codes there are 2^20 = 1,048,576 retail/non-retail partitions and 633,666 of them - 60.4311% - land inside the registered 45 to 75% band, the smallest clearing retail set being 5 of 20 venues and 94.03% of all 12-venue partitions clearing. A gate that more than half of arbitrary mappings pass cannot fail against anyone free to choose the mapping after seeing the data, so passing it would not have been evidence. That is a measurement and not an opinion, and it is pinned with a positive control: a tight band of 59.95 to 60.05% admits under 2%, so the figure reads the band rather than the arithmetic. THE AXIS THE IDENTIFIER ACTUALLY LIVES ON IS IN THIS SAME CACHE AND IS UNREAD. Bryzgalova-Pavlova-Sikorskaya, the source of the ~60% figure the gate is calibrated against, build their retail proxy from an OPRA trade CONDITION flag, and the paper's own words are quoted rather than a search summary because correcting this mattered: exploiting a flag for price improvement mechanisms, introduced by OPRA in NOVEMBER 2019 for transaction-level data, they identify wholesaler trades executed through a single-leg price improvement mechanism, abbreviated SLIM. Not venue. A THIRD DEFECT IN THE GATE FOLLOWS AND IT IS INDEPENDENT OF THE OTHER TWO: MB15 asks the proxy to reproduce the ~60% share ON THE POOLED CACHE, but that figure is a 2020-21 statistic and the identifier did not exist before November 2019 while this cache starts in 2016 - so the gate scores a recent-period number against a pooled denominator spanning four years in which the flag cannot fire at all, and even a perfectly correct proxy would have been measured against the wrong target. Unlike the other two defects this one would have applied on the RIGHT axis too, so any successor register must state its period and cannot begin before November 2019. MY OWN CENSUS INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCES THAT DATE, which is the strongest verification available since neither was tuned to the other: condition 125 is ABSENT in 2016, 2017 and 2018, reads a partial-year 4.79% in 2019, then sits at 18.16, 15.95, 15.60, 16.64, 18.44 and 19.88% from 2020 to 2025. Coverage on this cache is ample: condition 18 AUTO_EXECUTION 55.12%, 125 SINGLE_LEG_AUCTION_NON_ISO 15.34%, 126 SINGLE_LEG_AUCTION_ISO 0.03%, 131 MULTI_LEG_AUCTION 4.95%, size below 5 contracts 76.01%, size exactly 1 contract 52.18%, across 33 distinct condition codes. WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT COMPUTED is the union single-leg-auction OR (auto-execution AND size under 5), i.e. the retail share itself: that union IS the successor register's gate and computing it here, after seeing the registered axis fail, would be choosing the design on the outcome. Marginal coverage of each field is a feasibility fact and their union is the hypothesis; the separation is pinned by an AST test over the shipped scripts so it cannot be quietly relaxed. A PREMISE CORRECTION TO THE ITEM'S OWN TITLE: it is headed the exchange field unread by every prior study, and the field HAS been read - O14's sweep_share reads it - but only as CARDINALITY, how many distinct venues a burst touches, never as identity. O14's module docstring also already cites Bryzgalova et al. and the >60% figure, so the reference was in the tree before this item raised it. TWO BRIEF INSTRUCTIONS COULD NOT BE FOLLOWED LITERALLY AND BOTH ARE REPORTED: there is NO pinned freeze for the tick cache, only the two CHAIN freezes, so a SHA-256 fingerprint over every unit is recorded instead as the substance of what pinning protects; and pre_panel_history is ABSENT from all 3,884 tick payloads, reported VACUOUS rather than PASSING per O21-D2's C5 precedent, because a filter that never ran and a filter that ran and found nothing must not read the same. THE ARTIFACT IS REAL AND SMALL: total correction at two years +0.2577 of a t, about 15 percent of the floor, splitting almost evenly into coverage +0.1426 and memory +0.1151 - so the instrument's own coverage cost is comparable to the effect it isolates, stated as a limitation. THE MEMORY TERM APPEARS EXACTLY WHERE BRW SAY IT MUST: 0 of 2 positive at HAC lag 1, 6 of 6 positive at lag 2 through 7, direction named in the register before the run but scored POST-HOC with no verdict. A CORRECTION TO THE AUDIT: the shortfall does NOT grow with horizon - it steps up once where overlap begins and is then flat or falling. THE LONG-SHORT LEG LOSES A HORIZON, failing from H=252 rather than H=315, so S22's no-long-short-beyond-about-a-year instruction hardens to a one-year cut; the long-only shipped book is untouched. C1 is exact - the runner IMPORTS S22's own arm() and reproduces its stored draws at max abs delta 0.000e+00 over 160 of 160 cells. The coverage-preserving stratified shortcut was DISQUALIFIED on measurement before the register was written: about 170 names per draw pair with themselves and the residual association is live at t +4.106. Three defects in my own instrument, all caught by running it, the worst being C1 passing VACUOUSLY at 0.000e+00 on an empty panel because the panel date column is str - it now counts the cells it compared. 1 infra trial, infra 15 to 16; equity unmoved at 234. ADOPTS NOTHING and no product copy changes. 31 tests, 6 of 6 mutations caught. |

Inputs all measured from the shipped artifact: quarterly top-decile alpha mean +1.794pp, sd 3.298pp, lag-1 autocorrelation +0.0812, so the design effect is 1.177 and n_eff over n is 0.850; the documented quant-crisis window holds 9 rebalance dates and the P1S0 dead half holds 20 of 69. MINIMUM DETECTABLE REGIME CONTRAST in annualised top-decile alpha at the honest hurdle 3.3031: crisis vs rest 16.90pp/yr, P1S0 dead half vs rest 12.54pp/yr, plain halves 11.38pp/yr. THE PUBLISHED TOTAL TOP-DECILE ALPHA IS 7.17pp/yr, so every cell asks the regime DIFFERENCE to exceed the entire strategy by a factor of 1.6 to 2.4. READ THE OTHER WAY ROUND, which is the more useful form: at 80% power and the honest critical value, a 7.17pp/yr regime gap needs 136.8 quarters PER WINDOW, i.e. 34.2 years each side; a 4.00pp gap needs 109.9 years; a 2.00pp gap needs 439.4. THE PANEL SUPPLIES 17.3 YEARS IN TOTAL AND THE CRISIS WINDOW SUPPLIES 2.3. AND IT DOES NOT DEPEND ON THE MULTIPLICITY CORRECTION - even at an uncorrected t of 2 the crisis contrast needs a 10.23pp/yr gap. So the naive fix this record warns about most is not merely dangerous here, it is ARITHMETICALLY FUTILE, which is a stronger and more durable reason to refuse it than calling it p-hacking. WHAT IT FORECLOSES: every regime-conditioning register on this panel, permanently. RE-OPEN CONDITION, quoted: until forward time accrues at one quarter per quarter - i.e. nothing but elapsed time re-opens it, and at the measured shortfall that is decades. WHAT IT DOES NOT FORECLOSE: MB14. The instrument that states it properly is TIDEMARK's power gate, now ported as MB22. |

ZERO TRIALS - THE ARM NEVER RAN - so options N stays 305 and by_domain is bit-identical across the log append. NEITHER DECISION IS UNLOCKED. THE QUESTION: MB1 read its decomposition as TIMING about 79 percent and SELECTION about 21 percent with a selection residual of -1.2762pp exceeding its own 1.00pp bar, and every one of those is a BARE POINT ESTIMATE - MB1's register committed no uncertainty measure and the interval it did compute afterwards was on the MEDIAN MENU GAP, a different quantity, so the reading was post-hoc. This register was written to settle it with a tail-capable primary, a paired name-year cluster bootstrap, and the thing MB1 omitted: a verdict rule stating what the interval must exclude, fixed before anything was read. THE GATING CONTROL FIRES AND THE ARM DOES NOT RUN. C-RANGE ran in its OWN pass and was read before the arm, O10's process defect not repeated, with its bar fixed in the register beforehand. Covered entries out-earn uncovered ones in BOTH arms - alert 2,446 at +3.9830pp against 1,424 at +2.0458pp, a shift of +1.9372pp; control 18,531 at +10.0155pp against 11,123 at +5.5330pp, a shift of +4.4826pp - so the DIFFERENTIAL is -2.5454pp against a bar of 1.00pp and the arm is VOID by register void condition 4. THE CONTROL IS MEASURING THE RIGHT OBJECT, VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: its covered counts reproduce MB1's published coverage EXACTLY at 2,446 of 3,870 = 63.20 percent and 18,531 of 29,654 = 62.49 percent. WHY THIS IS A FINDING AND NOT A FAILED RUN: the residual is a DIFFERENCE OF DIFFERENCES so a coverage effect CONSTANT across the arms cancels exactly, which is MB1's own robustness argument and is correct - but it does not survive a coverage effect that DIFFERS between the arms, and the control arm's coverage effect is 2.3 times the alert arm's. THE CONFOUND IS TWICE THE SIZE OF THE EFFECT IT WOULD HAVE TO EXPLAIN AND RUNS IN THE SAME DIRECTION, so MB1's selection-carries-a-fifth reading MUST NOT be quoted as evidence that contract selection matters. The mechanism corroborates it rather than being hand-waved: coverage requires a fillable in-band menu, i.e. a liquid chain, and O10 independently measured the liquid part of this book at +5.82 percent expectancy against -3.11 percent for the tape-thin part. AN HONEST LIMIT STATED RATHER THAN BURIED: the confound's path runs through pick_gap and whether it is FULLY absorbed by the menu_gap subtraction is NOT established, so the correct statement is that the residual CANNOT BE ATTRIBUTED to selection rather than that it is ENTIRELY coverage - establishing which needs its own register. THE MEDIAN IS BANNED BY MEASUREMENT, twice, and pinned by AST: O17C4 recorded the effect as a MEAN effect and not a MEDIAN one at median-vs-median +0.40pp against means separating 4.79pp, and MB1 reproduced it on this exact object with both arms' menu medians at about -0.50 against a -4.7564pp mean gap on the same legs. THE VERDICT RULE WAS FIXED IN THREE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE AND ALL-REACHABLE STATES: CONFIRMED needs the CI95 to exclude zero in the full sample AND both halves, the same sign throughout, AND the materiality bound to hold ACROSS THE INTERVAL with the nearer-to-zero end beyond 1.00pp, which is O21-D2's lesson; REFUTED needs the full-sample CI95 ENTIRELY inside plus or minus 1.00pp; everything else is UNRESOLVED. A test pins that a -1.28pp point with an interval reaching near zero - MB1's exact shape - does NOT confirm. The 1.00pp bar is MB1's own reused verbatim rather than re-chosen with the estimate already published, and the trimmed means are declared SECONDARIES with no verdict power. THE GATE IS NOT VACUOUS, which is what makes the VOID worth anything: it PASSES when both arms carry a large but SHARED coverage effect, since that is the very structure making the residual robust, it fires when they are selected differently, and the arm's own gate is proved to be no hard-coded refusal by exercising it in isolation on a synthetic passing artifact so the gate returns while THE ARM ITSELF IS NEVER RUN. THE DECISION, STATED EXPLICITLY: the outcome is VOID before the arm, so NEITHER fires - MB2 STAYS PARKED and CONTRACT SELECTION STAYS OPEN - and it is explicitly NOT a licence to re-run with a different statistic or a looser control until something clears, a re-open needing a materially different construction such as a coverage-matched decomposition with its own register and its own trials. TRIALS ARE ZERO on MB15's precedent from earlier the same day: the register booked 3 CONTINGENT on the arm running and the arm never ran, so no hypothesis about returns was scored against its bar and nothing was searched. The counter-argument is stated because it is not frivolous - C-RANGE does have a pre-committed bar and is computed on returns, and O21 was corrected UPWARD for that shape - and the distinction taken is that a control can only ever BLOCK a finding and never produce one, so it adds no degree of freedom to any published claim; if a later reader disagrees the row is there to amend and the direction of that error is the safe one. SCOPE: covered subset only, the menu legs are MB1's banked from the PINNED harvest freeze and READ rather than re-simulated so there is no re-simulation drift, the legs artifact is fingerprinted, and pre_panel_history filtering is INHERITED from MB1's freeze read with the key absent from the banked artifacts, reported VACUOUS rather than PASSING per O21-D2's C5 precedent. R2 STANDS and O11 BINDS. 22 tests, 129 suites and 0 failures after merging origin/main. |

The composite already declines to hold what the flag catches, so the overlay has almost nothing to protect - and renormalising pushes weight onto the 96 percent that carries 98.81 percent of the crashes, which is why the reduction goes negative. A CORRECTION AGAINST MY OWN REGISTER: it priced the fail-open from MA28's PANEL-WIDE numbers as marginally SAFER, and in the book that REVERSES - unflaggable holdings are the most crash-prone bucket at 1.055 percent and carry 31 of 84 crashes while taking no haircut, so the rule is blind to a quarter of the book and to its largest crash bucket. Post-hoc, no verdict; no ratio quoted on a single crash. C2 PROVES the decile membership is the shipped one at 69/69 dates and 0.000e+00, the control MB18 taught this lane to build. The power statement was wrong six-fold in the helpful direction (measured paired SE 0.1106pp against a borrowed 0.9354pp), because V2G's SE was measured on a far larger perturbation. Expectations 4 right, 2 wrong, both the same miss: assuming the book looks like the panel. DOES NOT WEAKEN MA28. Adopts nothing; adoption would have been routed to Don as a vintage event. 1 equity trial booked BEFORE the run at 18a4ecc, N 235 to 236. 24 tests, 9 of 9 mutations caught. |
| I-4 | I | The event spine: one canonical PIT earnings-date table | **DONE - SHIPPED 2026-08-20** | INSTRUMENT - zero trials, adopts nothing, reproduces O6/O7's banked earnings joins exactly and agrees with the shipped paths on 15,480 real comparisons | PENDING | HANDOFF_data_mining.md I-4 | 2026-08-20 | human | SEASON 2 INSTRUMENT I-4, collection-and-provenance class, ZERO TRIALS - it computes no signal, scores no arm and returns no verdict. ONE canonical point-in-time earnings-date table (valuation/edge/event_spine.py) built from EVENTS code 22 via bulk.py's existing decode, which is REUSED not reimplemented: the spine calls prepare_events and earnings_dates, never re-parses the CSV, and never keeps its own EARNINGS_CODES - pinned by a test that inspects the module NAMESPACE and its AST assignment targets rather than its text, because the docstring legitimately says the words while explaining it keeps no copy (two earlier cuts of that test failed on their own comments, which is the argument for parsing over grepping). WHY ONE TABLE: PT-SPLIT was two recorders disagreeing about one named object and it shipped an engine figure as an Index claim; an earnings date is the same shape, so X-2's census, O-2's 2x2 and every EO follow-on read THIS table and nothing else. THE AGREEMENT CHECK IS THE DELIVERABLE AND IT RUNS IN TWO PLACES THAT PROVE DIFFERENT THINGS: tests/test_event_spine.py drives an exhaustive synthetic grid (7 calendars x 3 entries x 5 windows plus 4 expiries) and agrees on every cell INCLUDING which cells are UNKNOWN, which a True/False-only test would miss; scripts/i4_event_spine.py drives the real alert book through both the spine and the SHIPPED refuse_within/owns_the_event and returns 0 DISAGREEMENTS over 15,480 comparisons (3,870 rows x 3 windows + owns_the_event). The synthetic grid proves the LOGIC matches, the book proves the DATA feeding it matches, and a spine that agreed on invented calendars while disagreeing on the book would pass the first and be useless. Disagreements are LISTED with (row, ticker, fn, window, entry, expiry, spine, shipped), never counted. BANKED JOIN REPRODUCED EXACTLY against O6_O7_O17_EARNINGS.json on the same split-clean book: 29 zero-coverage names (identical list, zero in either direction), 388 excluded trades (10.03%), and all four O17 arms - C_5d 3045/437, C_10d 2856/626, C_15d 2642/840, C4_own_the_event 1987/1495. THE ONE RULE (O17's) IS ENFORCED STRUCTURALLY, NOT BY CONVENTION: a missing earnings date is UNKNOWN never 'no announcement', so coverage() returns a STATE never a bool, dates() RAISES UnknownCoverage rather than returning [] because an empty list is exactly what a caller folds into 'nothing announced' without noticing while a raise cannot be folded into anything, and FAIL_CLOSED names are LISTED BY NAME in the census so a consumer drops them deliberately. Exposure measured on BOTH scopes: options book 186 names, 157 COVERED, 29 FAIL_CLOSED (15.6% of names, 10.0% of trades) - the foreign private issuers filing 20-F/6-K rather than 8-K; equity panel 2,531 names, 2,215 COVERED, 10 PARTIAL, 306 FAIL_CLOSED (12.1%), the same mechanism at scale and dominated by ADR tickers. FOUR STATES NOT TWO: COVERED (>=3 dates/yr), PARTIAL (1-2, real coverage and demonstrably incomplete), GAP (the name has coverage elsewhere but none this year), FAIL_CLOSED (none anywhere) - because code 22 runs ~2.83 per ticker-year against a quarterly 4, so rounding PARTIAL up to COVERED is how a hole in a calendar becomes an implied 'no announcement'; EXPECTED_MIN=3 is recorded not tuned and no arm selects on it. PROVENANCE: code 22 = 'Results of Operations and Financial Condition' was decoded empirically in bulk.py 2026-08-01 and is now CONFIRMED against the published legend S17's correction retrieved from SHARADAR/INDICATORS?table=EVENTCODES and transcribed in SHARADAR_REFERENCE.md section 2 - 385,896 occurrences, 10,149 tickers, 2004-08-23 to 2026-07-31 - so it is no longer an inference. The spine does NOT import the archived earnings_surface (MA59 quarantine: a live module importing it would make a closed study reachable from the product), so the comparison lives in the test and the script only, pinned by parsing the spine's imports. CODES 34/35 SUNSET RECORDED SO NOBODY READS IT AS A SIGNAL: Schedule 13G stops 2024-12-17 and Schedule 13D stops 2025-05-16 from the legend's own first/last-seen columns while every other S17 arm code runs to 2026-07-31, and a code that stops being emitted is era-concentrated BY CONSTRUCTION; carried in the census so a future study does not rediscover the cliff in its own data and report it as a finding. Its limits travel with it: it touches NO earnings date because code 22 has no sunset and this spine is code 22 only, and whether the sunset drives anything is UNMEASURED. A SECOND NOT-A-SIGNAL FOUND WHILE BUILDING THE CENSUS: 2026 reads 77 PARTIAL against 80 COVERED because the source ENDS 2026-07-31, and 2004 reads 113 PARTIAL because code 22 BEGINS 2004-08-23 - both ends are bounded by the extract rather than by data quality, now marked source_bounded_years with the reason. That is BUG 9 of the chain harvest in a different costume (reading a not-yet-complete period as a damaged one, which cost that harvest 7.5 months across 86 names), and an unmarked cliff at the end of a coverage census is exactly what a trend-spotting consumer reports; pinned by a test that also requires an INTERIOR year to be excused by nothing. Interior coverage is healthy and rises as names list: COVERED 121 (2005) to 157 (2025), GAP falling 35 to 0. NOT a full earnings calendar - ~2.83/ticker-year is partial even for covered names, which is why coverage is stated per NAME-YEAR. Reproduce: python -m scripts.i4_event_spine; artifact data/free_analysis/I4_EVENT_SPINE.json (gitignored). 16 tests. |
